# Critical comparison of four translations: Xylander (1556) and Vinet (1591)

Reviewer: Claude Opus 5.5 (claude-opus-5-5), Anthropic; date 2026-11-21; web access: yes; a team of agents: a comparer and an independent sceptic for each group, and an assembler.

Translators are anonymous (labels A–D). Each segment gives the Latin reading text, its sense, the divergences between the translators, the errors of individual translators, the review of text proposals and doubts, the best base for each language, notes worth keeping and questions for the editor. Every finding was checked a second time by an independent sceptic (second pass: confirmed, modified or added). The term concordance and the overall assessment follow the segments.

## Overview

Across the 124 segments the team recorded 32 divergences, 1 major and 54 minor errors and 24 editor questions for Xylander, and 29 divergences, 0 major and 32 minor errors and 24 editor questions for Vinet. All four translators understand both Latin versions and recompute every ratio correctly; no wrong number, ratio or ratio bracket was found in the running text of any of the four, and there is only one major error in the whole set (B's English at X28). The four fall into two pairs. A and C are close, literal and consistent: they keep the Latin's construction, its signs and its word pairs, and they are the best base almost everywhere (most often jointly, 'A = C'); C has the fewest faults and the most useful notes and is the best single base, A is close behind and often more exact in its terminology. B and D write freer, more idiomatic prose but leave out many of the connectives that carry the argument, add words without brackets, paraphrase antitheses and vary their technical terms; their Russian and English are rarely the best base, though individual phrasings are worth taking over. The decisions left to the editor are chiefly terminological: intensio ('tension' throughout, or 'pitch' in the definitions); how to render Xylander's double naming of the intervals (X28–X30, X35); proportio beside ratio in Xylander; the form of paraphonon / antiphonon; transliteration of the Greek compounds in X15 and of mese monos (X14); Vinet's simplex and melos; the name Exodius; and whether a translator's conjecture may ever stand in the running text (X24, X38). The term concordance recommends one rendering for each, consistent with about.md § 4, and lists twenty term inconsistencies, all minor, chiefly in B and D.

## Contents

- Xylander: X01–X58
- Vinet: V01–V66
- Terms
- Term inconsistencies
- Overall assessment


## Xylander (Basel 1556)


### X01 (Xyl. 40.1–2)

> Exacta summa hic, evidensque Musicae.

**Sense.** Title: 'Here [is] an exact and clear summary of Music.' Summa is a compendious account of the whole; Musicae is the genitive of the discipline.

**Divergences**

1. *Exacta summa*: What kind of text summa names: a compendium/summary, or a general account.
   - **A, C**: summa as a compendium: 'свод' / 'summary'
     - A: «Здесь — точный и ясный свод музыки.» / “Here is an exact and clear summary of Music.”
     - C: «Здесь — точный и ясный свод музыки.» / “Here is an exact and clear summary of music.”
   - **D**: summa turned into an adverb of brevity ('кратко изложено') in Russian; 'summary' in English
     - D: «Здесь кратко, точно и ясно изложено [учение] о музыке.» / “Here is a precise and lucid summary of music.”
   - **B**: summa as a general 'exposition, account' without the notion of a summary
     - B: «Здесь дано точное и ясное изложение музыки.» / “Here is an exact and clear account of music.”
   - *Verdict* (medium confidence; second pass: confirmed): Summa in this position is the 'sum, summary, compendium' of a subject (the sense of the title word in medieval and humanist usage); A, C and D keep the notion of a compendium, B's 'изложение / account' loses it. A legitimate but weaker choice, not an error. «свод музыки» is a slightly stiff genitive in Russian; D's restructuring is idiomatic and correctly bracketed.
   - *Recommended*: Здесь — точный и ясный свод [учения о] музыке. / Here is an exact and clear summary of music.
   - *Evidence*: Lewis & Short s.v. summa, II ('the main thing, chief point, principal matter; the sum, substance'), II.B.3 ('the sum, the whole') [checked]; Running heads of the volume: COMPENDIVM on the recto (xylander-p43) [checked]

**Best base.** Russian: A = C (Keeps summa as a compendium and the bare heading form; D is equally faithful but more paraphrastic.) English: A = C (Literal and exact ('exact and clear summary'); D equally good.)

**Notes to keep**

- C: The heading names no author; the running heads of this part of the volume read COMPENDIVM on the rectos (confirmed on p. 43).


### X02 (Xyl. 40.3–7)

> Universum hoc, antiqui Musica contineri perhibuerunt. Nihil enim in rerum natura absque commensuratione et proportione consistit:

**Sense.** The ancients declared that this universe is held together by (or rests on) music. For nothing in the nature of things subsists without commensuration and proportion,

**Text proposals and doubts**

- **C**, doubt: printed «Vniuersum hoc, antiqui Musica contineri perhibuerunt (40.3–4)»; proposal: «Эта вселенная, как утверждали древние, заключена в музыке.» / 'This universe, the ancients declared, is contained in music.'. *Verdict: open*; second pass: confirmed. Contineri with a bare ablative can mean 'be held together by' (L&S contineo I.B.1) or 'be contained in, consist in, rest upon' (L&S I.B.3.b, e.g. Cic. Fin. 2.48 'quattuor his virtutibus continetur'). The following argument (nothing subsists without proportion) favours 'held together', which all four chose; the alternative is grammatically sound and worth a note. *Evidence*: Lewis & Short s.v. contineo, I.B.1 and I.B.3.b [checked]

**Best base.** Russian: A = C (Exact and natural («эта вселенная держится музыкой»); B's «весь этот мир» slightly over-renders universum hoc.) English: A = C (Exact; C keeps the word order of the Latin, A is equally sound.)

**Notes to keep**

- C: Commensuratio is a late (Boethian) word, 'due measure, symmetry'; Xylander pairs it with proportio here and in X03 and with proportionalitas in X04; Vinet has symmetria.
- A: Proportio later (X25 and after) is Xylander's Boethian word for the numerical ratio of an interval; the rendering 'proportion' keeps his word.

**For the editor**

- Musica contineri: keep 'held together by music' (all four) and record 'rests on / is contained in music' in a note?


### X03 (Xyl. 40.7–12)

> neque quicquam eorum quae fiunt, sine debita commensuratione et proportione si fiat, recte productum natumve esse dicitur: sive id ab arte efficiatur, sive a natura: et sive sub sensum illud cadat, sive solo intellectu perspiciatur.

**Sense.** and nothing that comes to be, if it comes to be without due commensuration and proportion, is said to have been rightly produced or born, whether it is brought about by art or by nature, and whether it falls under sense or is discerned by the intellect alone.

**Best base.** Russian: C = A (Both keep the conditional si fiat and the plain dicitur («не называется», «не говорят»); B and D shift dicitur to a modal («нельзя назвать / нельзя сказать»).) English: C (Closest to the structure (neque ... si fiat ... dicitur) and fully idiomatic.)


### X04 (Xyl. 40.13–15)

> Caeterum Musica ipsa per se commensuratio quaedam omnino et proportionalitas est, nimirum harmonia universi existens.

**Sense.** Music itself, in its own right, is altogether a kind of commensuration and proportionality, being namely (doubtless) the harmony of the universe.

**Divergences**

1. *commensuratio quaedam omnino et proportionalitas est*: Whether omnino means 'altogether, wholly' or is an asseverative 'certainly, assuredly'.
   - **A, C**: omnino = 'altogether, wholly'
     - A: «есть всецело некая соразмерность и пропорциональность» / “is in its own right altogether a certain commensuration and proportionality”
     - C: «есть всецело некая соразмерность и пропорциональность» / “is in itself altogether a kind of commensuration and proportionality”
   - **B, D**: omnino = 'certainly, assuredly'
     - B: «несомненно, есть своего рода соразмерность и пропорциональность» / “is assuredly a kind of commensurateness and proportionality”
     - D: «есть, без сомнения, некая соразмерность и пропорциональность» / “is certainly a kind of commensuration and proportionality”
   - *Verdict* (medium confidence; second pass: confirmed): A and C. The basic sense of omnino is 'altogether, wholly, entirely' (L&S I); the asseverative 'certainly, to be sure' is concessive and normally answered by sed or autem (L&S II.B), which is absent here. B and D are possible as an emphatic 'absolutely', but less natural; not an error.
   - *Recommended*: Музыка же сама по себе есть всецело некая соразмерность и пропорциональность, будучи, разумеется, гармонией вселенной. / Music itself, moreover, is in its own right altogether a kind of commensuration and proportionality, being, to be sure, the harmony of the universe.
   - *Evidence*: Lewis & Short s.v. omnino, I and II.B (concessive use with sed, autem) [checked]

**Errors**

- **D** (ru; omission; minor; second pass: confirmed): «Сама же музыка есть, без сомнения, некая соразмерность». per se ('in its own right') is not rendered in the Russian (the English has 'in itself'). *Fix:* Сама же музыка сама по себе есть …
- **D** (notes; note-claim; minor; second pass: confirmed): «Xylander changes from proportio to proportionalitas. He gives as his reason that music is harmonia universi». Xylander gives no reason for a change of word; the participial phrase explains why music is a commensuration, not why he writes proportionalitas. *Fix:* Xylander here has proportionalitas instead of proportio; the phrase harmonia universi existens explains the predicate.

**Text proposals and doubts**

- **B**, doubt: printed «commensuratio quædam omnino et proportionalitas est (40.13–14)»; proposal: «Сама музыка по себе во всех отношениях есть своего рода соразмерность и пропорциональность.» / 'Music itself is in every respect a kind of commensurateness and proportionality.'. *Verdict: sound*; second pass: confirmed. The alternative takes omnino in its primary sense 'altogether, wholly' (L&S I), which is preferable to B's chosen 'assuredly' (the concessive sense wants a following sed/autem). *Evidence*: Lewis & Short s.v. omnino [checked]

**Best base.** Russian: A (Renders ipsa per se, omnino and the participle (будучи … гармонией) exactly.) English: A = C (Both keep omnino as 'altogether' and the participial explanation.)

**Notes to keep**

- A: proportionalitas here replaces proportio (in Boethius, proportion as a likeness of ratios); the difference of words is kept ('proportionality').
- D: In the corresponding passage Vinet calls music the universe itself (ipsaque adeo Universitas) — verified in Vinet V05; drop D's claim about Xylander's 'reason'.


### X05 (Xyl. 40.15–20)

> Fortassis et ipsam divinam naturam haud inconvenienter sui ipsius et universi harmoniam quispiam dixerit: siquidem in Deo concinnatus, et ad consonantiam coaptatus mundus, bene, atque adeo quam optime se habere videtur.

**Sense.** Perhaps someone might not unfittingly call the divine nature itself the harmony of itself and of the universe, since the world, joined fitly together in God and fitted to consonance, is seen to be in a good, indeed the best possible, state.

**Best base.** Russian: A (Keeps the potential subjunctive (назвал бы), the litotes in sense and the pair слаженный / прилаженный for concinnatus / coaptatus.) English: C = A (Both exact; A's 'framed' and C's 'attuned' for concinnatus are both defensible.)

**Notes to keep**

- A: concinnare ('to join fitly') and coaptare ('to fit together', ecclesiastical Latin) are Xylander's standing words for the fitting together of harmony (X12, X44–47, X56); haud inconvenienter is a litotes.
- C: consonantia here is the concord of the cosmos, rendered 'consonance' by the project's rule; the text itself links cosmic harmony and musical consonance.

**For the editor**

- Consonantia in the cosmic sense (ad consonantiam coaptatus mundus): keep «консонанс» / 'consonance' by the convention, as all four do, or allow 'concord' here with a note?


### X06 (Xyl. 40.20–21)

> Tanto nimirum Musicam illi dignati sunt miraculo.

**Sense.** Of so great a wonder, doubtless, did they (the ancients) deem music worthy.

**Best base.** Russian: A = C (Both keep dignari as 'удостоили' and the degree tanto; C also renders nimirum («разумеется»).) English: C = A (Both literal and correct, with nimirum rendered ('indeed' / 'evidently').)

**Notes to keep**

- A: illi are the ancients of X02; miraculum is 'a wonder, a thing to marvel at', not an ecclesiastical miracle (L&S, cf. Plin. 12.5 digna miraculo).


### X07 (Xyl. 40.21–41.3)

> Ea quando in omnibus conspicitur, atque universa temperat, exornatque, tum sensui voluptatem, congruentiam autem (harmoniam dico) Naturae, beatitatem denique intellectui largitur: nos de ea Musica quae in sensibus, aut potius uno sensuum auditu consideratur, paucis disseremus.

**Sense.** Since it is seen in all things, tempers and adorns all things, and bestows pleasure on sense, congruence (I mean harmony) on Nature, and finally blessedness on the intellect, we shall discuss briefly that music which is considered in the senses, or rather in the one sense of hearing.

**Divergences**

1. *de ea Musica quae in sensibus, aut potius uno sensuum auditu consideratur*: in sensibus ... consideratur: music 'considered in (the domain of) the senses' or 'perceived/considered by means of the senses'.
   - **A, C**: locative: considered in the senses
     - A: «о той музыке, которая рассматривается в чувствах, или, вернее, в одном из чувств — слухе» / “of that Music which is considered in the senses, or rather in one of the senses, hearing”
     - C: «о той музыке, которая рассматривается в чувствах, или, вернее, в одном из чувств — в слухе» / “that music which is considered in the senses, or rather in the one sense of hearing”
   - **B**: instrumental: considered by means of the senses
     - B: «о музыке, которую рассматривают посредством чувств, вернее, одного из них — слуха» / “of music as considered by the senses, or rather by one of them, hearing”
   - **D**: instrumental, with consideratur turned into 'is perceived'
     - D: «о той музыке, которую воспринимают чувствами, вернее, одним из чувств — слухом» / “of the music that is perceived by the senses, or, more precisely, by one of them, hearing”
   - *Verdict* (medium confidence; second pass: confirmed): A and C: in with the ablative is locative, and the clause distinguishes the music that is found and studied in the sphere of sense from cosmic music (X02–X06). B's 'by the senses' is a slight shift; D's 'perceived' also replaces consideratur ('is observed, contemplated'). Low stakes; not counted as errors.
   - *Recommended*: о той музыке, которая рассматривается в чувствах, или, вернее, в одном из чувств — в слухе / that music which is considered in the senses, or rather in the one sense of hearing
   - *Evidence*: Lewis & Short s.v. considero, I ('to look at closely, attentively, carefully, to inspect, examine') [checked]

**Best base.** Russian: C (Keeps the causal period, temperat as «умеряет» and the locative in sensibus.) English: C (Exact and idiomatic throughout; A equally exact.)

**Notes to keep**

- C: beatitas is Cicero's coinage (N.D. 1.95, where he calls both beatitas and beatitudo harsh), later rare; Xylander prefers it to beatitudo, which Vinet uses.
- A: A threefold gift: to sense, to nature, to the intellect; the treatise confines itself to the first. Compare and contrast Boethius' musica mundana, humana, instrumentalis (I.2).


### X08 (Xyl. 41.4–5)

> Sonus est, vocis nullo intervallo divisae harmoniae particeps intensio, seu tenor.

**Sense.** A note is a tension (i.e. a pitch), or steady level, of the voice undivided by any interval, [a tension] partaking of harmony.

**Divergences**

1. *intensio*: Literal 'tension' (the Latin image, Boethius' intensio, Greek τάσις) or the modern 'pitch'.
   - **A, C**: 'tension'
     - A: «причастное гармонии напряжение, или удержание, голоса» / “a tension, or sustained holding, of a voice”
     - C: «причастное гармонии напряжение, или высота, голоса» / “the tension, or pitch, of a voice”
   - **B, D**: 'pitch'
     - B: «высота, или выдерживаемый тон, голосового звучания» / “a pitch, or sustained tone, of vocal sound”
     - D: «высота голоса, не разделённого никаким интервалом, иначе говоря, его ровное звучание» / “the pitch, or steady tone, of a voice”
   - *Verdict* (medium confidence; to the editor; second pass: modified): Both are legitimate: intensio is literally 'tension' (Greek τάσις) and is Boethius' word in the definition of the note ('Sonus igitur est vocis casus emmeles, id est aptus melo, in unam intensionem', I.8); Lewis & Short already give intensio for the pitch of the voice ('the tune: gravis, media, acuta', Censorinus 12). about.md leaves intensio alone to the translators. Russian «напряжение голоса» risks being read as vocal strain; 'pitch / высота' is the modern-intelligible term (about.md §4.1). The choice must stay consistent through the version (cf. intensio et remissio = повышение и понижение).
   - *Recommended*: Звук есть причастное гармонии напряжение, или высота, голоса, не разделённого никаким интервалом. / A note is the tension, or pitch, of a voice undivided by any interval, [a tension] partaking of harmony.
   - *Evidence*: Boethius, De inst. mus. I.8, ed. Friedlein p. 195 ('Sonus igitur est vocis casus emmeles, id est aptus melo, in unam intensionem'), TML transcription [checked]; Lewis & Short s.v. intensio, II ('The tune: gravis, media, acuta', Censor. 12) [checked]; about.md §4.1, §4.5 [checked]
2. *seu tenor*: What tenor adds as a gloss on intensio.
   - **A**: 'holding' of the voice
     - A: «или удержание» / “or sustained holding”
   - **B, D**: a sustained/steady tone
     - B: «или выдерживаемый тон» / “or sustained tone”
     - D: «иначе говоря, его ровное звучание» / “or steady tone”
   - **C**: 'pitch' (tenor = the tone of the voice)
     - C: «или высота» / “or pitch”
   - *Verdict* (medium confidence; second pass: confirmed): Tenor is 'an uninterrupted holding, course' (L&S I) and, in Quintilian, the tone or accent of the voice; as a gloss (seu) on intensio it names the steady pitch level. All four are defensible; A's «удержание голоса» is unidiomatic Russian (it suggests holding the voice back). If intensio is 'tension', tenor is best 'pitch' (C); if intensio is 'pitch', tenor is best 'sustained tone' (B).
   - *Recommended*: или высота / or pitch
   - *Evidence*: Lewis & Short s.v. tenor, I ('a holding on, holding fast; hence, an uninterrupted course'; Quint. 1.5.26 'tone, accent') [checked]

**Errors**

- **A** (ru; language; minor; second pass: confirmed): «напряжение, или удержание, голоса». «удержание голоса» is not idiomatic for a held pitch and can be read as 'restraint of the voice'. *Fix:* напряжение, или высота, голоса (or: …, или выдерживаемый тон, …)

**Text proposals and doubts**

- **A**, doubt: printed «uocis nullo interuallo diuisæ harmoniæ particeps intensio (41.4–5)»; proposal: «Звук есть напряжение, или удержание, голоса, причастное гармонии, не разделённой никаким интервалом.» / 'A note is a tension ... of the voice that partakes of a harmony divided by no interval.'. *Verdict: unsound*; second pass: confirmed. Divisae can agree with vocis or harmoniae, but X12 explains the two marks separately (the note is indivisible like a point; it partakes of harmony because it can be attuned with another note): 'undivided' belongs to the voice/note. A rightly rejected it. *Evidence*: X12 of this text [checked]

**Best base.** Russian: C (Exact construal, idiomatic, keeps intensio and tenor distinct («напряжение, или высота»).) English: C (Exact, with the bracketed [a tension] making the agreement of particeps clear; A equally exact.)

**Notes to keep**

- C: Parallels: Thrasyllus in Theon (Hiller p. 47) defines the note as the tension of a voice partaking of harmony; Boethius I.8 'vocis casus emmeles ... in unam intensionem'; Xylander explains harmoniae particeps himself in X12; Vinet has conveniens. (The Theon reference is not verified here.)
- A: A definition by three marks: intensio seu tenor (one pitch), nullo intervallo divisae (no extension, cf. the point in X12), harmoniae particeps (fitness for melody, X12–13).

**For the editor**

- intensio: «напряжение» / 'tension' (A, C) or «высота» / 'pitch' (B, D) for the whole version?


### X09 (Xyl. 41.5–7)

> Intervallum autem, sonorum mutua unius ad alterum ratio, sive respectus.

**Sense.** An interval is the mutual ratio, or relation, of notes, of the one to the other.

**Best base.** Russian: C = A (Both keep mutua, ratio and respectus as a double rendering in the Latin order.) English: A (Exact and natural.)

**Notes to keep**

- A: ratio, sive respectus is a double rendering of one term; respectus is the scholastic 'relation' (DMLBS 4), cf. respectu in X18.
- C: Cf. Thrasyllus in Theon: an interval is a certain relation (ποιὰ σχέσις) of notes to one another; Vinet has habitudo.
- D: Contrast Boethius I.8, for whom an interval is the distance (distantia) of a high and a low note.


### X10 (Xyl. 41.7–8)

> Systema, complexus est certus intervallorum.

**Sense.** A system is a definite combination of intervals.

**Text proposals and doubts**

- **C**, doubt: printed «Systema, complexus est certus interuallorum (41.7–8)»; proposal: «Система — некое сочетание интервалов.» / 'A system is a certain combination of intervals.'. *Verdict: open*; second pass: confirmed. Certus can approach quidam ('a certain'), but in a definition its normal sense 'fixed, definite' is the better choice; all four chose it. Vinet's quaedam is his own word, not a reason to read certus as quidam. *Evidence*: Lewis & Short s.v. certus, II.A.1.b ('sometimes indef., like quidam, and our certain') [checked]; Vinet V13 'quaedam intervallorum complexio' [checked]

**Best base.** Russian: B = C («определённое сочетание интервалов» is exact; A's «совокупность» is also acceptable.) English: B = C ('a definite combination of intervals'.)

**Notes to keep**

- C: complexus 'combination'; cf. Thrasyllus in Theon (a certain comprehension, ποιὰ περιοχή, of intervals); Vinet has quaedam intervallorum complexio.


### X11 (Xyl. 41.8–9)

> Harmonia, ipsa constructio systematum.

**Sense.** Harmony is the very construction of systems.

**Best base.** Russian: A = C (All four Russian versions are identical and correct.) English: A = C ('the very construction' renders ipsa exactly (D identical).)

**Notes to keep**

- C: Cf. Thrasyllus in Theon: harmony is an arrangement (σύνταξις) of systems such as the Lydian, Phrygian, Dorian.


### X12 (Xyl. 41.9–13)

> Enimvero sonus divisionis capax non est, ut neque punctum in Geometria. Harmoniae participem diximus, quia ita effertur, ut possit etiam cum alio sono ad harmoniam concinnari.

**Sense.** For a note is not capable of division, any more than a point in geometry. We called it 'partaking of harmony' because it is uttered in such a way that it can also be fitted together with another note into harmony.

**Errors**

- **B** (en; omission; minor; second pass: confirmed): «A note admits of no division, any more than a point in geometry.». Enimvero, which marks the explanation of the definition in X08, is not rendered in the English (the Russian has «В самом деле»). *Fix:* For indeed a note admits of no division …

**Best base.** Russian: A = C (Exact, keeping the explanatory structure and concinnari as «слажен».) English: A = C (Exact; the quoted 'partaking of harmony' signals the back-reference to the definition.)

**Notes to keep**

- C: A point is that which has no part (Euclid, Elements I def. 1).


### X13 (Xyl. 41.13–16)

> Quod nisi fiat, iam is dissonus sit sonus, non consonus: imo neque sonus dicetur esse, sed strepitus quispiam temere editus, et ad consonantiam nulli existens usui.

**Sense.** If this does not happen, that note would be dissonant, not consonant; indeed it will not even be called a note, but some noise produced at random and of no use for consonance.

**Errors**

- **B** (en; mistranslation; minor; second pass: confirmed): «the note will be dissonant, with no consonance». non consonus is 'not consonant'; 'with no consonance' turns the adjective into a statement about consonance in general. *Fix:* that note would be dissonant, not consonant

**Best base.** Russian: C = A (Both keep the potential subjunctive (был бы / окажется) and the contrast dissonus / consonus.) English: A = C (Exact, including the shift of mood (would be … will be called).)

**Notes to keep**

- A: dissonus / consonus here describe a single note's fitness to be combined in harmony, not consonance of intervals in the narrow sense of X31–33 (an interpretation; keep it marked as such).


### X14 (Xyl. 41.16–25)

> Proinde diversae sunt sonorum, quas illi progressu et ordine, quem a gravi ad acutum observant, nanciscuntur nomenclationes: Proslambanomenos, Hypate hypaton, parhypate hypaton, hypate diatonos, hypate meson, parhypate meson, diatonos meson, mese monos, paramesos, trite diezeugmene, diatonos diezeugmene, nete diezeugmene, trite hyperbolaeon, diatonos hyperbolaeon, nete hyperbolaeon.

**Sense.** Accordingly, the names that the notes acquire from the progression and order they keep from low to high are various: proslambanomenos, hypate hypaton, parhypate hypaton, hypate diatonos, hypate meson, parhypate meson, diatonos meson, mese monos, paramesos, trite diezeugmene, diatonos diezeugmene, nete diezeugmene, trite hyperbolaeon, diatonos hyperbolaeon, nete hyperbolaeon (fifteen notes, the disjunct two-octave system).

**Divergences**

1. *mese monos*: Whether monos (Greek 'alone, single', left untranslated by Xylander) is kept as part of the name, translated, or taken as a comment ('simply mese').
   - **A**: kept as part of the name in both languages
     - A: «меса монос» / “mese monos”
   - **C**: part of the name; the qualifier translated in Russian, kept in English
     - C: «меса единственная» / “mese monos”
   - **B, D**: a comment on the name: 'simply mese'
     - B: «просто меса» / “simply mese”
     - D: «просто меса» / “simply mese”
   - *Verdict* (medium confidence; to the editor; second pass: confirmed): A and C follow about.md §4.4 ('keep the names as the version gives them'): Xylander prints monos inside the series of names, in the same lower case as the other qualifiers (41.22), and the convention keeps the version's names. B and D interpret it away; both argue it in their doubts, so it is not a fault, but the name as printed disappears. In English 'mese monos' is right. In Russian, «меса монос» keeps the Greek word as the other names keep their Greek base; «меса единственная» translates it as the tetrachord qualifiers are translated. The note should say that monos is Greek 'alone' and that Vinet has Mese solum.
   - *Recommended*: меса монос / mese monos
   - *Evidence*: about.md §4.4 [checked]; printed line 41.22 'mese monos' (diplomatic transcription) [checked]; page image xylander-p41, line 22: 'mese monos,' inside the series, lower case, no break [checked]; Vinet V17 'Mese solum' [checked]; LSJ s.v. μόνος [from memory]

**Text proposals and doubts**

- **B**, doubt: printed «mese monos (41.22)»; proposal: «одна лишь меса» / 'mese alone'. *Verdict: open*; second pass: confirmed. A possible reading of monos as a comment, but the convention keeps the printed name; better kept in a note. *Evidence*: about.md §4.4 [checked]
- **D**, doubt: printed «mese monos (41.22)»; proposal: «меса монос» / 'mese monos'. *Verdict: sound*; second pass: confirmed. D's own alternative is the convention-conforming rendering of the printed name and is preferable to D's chosen 'simply mese'. *Evidence*: about.md §4.4 [checked]
- **C**, doubt: printed «mese monos (41.22)»; proposal: «…меса — одна…» / '…the mese alone…'. *Verdict: unsound*; second pass: confirmed. Nothing in the print sets monos off from the list; C rightly keeps it as part of the series. *Evidence*: printed line 41.22 [checked]
- **A**, doubt: printed «mese monos (41.22)»; proposal: «меса одиночная» / 'the single mese'. *Verdict: open*; second pass: modified. Translating monos is a legitimate option: C's Russian does the same with «меса единственная», which the divergence leaves to the editor; «одиночная» is a less apt word than «единственная». A itself kept the printed name in the text, as about.md §4.4 prefers, and explained monos in a note. *Evidence*: about.md §4.4 [checked]; C's Russian «меса единственная» (X14) [checked]

**Best base.** Russian: A (Exact construal of the relative clause, all fifteen names by the convention, monos kept as printed.) English: A = C (Both keep 'mese monos' and the full construal (progression and order which they keep).)

**Notes to keep**

- C: Departures from the Boethian nomenclature: hypate diatonos for lichanos hypaton, diatonos for lichanos/paranete in three tetrachords, paramesos for paramese, diezeugmene (singular) for diezeugmenon; no synemmenon tetrachord (fifteen notes).
- A: monos is Greek 'alone, single', masculine beside feminine mese, presumably because mese alone bears no tetrachord name; Vinet translates Mese solum.

**For the editor**

- mese monos in Russian: «меса монос» (A) or «меса единственная» (C)? (B and D's «просто меса» drops the printed name.)


### X15 (Xyl. 41.25–42.5)

> Per has sonorum species heccaedecachordum, hoc est, sedecim chordarum instrumentum in quindecim fidibus instructo organo (Pentecaedecachordum vocant Graeci) absolvitur, fine octachordi ad principium sequentium continuo assumpto.

**Sense.** Through these species of notes the hekkaidekachord, that is, an instrument of sixteen strings, is completed on an instrument fitted with fifteen strings (the Greeks call it the pentekaidekachord), the end of the octachord being taken directly as the beginning of the following [notes]: two octachords of eight notes share the eighth note, so sixteen places need fifteen strings.

**Divergences**

1. *heccaedecachordum, hoc est, sedecim chordarum instrumentum ... (Pentecaedecachordum vocant Graeci)*: Transliterate the two Greek compounds, which the Latin itself glosses and labels as Greek, or translate them.
   - **A, C**: transliterated; the Latin gloss and 'vocant Graeci' keep their point
     - A: «геккайдекахорд, то есть инструмент о шестнадцати струнах, … (греки называют его пентекайдекахордом)» / “the hekkaidekachord, that is, the instrument of sixteen strings, … (the Greeks call it a pentekaidekachord)”
     - C: «геккайдекахорд, то есть инструмент из шестнадцати струн, … (греки называют его пентекайдекахордом)» / “the hekkaidekachord, that is, an instrument of sixteen strings, … (the Greeks call it the pentekaidekachord)”
   - **D**: translated as descriptions
     - D: «(греки называют его пятнадцатиструнным) складывается шестнадцатиструнный [инструмент], то есть инструмент с шестнадцатью струнами» / “(the Greeks call it the fifteen-stringed instrument), these kinds of notes complete the sixteen-stringed instrument, that is, an instrument of sixteen strings”
   - **B**: translated, heccaedecachordum as a 'system', Pentecaedecachordum by a coined noun
     - B: «образуют шестнадцатиструнную систему, то есть шестнадцатиструнный инструмент в инструменте, снабжённом пятнадцатью струнами (греки называют его пятнадцатиструнником)» / “complete a sixteen-string system, that is, an instrument of sixteen strings within an instrument equipped with fifteen strings (the Greeks call it a fifteen-string instrument)”
   - *Verdict* (medium confidence; to the editor; second pass: confirmed): A and C. Xylander glosses heccaedecachordum himself (hoc est …) and presents Pentecaedecachordum as the Greek name; translated, the gloss becomes a tautology ('sixteen-stringed [instrument], that is, an instrument of sixteen strings') and 'the Greeks call it fifteen-stringed' loses its point (D). B adds 'system', a term the text defines in X10 and does not use here, and coins «пятнадцатиструнник» (against about.md §4.1). πεντεκαιδεκάχορδος is a real Greek word (Adrastus in Theon; Ptolemy, Harm. III.1, title); ἑκκαιδεκάχορδος is not in LSJ. Whether the running text may carry transliterations here is the editor's decision under §4.1.
   - *Recommended*: Посредством этих видов звуков геккайдекахорд, то есть инструмент из шестнадцати струн, осуществляется на инструменте, снабжённом пятнадцатью струнами (греки называют его пентекайдекахордом) / Through these species of notes the hekkaidekachord, that is, an instrument of sixteen strings, is completed on an instrument fitted with fifteen strings (the Greeks call it the pentekaidekachord)
   - *Evidence*: LSJ s.v. πεντεκαιδεκάχορδος ('with fifteen strings', Adrast. ap. Theon. Sm. p. 64 H.; Ptol. Harm. 3.1 tit.; Porph. in Harm. p. 168 D.), via lsj.gr [checked]; No LSJ headword ἑκκαιδεκάχορδος (lsj.gr page absent) [checked]; Perseus LSJ: entry πεντεκαιδεκάχορδος present; list of headwords beginning ἑκκαιδεκα- (18 entries) has no ἑκκαιδεκάχορδος [checked]; about.md §4.1 [checked]

**Errors**

- **B** (both; term; minor; second pass: confirmed): «шестнадцатиструнную систему … (греки называют его пятнадцатиструнником) / a sixteen-string system». 'system' (the technical systema of X10) is added for heccaedecachordum, which the Latin calls an instrumentum; «пятнадцатиструнник» is an unattested coinage. *Fix:* геккайдекахорд, то есть инструмент из шестнадцати струн … (греки называют его пентекайдекахордом) / the hekkaidekachord, that is, an instrument of sixteen strings … (the Greeks call it the pentekaidekachord)
- **A** (notes; note-claim; minor; second pass: confirmed): «the compounds were looked for as headwords in LSJ via Logeion and not found there [checked]». πεντεκαιδεκάχορδος is an LSJ headword (Adrastus in Theon, Ptolemy, Porphyry); only ἑκκαιδεκάχορδος is missing. *Fix:* πεντεκαιδεκάχορδος is in LSJ ('with fifteen strings': Adrast. ap. Theon.; Ptol. Harm. 3.1 tit.); ἑκκαιδεκάχορδος is not.

**Text proposals and doubts**

- **B**, doubt: printed «fine octachordi ad principium sequentium continuò assumpto (42.4–5)»; proposal: «конец октахорда сразу принимают за начало следующих [октахордов]» / 'the end of the octachord being taken immediately as the beginning of the [octachords] that follow'. *Verdict: unsound*; second pass: confirmed. Only one octachord follows; the plural sequentium more naturally means the following notes (or strings), as B itself chose. *Evidence*: X14–X16 of this text (fifteen notes, 1:8 = 8:15) [checked]

**Best base.** Russian: C = A (Exact construal, transliterations that keep Xylander's gloss meaningful; A's «поскольку» slightly over-interprets the ablative absolute.) English: C = A (Exact, with the ablative absolute kept as a participle.)

**Notes to keep**

- C: The sense of the compressed sentence: two octachords (sixteen places) are realised on fifteen strings because the eighth note ends the first and begins the second; πεντεκαιδεκάχορδος is attested (Adrastus in Theon; Ptolemy, Harm. III.1 title), ἑκκαιδεκάχορδος is not in LSJ.
- A: Xylander has three words for a string: chorda, fides (here), nervus (X29–30, X41–42); all rendered 'string'.

**For the editor**

- heccaedecachordum / Pentecaedecachordum: transliterate in the running text (A, C), which keeps Xylander's gloss and 'vocant Graeci' meaningful, or describe them (D), at the cost of a tautology?


### X16 (Xyl. 42.5–7)

> Quam enim rationem habet primus sonus ad octavum, eandem octavus ad decimumquintum obtinet.

**Sense.** For the ratio that the first note has to the eighth, the eighth holds to the fifteenth (each an octave, 2:1).

**Best base.** Russian: A = C (Keep the correlative quam … eandem («какое … такое же»).) English: A = C (Literal and clear.)


### X17 (Xyl. 42.7)

> Et haec de sonis.

**Sense.** So much for the notes.

**Errors**

- **A** (ru; language; minor; second pass: confirmed): «Это о звуках.». Not an idiomatic closing formula in Russian. *Fix:* Вот и всё о звуках.
- **B** (ru; language; minor; second pass: added): «Вот что касается звуков.». «Вот что касается …» reads in Russian as an announcement of what follows ('here is what concerns the notes:'), not as the closing formula Et haec de sonis ('so much for the notes'); B's English 'So much for notes' is right. *Fix:* Вот и всё о звуках.

**Best base.** Russian: C = D («Вот и всё о звуках» is the idiomatic formula.) English: A = B ('So much for (the) notes' in all four.)


### X18 (Xyl. 42.8–15)

> Intervallorum autem minimum genus est, quod diesin vocant: deinde semitonium, cuius dupla ad acutum est accessio, respectu dieseos. Deinde est tonus duplici constans semitonio, et rationem sesquioctavam constituens, ut est novenarii ad octonarium numerum. Quo fit, ut semitonium sit quasi dimidium sesquioctavae diesis autem quasi quadrans eiusdem.

**Sense.** The smallest kind of interval is the one they call the diesis; next the semitone, whose advance towards the high is double compared with the diesis. Next is the tone, consisting of two semitones and constituting the sesquioctave ratio [9:8], as of nine to eight. Hence the semitone is, as it were, half of the sesquioctave, and the diesis, as it were, a quarter of it.

**Divergences**

1. *Intervallorum autem minimum genus est*: Genus here is the ordinary 'kind': how to render it so that it is neither confused with the three genera of melody nor with species («вид», X15, X26).
   - **A**: «род» / 'kind'
     - A: «Наименьший же род интервалов» / “Of intervals, the smallest kind”
   - **C**: «род» / 'genus' (the Latin word kept visible)
     - C: «Наименьший же род интервалов» / “The smallest genus of intervals”
   - **B, D**: «вид» / 'kind'
     - B: «Наименьший вид интервала» / “The smallest kind of interval”
     - D: «Наименьший вид интервала» / “The smallest kind of interval”
   - *Verdict* (medium confidence; second pass: confirmed): A. Genus is non-technical here; English 'genus' (C) invites confusion with the genera of X44 ff., though C explains its choice. Russian «род» is idiomatic for 'kind' and keeps genus apart from species, which the same translators render «вид» (X15, X26); B and D's «вид» merges the two Latin words. A legitimate choice either way, not an error.
   - *Recommended*: Наименьший же род интервалов — тот, который называют диесой / The smallest kind of interval is the one they call the diesis
   - *Evidence*: Lewis & Short s.v. genus (II: 'kind, sort') [from memory]; X15, X26 (species = «вид») of the translations [checked]

**Text proposals and doubts**

- **A**, text issue: printed «dimidium sesquioctauæ diesis autem (42.14)»; proposal: punctuate: dimidium sesquioctavae, diesis autem. *Verdict: sound*; second pass: confirmed. The print has no stop between the two clauses (line 42.14); a comma is plainly wanted and all four translate accordingly. No effect on sense. *Evidence*: printed line 42.14 (diplomatic transcription) [checked]

**Best base.** Russian: A = C (Exact; both keep quasi («как бы») and the bracket at both ratio names.) English: A (Exact, with 'kind' for genus; C equally exact but 'genus' is misleading.)

**Notes to keep**

- A: The diesis is the quarter-tone; the magnitudes are Aristoxenian (tone = 2 semitones = 4 dieses), the tone is defined by the Pythagorean 9:8; quasi marks that half and quarter of 9:8 are not numerical ratios (argued in X19–25); dupla accessio is an ordinary 'double', no bracket.
- C: Vinet here gives arithmetical numbers (8½ and 8¼ against 8, i.e. 17:16 and 33:32), Xylander a division made geometrical in X19–X20; cf. Boethius I.21 'diesis autem est semitonii dimidium' and I.16 (the tone not divisible into equal halves in numbers).


### X19 (Xyl. 42.15–18)

> Quamvis enim ratione numeri unitas dividi non potest, utique tamen unitas quae in materia conspicitur, dividitur Geometrice.

**Sense.** For although by the reckoning of number unity cannot be divided, yet unity seen in matter is certainly divided geometrically.

**Best base.** Russian: C (Exact, renders utique («безусловно») and the indicative dividitur.) English: C (Exact, including utique ('certainly').)

**Notes to keep**

- A: The excess of 9 over 8 is a unit; halving 9:8 in numbers gives 16:17:18 with 17:16 ≠ 18:17, so an exact half would require dividing the unit, possible only for a magnitude on the string; ratione numeri is not the term 'ratio'.
- C: Theon of Smyrna: the unit is indivisible as number, the sensible one divisible; Theon denies that the tone can be halved exactly, Xylander's text affirms a geometrical division (Theon references not verified here).


### X20 (Xyl. 42.18–21)

> Nam chordam eam quae observat rationem sesquioctavam, possibile est dividi in semisesquioctavam, quod est semitonium, et hoc denuo dividi in diesin.

**Sense.** For the string that keeps the sesquioctave ratio [9:8] can be divided into the semisesquioctave, which is the semitone, and this again into the diesis.

**Best base.** Russian: C = A (Exact; semisesquioctava rendered «полусверхосминное» without bracket, as the convention requires.) English: C = A (Exact.)

**Notes to keep**

- A: Geometrically, half of a ratio is the mean proportional between the two string lengths (between 9 and 8: √72 ≈ 8.485); semisesquioctava is formed like semitonium; Vinet has the word too.


### X21 (Xyl. 42.21–25)

> Geometriae enim est, semper a maiori recta minorem posse detrahere, semperque posse minori apponere maiorem. ex quo sequitur, ut quaelibet figura ad placitum possit cum augeri, tum diminui.

**Sense.** For it belongs to geometry always to be able to take a smaller straight line from a greater and always to add a greater to a smaller; from which it follows that any figure can at will be both increased and diminished.

**Best base.** Russian: A = C (Keep Geometriae est ('геометрии свойственно') and cum … tum.) English: A = C (Exact.)

**Notes to keep**

- C: Recta = recta linea; cf. Euclid, Elements I.3 (cutting off from the greater straight line one equal to the less).


### X22 (Xyl. 42.25–43.1)

> Id vero in Arithmetica locum non habet.

**Sense.** But in arithmetic this has no place.

**Best base.** Russian: A = C («этому нет места» / «не имеет места» keep locum non habet.) English: A = C ('this has no place in arithmetic'.)


### X23 (Xyl. 43.1–10)

> Ad haec, propositum quadratum in aequalia partiri quadrata, aut duplum efficere non potest. Numerorum enim sedecim, qui est quadratus, neque in duo potest diducere quadrata (octo enim Arithmetice quadratum esse, aut eius numeri dare radicem quadratam, impossibile est) neque duplum eius aliud quadratum dare (fieri enim non potest, ut numerus triginta duo sit quadratus) At Geometriae hoc factu non est difficile.

**Sense.** Moreover, [arithmetic] cannot divide a proposed square into equal squares, or make it double. For sixteen among numbers (the number sixteen), which is a square, it can neither split into two squares (for it is impossible for eight to be a square arithmetically, or to give the square root of that number), nor give another square double of it (for it cannot be that the number thirty-two is a square). But for geometry this is not difficult to do.

**Divergences**

1. *propositum quadratum in aequalia partiri quadrata, aut duplum efficere non potest*: The understood subject of non potest (arithmetic, from X22) and whether it is marked as an addition.
   - **A, C**: subject 'arithmetic', supplied in brackets
     - A: «Кроме того, [арифметика] не может разделить предложенный квадрат на равные квадраты» / “Moreover, [arithmetic] cannot divide a given square into equal squares”
     - C: «Кроме того, [арифметика] не может ни разделить предложенный квадрат на равные квадраты» / “Moreover, [arithmetic] cannot divide a proposed square into equal squares”
   - **D**: subject 'arithmetic', supplied without brackets
     - D: «Кроме того, арифметика не может ни разделить данный квадрат на равные квадраты» / “Moreover, arithmetic cannot divide a given square into equal squares”
   - **B**: impersonal passive, no subject
     - B: «Кроме того, данный квадрат нельзя разделить на равные квадраты» / “Moreover, a given square cannot be divided into equal squares”
   - *Verdict* (high confidence; second pass: confirmed): A and C: potest needs a subject, which is Arithmetica from X22 (Id vero in Arithmetica locum non habet), and the contrast is closed by At Geometriae; the supplied word belongs in brackets (about.md §4.6). D's unbracketed addition is a sign fault (listed below). B's impersonal rendering keeps the sense but blurs the subject; acceptable.
   - *Recommended*: Кроме того, [арифметика] не может ни разделить предложенный квадрат на равные квадраты, ни сделать его двойным. / Moreover, [arithmetic] cannot divide a proposed square into equal squares, or make it double.
   - *Evidence*: X22 of this text [checked]; about.md §4.6 [checked]
2. *Numerorum enim sedecim, qui est quadratus*: The printed genitive plural Numerorum: rendered as a partitive ('among numbers, sixteen') or as 'the number sixteen'.
   - **A**: literal partitive 'among numbers, sixteen'
     - A: «Ведь среди чисел шестнадцать, которое есть квадрат, она не может ни разложить на два квадрата» / “For among numbers sixteen, which is a square, it can neither part into two squares”
   - **B, C, D**: 'the number sixteen' (C explicitly as if Numerum)
     - B: «Ведь число шестнадцать — квадратное, но его нельзя ни разложить на два [равных] квадрата» / “For one cannot split the number sixteen, which is square, into two [equal] squares”
     - C: «Ведь число шестнадцать, которое является квадратом, она не может ни разложить на два квадрата» / “For it cannot split the number sixteen, which is a square, into two squares”
     - D: «Таково, например, квадратное число шестнадцать. Арифметика не может ни разложить его на два квадрата» / “Take the number sixteen, which is a square. Arithmetic can neither resolve it into two squares”
   - *Verdict* (medium confidence; second pass: confirmed): Both are acceptable: the printed partitive with numerus understood before qui (A's and D's analysis) and the conjectured Numerum give the same sense, so 'the number sixteen' is a fair rendering of either; A is more literal but stiff. The printed form should be recorded in a note (see text issues).
   - *Recommended*: Ведь число шестнадцать, которое есть квадрат, она не может ни разложить на два квадрата / For the number sixteen, which is a square, it can neither split into two squares
   - *Evidence*: Allen & Greenough §346 (partitive genitive) [from memory]; page image xylander-p43, line 3 'Numerorum' [checked]

**Errors**

- **D** (both; sign; minor; second pass: modified): «Кроме того, арифметика не может … / Moreover, arithmetic cannot …». The supplied subject (from X22) is not marked as an addition, in either language (about.md §4.6). *Fix:* Кроме того, [арифметика] не может … / Moreover, [arithmetic] cannot …
- **D** (both; addition; minor; second pass: confirmed): «Таково, например, квадратное число шестнадцать. / Take the number sixteen, which is a square.». 'for example' is added and the causal enim ('for') is dropped, so the example no longer gives the reason for the preceding claim. *Fix:* Ведь число шестнадцать, которое есть квадрат, [арифметика] не может … / For the number sixteen, which is a square, [arithmetic] can neither …

**Text proposals and doubts**

- **A**, text issue: printed «Numerorum enim sedecim, qui est quadratus (43.3)»; proposal: Numerum enim sedecim, qui est quadratus. *Verdict: open*; second pass: confirmed. The print reads Numerorum (confirmed on the page image); Numerum would give diducere an object and qui a singular antecedent, a likely slip (-orum for -um), but the partitive is construable and the sense unchanged. Keep the reading text; record the conjecture in a note. *Evidence*: page image xylander-p43, line 3 [checked]
- **B**, text issue: printed «Numerorum enim sedecim, qui est quadratus (43.3)»; proposal: Numerum enim sedecim, qui est quadratus. *Verdict: open*; second pass: confirmed. As for A: plausible, unnecessary for the sense. *Evidence*: page image xylander-p43, line 3 [checked]
- **C**, text issue: printed «Numerorum enim sedecim, qui est (43.3)»; proposal: Numerum enim sedecim, qui est quadratus. *Verdict: open*; second pass: confirmed. As for A; C's parallel 'numerus triginta duo' a few lines below supports the expected construction, but the printed partitive remains construable. *Evidence*: page image xylander-p43, lines 3 and 8 [checked]

**Best base.** Russian: C = A (Both bracket the subject and render every clause; C reads more naturally («число шестнадцать»).) English: C (Exact, bracketed subject, natural 'the number sixteen'.)

**Notes to keep**

- C: The two dot figures in the outer margin of p. 43: a 4 × 4 square of 16 points cut after the second row into 8 + 8, and eight rows of 4 points cut after the fourth row into 16 + 16 (confirmed on the page image); A and B describe them the same way.
- D: Sixteen can be divided into four equal square numbers (4 × 4); the impossibility concerns two equal squares, as the figure shows.


### X24 (Xyl. 43.10–14)

> nam Geometer dati quadrati latere loco diametri accepto, ubi circumposuit quadratum, id statim propositi est dimidium: iterumque accepta dati quadrati diametro loco lateris, ex eo quadratum describet, quod propositum dupli esse constat.

**Sense.** For the geometer, taking the side of the given square as the diagonal, as soon as he has set a square about it, that is at once half of the proposed one; and again, taking the diagonal of the given square as the side, he will describe on it a square which, as is agreed, is the proposed [square] of double [size] (printed: propositum dupli; the expected propositi duplum would mean 'double the proposed').

**Divergences**

1. *quod propositum dupli esse constat*: How to translate the printed propositum dupli, where the parallel 'propositi est dimidium' leads one to expect propositi duplum.
   - **C**: printed text: propositum predicate, dupli genitive of measure ('the proposed [square] of double [size]')
     - C: «который, как известно, есть предложенный [квадрат] в двойном размере» / “which, as is evident, is the proposed [square] of double [size]”
   - **D**: printed text: propositum 'the thing proposed', dupli 'of the double' ('the doubling proposed')
     - D: «и тем самым, очевидно, получит искомое удвоение» / “which clearly gives the doubling proposed”
   - **A, B**: the sense of the conjecture propositi duplum ('double the proposed/given')
     - A: «который, как известно, вдвое больше предложенного» / “which is known to be the double of the one proposed”
     - B: «который, как ясно, вдвое больше данного» / “evidently twice the size of the original”
   - *Verdict* (medium confidence; to the editor; second pass: modified): C and D translate the reading text; A and B translate the conjecture propositi duplum, which they argue in text_issues (minor errors below). The geometric sense is the same in all four (the square on the diagonal has twice the area). C's construal is closest to the letters; D's ('the doubling proposed', i.e. the task set in X23, duplum efficere) is equally possible and more natural. Whether to adopt the conjecture is for the editor.
   - *Recommended*: …он опишет на ней квадрат, который, как известно, и есть предложенный [квадрат] в двойном размере. / …he will describe on it a square which, as is evident, is the proposed [square] of double [size].
   - *Evidence*: page image xylander-p43, line 14 'propositũ dupli' [checked]; X23 'propositum quadratum … duplum efficere' [checked]; Lewis & Short s.v. duplus, II.A (substantival duplum; genitive dupli, e.g. 'furem dupli condemnari', Cato) [checked]; Allen & Greenough §345 (genitive of quality/measure) [from memory]

**Errors**

- **A** (both; construal; minor; second pass: confirmed): «который, как известно, вдвое больше предложенного / which is known to be the double of the one proposed». Translates the conjecture propositi duplum (A's own text issue), not the printed propositum dupli; the sense is unchanged, but the emendation belongs in the note. *Fix:* который, как известно, есть предложенный [квадрат] в двойном размере / which is agreed to be the proposed [square] of double [size]
- **B** (both; construal; minor; second pass: confirmed): «который, как ясно, вдвое больше данного / evidently twice the size of the original». Translates the conjecture propositi duplum (B's text issue) rather than the printed text; same geometric sense. *Fix:* который, как известно, есть предложенный [квадрат] в двойном размере / which, as is evident, is the proposed [square] of double [size]

**Text proposals and doubts**

- **A**, text issue: printed «quod propositū dupli esse cōstat (43.14)»; proposal: quod propositi duplum esse constat. *Verdict: open* (to the editor); second pass: confirmed. Attractive (parallel propositi … dimidium; exchange of endings is an easy slip), but the printed words can be construed (C, D) and the geometry is unaffected; keep the reading text and give the conjecture in a note. The print is confirmed on the page image. *Evidence*: page image xylander-p43, line 14 [checked]
- **B**, text issue: printed «quod propositũ dupli esse cōstat (43.14)»; proposal: quod propositi duplum esse constat. *Verdict: open* (to the editor); second pass: confirmed. As for A. *Evidence*: page image xylander-p43, line 14 [checked]
- **C**, text issue: printed «dratum describet, quod propositum dupli esse constat (43.13–14)»; proposal: quod propositi duplum esse constat. *Verdict: open* (to the editor); second pass: confirmed. C rightly translates the reading text and offers the conjecture as an alternative; the difference in sense is slight. *Evidence*: page image xylander-p43, line 14 [checked]
- **D**, text issue: printed «quod propositum dupli esse constat (43.14)»; proposal: quod propositi duplum esse constat; alternative «который, как известно, вдвое больше исходного квадрата» / 'which is known to be twice the original square'. *Verdict: open* (to the editor); second pass: confirmed. As for A; D translates the reading text and records the conjecture correctly. *Evidence*: page image xylander-p43, line 14 [checked]

**Best base.** Russian: D = C (Both translate the printed text; D is idiomatic («искомое удвоение»), C closer to the letters and keeps the anacoluthon.) English: C = D (Both translate the printed text; C is the more literal.)

**Notes to keep**

- A: diametrum is the diagonal (Greek usage); doubling the square on the diagonal is the construction of Plato's Meno (82b–85b), the halving reverses it; circumposuit 'has placed around'.
- C: Geometer is a late Latin form (Boethius' Topics) for classical geometres; the first clause is an anacoluthon (geometer gives way to id).

**For the editor**

- X24 'quod propositum dupli esse constat': translate the print (C, D) and give the conjecture propositi duplum in a note, or adopt the conjecture (A, B)?


### X25 (Xyl. 43.15–16)

> Sic etiam sesquioctava toni proportio dividi poterit in semitonium, iterumque in diesin.

**Sense.** So too the sesquioctave proportion [9:8] of the tone can be divided into the semitone, and again into the diesis.

**Divergences**

1. *sesquioctava toni proportio*: proportio for a numerical ratio: kept as 'proportion' (Xylander's word, distinct from his ratio) or rendered 'ratio'.
   - **A, B, C**: «пропорция» / 'proportion'
     - A: «сверхосминная [9:8] пропорция тона» / “the sesquioctave [9:8] proportion of the tone”
     - B: «сверхосминную [9:8] пропорцию тона» / “the sesquioctave [9:8] proportion of the tone”
     - C: «сверхосминная [9:8] пропорция тона» / “the sesquioctave [9:8] proportion of the tone”
   - **D**: «отношение» / 'ratio'
     - D: «сверхосминное [9:8] отношение тона» / “the sesquioctave [9:8] ratio of the tone”
   - *Verdict* (medium confidence; to the editor; second pass: confirmed): Both are legitimate. Xylander, like Boethius, uses proportio for the ratio of two numbers, and he also writes ratio for the same thing (X16, X18, X20); A and C keep the Latin words apart deliberately (their notes to X02), D renders by sense (and has «пропорция» for proportio in X02–X03). about.md does not fix the word; the version should be consistent.
   - *Recommended*: сверхосминная [9:8] пропорция тона / the sesquioctave [9:8] proportion of the tone
   - *Evidence*: Boethius, De inst. mus. I.10, ed. Friedlein p. 198 ('secundum epitritam proportionem'; 'in sesquioctava proportione resonabant tonum'), TML transcription [checked]; about.md §4.5 (other words consistent within a version) [checked]

**Best base.** Russian: C (Exact and natural; A's «сможет быть разделена» is stiffer.) English: A = C (Exact.)

**For the editor**

- proportio as a numerical ratio (X25, X35–41): «пропорция» / 'proportion' (A, B, C) to keep it distinct from ratio, or «отношение» / 'ratio' (D)?


### X26 (Xyl. 43.16–21)

> Eorundem quoque auctione, compositae quoque reliquae intervallorum species constituuntur: tridiesis, trisemitonium, ditonus, tritonus, et quo quis modo voluerit, idque rhythmus admiserit, veluti ex sequentibus cognoscetur.

**Sense.** By augmentation of the same (diesis, semitone, tone) the remaining species of intervals are also constituted, these too composite: the tridiesis, the trihemitone, the ditone, the tritone, and [others] in whatever way one wishes and rhythm admits, as will be learned from what follows.

**Divergences**

1. *compositae quoque reliquae intervallorum species constituuntur*: Whether the second quoque is rendered with compositae ('these too composite'), which prepares the contrast with Incomposita autem dicuntur in X27.
   - **C**: 'the remaining species, likewise composite'
     - C: «образуются и остальные виды интервалов, также составные» / “the remaining species of intervals, likewise composite, are also constituted”
   - **A, B, D**: attributive 'the remaining composite species', the second quoque absorbed
     - A: «образуются и прочие, составные, виды интервалов» / “the remaining, composite species of intervals are also constituted”
     - B: «образуются и остальные составные виды интервала» / “the remaining composite kinds of interval are also formed”
     - D: «образуются и остальные виды интервалов — составные» / “one also forms the other, composite kinds of intervals”
   - *Verdict* (medium confidence; second pass: modified): C. Quoque is regularly placed after the word it emphasizes (L&S), so compositae quoque is 'these too composite'; C renders it and so makes visible that Xylander first calls these intervals composite and then (X27) explains why they are nevertheless called incomposite. A, B and D attach the 'also' to reliquae and use compositae attributively; with the doubled quoque partly pleonastic this is acceptable, not an error.
   - *Recommended*: Их же наращиванием образуются и остальные виды интервалов, также составные: / By the augmentation of the same the remaining species of intervals, likewise composite, are also constituted:
   - *Evidence*: page image xylander-p43, line 17 'compositæ quoque' [checked]; X27 'Incomposita autem dicuntur' [checked]; Lewis & Short s.v. quoque, I ('also, too (subjoined to the emphatic word in a clause)') [checked]

**Text proposals and doubts**

- **B**, text issue: printed «compositæ quoque reliquæ inter- / uallorum species (43.17–18)»; proposal: Possibly incompositae quoque reliquae intervallorum species. *Verdict: open* (to the editor); second pass: modified. The print clearly has compositae (page image). The transmitted text makes sense with an adversative autem in X27 (composite in formation, called incomposite because sounded as one interval), so no emendation is needed to translate Xylander. Incompositae would read smoothly with X27 and X29 ('neque incomposita'), and Vinet's version has simplicia at the same point (V31 'alia quoque simplicia intervalla fiunt'), which suggests what the Greek said but not what Xylander wrote; the conjecture is worth a note. B translates the reading text, correctly. *Evidence*: page image xylander-p43, line 17 [checked]; X27, X29 of this text [checked]; Vinet V31 in latin-texts.json ('Horum autem adauctu, alia quoque simplicia intervalla fiunt') [checked]
- **C**, text issue: printed «quoque auctione, compositæ quoque reliquæ in- (43.17)»; proposal: Keep compositae; incompositae possible but unnecessary. *Verdict: sound*; second pass: confirmed. Agrees with the page image and with the adversative reading of X27. *Evidence*: page image xylander-p43, line 17 [checked]

**Best base.** Russian: C (Renders both quoque, keeps compositae and the open-ended clause without unbracketed additions.) English: C (Same reasons; A equally exact except for the second quoque.)

**Notes to keep**

- C: compositae here and incomposita in X27: Xylander calls the enlarged intervals composite and then explains why they are 'called incomposite' (adversative autem); cf. X29, X49; Vinet has simplicia.
- A: rhythmus is unexpected in an argument about the size of intervals (one expects 'melody' or 'scale'); translated as it stands.
- D: In tones: tridiesis ¾, trihemitone 1½, ditone 2 (81:64), tritone 3 (729:512).

**For the editor**

- compositae (print) against the possible conjecture incompositae (B): keep the print with a note?


### X27 (Xyl. 43.21–44.1)

> Incomposita autem dicuntur, quia tanquam una cantilena ex coalescentibus in unum tribus diesibus, aut semitoniis totidem, aut diversis tonis conflatur, potentia quidem sive facultate diversa intervalla continens, sed unico tamen intervallo edita.

**Sense.** But they are called incomposite because, as it were, a single tune is fused together from three dieses coalescing into one, or as many semitones, or several tones, containing in potency or capacity different intervals, yet uttered as one single interval.

**Divergences**

1. *aut diversis tonis*: diversis: 'different' or, in later usage, 'several / separate' (the ditone and tritone are made of two or three equal tones).
   - **A, C**: 'various / several' tones, i.e. two or three
     - A: «или различных тонов» / “or various tones”
     - C: «или из нескольких тонов» / “or from several tones”
   - **B, D**: 'different / distinct' tones
     - B: «или разных тонов» / “or different tones”
     - D: «или из разных тонов» / “or distinct tones”
   - *Verdict* (medium confidence; second pass: confirmed): C, with A (whose note gives the same sense). The tones in question are equal 9:8 steps, two or three of them (ditone, tritone, X26); diversus here is 'separate, several' (L&S II.A 'apart, separate'; later 'various, several'), and 'different tones' may suggest tones of different sizes. Not an error in B and D, but less clear. A's Russian «различных» has the same weakness as B and D; its English 'various' is fine.
   - *Recommended*: или из нескольких тонов / or from several tones
   - *Evidence*: Lewis & Short s.v. diverto (P. a. diversus), II ('In different directions, apart, separate'; 'a diversis emebantur, by various people', Cic. Phil. 2.37) [checked]; Du Cange s.v. diversus ('Varius') [from memory]; X26 list: ditonus, tritonus [checked]

**Best base.** Russian: C (Exact construal (cantilena as subject, participles agreeing with it), «нескольких тонов», natural Russian.) English: C (Exact; A equally exact ('fused', 'one single interval').)

**Notes to keep**

- C: potentia sive facultate is the Aristotelian 'potency' glossed by facultas; cantilena is Boethius' word for melody (heading of I.21 'De generibus cantilenae'); diversis tonis = several tones (Vinet: multis etiam tonis).
- A: The intervals called composite in magnitude in X26 are called incomposite as one step in melody, with no note between (the doctrine of the incomposite interval bounded by successive notes; cf. X49 trisemitonium incompositum, Cleonides 5).


### X28 (Xyl. 44.2–5)

> Praeter haec sunt et alia intervalla: diatessaron, quartam vulgo dicunt: diapente, iisdem quinta est: et diapason, quam trito vocabulo octavam nominant.

**Sense.** Besides these there are other intervals: diatessaron ('through four'), which is commonly called quarta; diapente ('through five'), which for the same people is quinta; and diapason ('through all'), which by a well-worn word they name octava.

**Divergences**

1. *diatessaron, quartam vulgo dicunt: diapente, iisdem quinta est: et diapason, quam trito vocabulo octavam nominant*: The convention renders both the Greek names and their Latin equivalents as 'fourth, fifth, octave'; how to keep Xylander's gloss of the Greek names by the common Latin ones visible.
   - **A**: Greek names rendered literally ('through four'); the common Latin names by the established terms
     - A: ««через четыре», который в обиходе называют квартой; «через пять» — у них же это квинта; и «через все», который расхожим словом именуют октавой» / “the ‘through four’, which they commonly call the fourth; the ‘through five’, which for the same people is the fifth; and the ‘through all’, which they name by the well-worn word ‘octave’”
   - **C**: Greek names transliterated; the common Latin names by the established terms
     - C: «диатессарон — в обиходе его называют квартой; диапенте — у тех же это квинта; и диапазон, который расхожим словом именуют октавой» / “diatessaron, which is commonly called the fourth; diapente, which for the same people is the fifth; and diapason, which by a well-worn word they name the octave”
   - **D**: Russian: Greek names by the established terms with literal glosses in brackets, the common names as literal ordinals; English: Greek names literal, common names by the established terms
     - D: «Кварту [«через четыре»] в обиходе называют «четвёртой», квинту [«через пять»] те же люди называют «пятой», а октаву [«через все»], по привычному выражению, — «восьмой».» / “The interval ‘through four’ is commonly called the fourth, ‘through five’ is what the same people call the fifth, and ‘through all’ goes by the familiar name of octave.”
   - **B**: Russian: Greek names by the established terms, common names as literal ordinals; English: the Greek names dropped
     - B: «Кварту обычно называют «четвёртой», квинту те же люди называют «пятой», а для октавы употребляют расхожее слово «восьмая».» / “The fourth and fifth go by those names in ordinary speech, and the octave is commonly called the “eighth.””
   - *Verdict* (medium confidence; to the editor; second pass: modified): A or C. The modern established terms descend from the common Latin names that Xylander cites (quarta, quinta, octava; English fourth, fifth, octave), so the established term belongs on them, and the Greek names need a literal rendering (A) or a transliteration (C), as about.md §4.1 allows where a name is explained; both explain their departure from §4.2 in a note. B's and D's Russian invert the historical relation (diatessaron becomes «кварта», quarta becomes «четвёртая») but stay intelligible; D's English follows the opposite strategy to its Russian without comment; B's English loses the Greek names and with them the content of the sentence (major error below). The choice between literal and transliterated Greek names must agree with X29–X30 and X35. Literal glosses added to transliterated names in the running text go in square brackets (about.md §4.6), or into a note, since X29 explains the names at once. If transliteration is chosen, the note should warn that Russian «диапазон» is also the everyday word for 'range'.
   - *Recommended*: Кроме них есть и другие интервалы: диатессарон [«через четыре»] — в обиходе его называют квартой; диапенте [«через пять»] — у тех же это квинта; и диапазон [«через все»], который расхожим словом именуют октавой. / Besides these there are also other intervals: diatessaron [‘through four’], which is commonly called the fourth; diapente [‘through five’], which for the same people is the fifth; and diapason [‘through all’], which by a well-worn word they name the octave.
   - *Evidence*: about.md §4.1 (explaining names) and §4.2 [checked]; Gaffiot s.v. tritus ('usité, commun') [from memory]; about.md §4.6 [checked]; Ozhegov–Shvedova s.v. диапазон ('range of a voice or instrument; scope') [from memory]

**Errors**

- **B** (en; omission; major; second pass: confirmed): «The fourth and fifth go by those names in ordinary speech, and the octave is commonly called the “eighth.”». The Greek names diatessaron, diapente, diapason, which the sentence exists to gloss by the common Latin names, disappear; 'the fourth and fifth go by those names' is circular, and the English reader cannot follow X29, which explains the Greek names. *Fix:* Besides these there are also other intervals: diatessaron (‘through four’), which is commonly called the fourth; diapente (‘through five’), which for the same people is the fifth; and diapason (‘through all’), which by a well-worn word they call the octave.
- **D** (en; consistency; minor; second pass: confirmed): «The interval ‘through four’ is commonly called the fourth». The English renders the Greek names literally against about.md §4.2 while the Russian keeps «кварта» for diatessaron and makes quarta «четвёртая»; the two languages follow opposite strategies and neither departure is explained. *Fix:* Use one strategy in both languages and say why in a note (e.g. A's or C's).

**Best base.** Russian: C = A (Both keep the established term on quarta/quinta/octava and the Greek names visible (transliterated or literal), with a note explaining the departure.) English: C = A (Same; C's transliteration reads more naturally in English.)

**Notes to keep**

- A: Xylander glosses the Greek names with the current Latin quarta, quinta, octava (vulgo; trito vocabulo 'by the worn, common word'); hence the Greek names are given literally/transliterated here and the common names by the established term.
- B: Vinet uses only the Greek names, with hyphens (Dia-tessaron etc.).

**For the editor**

- X28–X30: Greek interval names literal («через четыре», A) or transliterated («диатессарон», C) where Xylander glosses them by quarta/quinta/octava? (B's English drops them; B and D's Russian put «кварта» on diatessaron.)


### X29 (Xyl. 44.5–13)

> Haec autem neque immediata sunt, neque incomposita, sed per media omnia dicta intervalla transeunt. Atque haec quidem intervalla dicuntur, ratione sonitus qui est a primo sono ad extremum: quantitate autem intermediorum sonorum huiusmodi appellationes invenerunt, diatessaron quidem, quia per quatuor sonos aut nervos transit: diapente, quia per quinque: diapason, quia per omnes, hoc est, per octo.

**Sense.** These, however, are neither immediate nor incomposite, but pass through all the aforesaid intervals lying between. And they are called intervals by reason of the sounding from the first note to the last, but from the number of intermediate notes they got such names: diatessaron because it passes through four notes or strings; diapente, through five; diapason, through all, that is, through eight.

**Divergences**

1. *diatessaron quidem, quia per quatuor sonos aut nervos transit: diapente, quia per quinque: diapason, quia per omnes*: How the etymological explanation of the names is kept readable (about.md §4.1).
   - **A**: established term with the literal meaning in brackets, for all three
     - A: «кварта [«через четыре»] — потому что проходит через четыре звука, или струны; квинта [«через пять»] — потому что через пять; октава [«через все»] — потому что через все, то есть через восемь» / “the fourth [‘through four’], because it passes through four notes or strings; the fifth [‘through five’], because through five; the octave [‘through all’], because through all, that is, through eight”
   - **C**: Greek names transliterated, as in X28
     - C: «диатессарон — потому что проходит через четыре звука, или струны; диапенте — потому что через пять; диапазон — потому что через все, то есть через восемь» / “diatessaron, because it passes through four notes or strings; diapente, because through five; diapason, because through all, that is, through eight”
   - **B**: established terms only; literal meanings given in a note
     - B: «кварта — потому что проходит через четыре звука или струны, квинта — через пять, октава — через все, то есть через восемь» / “the fourth because it passes through four notes or strings, the fifth because it passes through five, and the octave because it passes through all of them, that is, through eight”
   - **D**: established terms, the literal meaning bracketed for the fourth only
     - D: «Так, кварта [называется «через четыре»], потому что проходит через четыре звука или струны, квинта — через пять, октава — через все, то есть через восемь.» / “The fourth [is called ‘through four’] because it passes through four notes or strings, the fifth because it passes through five, and the octave because it passes through all, that is, through eight.”
   - *Verdict* (medium confidence; to the editor; second pass: confirmed): A and C keep the explanation readable in the running text (A by the established term plus literal rendering, as §4.1 suggests; C by transliteration consistent with its X28). B relies on a note, which §4.1 allows, but its text 'the octave because it passes through all of them' is opaque without it. D glosses only the fourth. The choice must match the one made for X28.
   - *Recommended*: кварта [«через четыре»] — потому что проходит через четыре звука, или струны; квинта [«через пять»] — потому что через пять; октава [«через все»] — потому что через все, то есть через восемь / the fourth [‘through four’], because it passes through four notes or strings; the fifth [‘through five’], because through five; the octave [‘through all’], because through all, that is, through eight
   - *Evidence*: about.md §4.1 [checked]
2. *huiusmodi appellationes invenerunt*: Subject of invenerunt: the intervals ('got such names') or people ('devised such names').
   - **A, C, D**: the intervals received/acquired the names
     - A: «а такие названия они получили по количеству промежуточных звуков» / “but from the quantity of the intermediate notes they have found names of this kind”
     - C: «а по количеству промежуточных звуков они получили такие наименования» / “but from the quantity of the intermediate notes they received names of this kind”
     - D: «а свои особые названия они получили по числу промежуточных звуков» / “They acquired their particular names from the number of intervening notes.”
   - **B**: people devised the names (impersonal passive)
     - B: «а названия такого рода были придуманы по числу промежуточных звуков» / “Their particular names were devised from the number of intervening notes”
   - *Verdict* (medium confidence; second pass: confirmed): A, C, D: the subject continues from 'haec … intervalla dicuntur', and X30 'nomina, quae sequentibus intervallis obtigerunt' treats the intervals as the recipients of names; invenio can mean 'to get, obtain'. B's reading is grammatically possible (an indefinite 'they') and does not change the doctrine.
   - *Recommended*: а по количеству промежуточных звуков они получили такие наименования / but from the number of the intermediate notes they received names of this kind
   - *Evidence*: X30 'nomina, quae sequentibus intervallis obtigerunt' [checked]; Lewis & Short s.v. invenio, II.D ('To acquire, get, earn': 'qui primus hoc cognomen invenit', Cic. Fin. 1.7.23) [checked]

**Errors**

- **B** (both; construal; minor; second pass: confirmed): «поскольку проходят через все названные промежуточные интервалы / for they pass through all the intermediate intervals just mentioned». sed ('but') is turned into a causal 'for'; the Latin contrasts what these intervals are not with what they do. *Fix:* но проходят через все названные промежуточные интервалы / but pass through all the aforesaid intervals in between

**Best base.** Russian: A (Exact construal, and the etymologies readable through established term plus literal rendering.) English: A (Same reasons; C equally exact if transliteration is chosen in X28.)

**Notes to keep**

- A: The counts (4, 5, 8) include both extreme notes although the text speaks of 'intermediate' notes; sonitus (the sound filling the span) as distinct from sonus (note), cf. X37.
- C: διὰ τεσσάρων 'through four', διὰ πέντε 'through five', διὰ πασῶν 'through all [the strings]'; immediatus is scholastic Latin 'without a middle' (Vinet: non sine medio).


### X30 (Xyl. 44.13–20)

> Nam quia omnia intervalla complentur in octachordo, atque inde iterum geminantur, diapason dicitur, quod per octo nervos transit. Argumento sunt etiam nomina, quae sequentibus intervallis obtigerunt, ut sunt diapason cum diatessaron, diapason cum diapente: disdiapason, hoc est, octava cum quarta, octava cum quinta, et duae octavae.

**Sense.** For since all the intervals are completed within the octachord and from there are doubled again, it is called diapason ('through all'), because it passes through eight strings [or: that which passes through eight strings is called diapason]. The names that fell to the following (larger) intervals are further proof: diapason with diatessaron, diapason with diapente, disdiapason, that is (in Xylander's own Latin terms) 'octava cum quarta', 'octava cum quinta' and 'duae octavae'.

**Divergences**

1. *diapason dicitur, quod per octo nervos transit*: quod: causal conjunction ('is called diapason because it passes through eight strings') or neuter relative supplying the subject ('that which passes through eight strings is called diapason')
   - **A, B, C**: causal quod; subject of dicitur understood (the octave)
     - A: «[интервал] называется октавой [«через все»], потому что проходит через восемь струн» / “it is called the octave [‘through all’], because it passes through eight strings”
     - B: «октава называется «через все», так как проходит через восемь струн» / “The octave is called “through all” because it passes through eight strings”
     - C: «[этот интервал] называется диапазоном, поскольку проходит через восемь струн» / “it is called diapason, because it passes through eight strings”
   - **D**: relative quod as subject of dicitur
     - D: «Поэтому интервал, проходящий через восемь струн, называют октавой [«через все»]» / “Hence the interval that passes through eight strings is called the octave [‘through all’]”
   - *Verdict* (medium confidence; second pass: confirmed): Both construals are grammatical. The causal reading is preferable: 'X dicitur, quod ...' is the standing Latin formula of etymological explanation, and it continues the pattern of X29 ('diapason, quia per omnes, hoc est, per octo'); the quia-clause explains why 'all' equals eight, the quod-clause states the name's ground. D's relative reading is defensible but not required; it does not change the doctrine.
   - *Recommended*: [этот интервал] называется октавой [«через все»], потому что проходит через восемь струн / it is called the octave [‘through all’], because it passes through eight strings
   - *Evidence*: Allen and Greenough §540 (causal quod with indicative) [from memory]; X29 of this text, parallel 'quia per omnes, hoc est, per octo' [checked]
