2. *diapason cum diatessaron, diapason cum diapente: disdiapason, hoc est, octava cum quarta, octava cum quinta, et duae octavae*: Xylander glosses the Greek names by the Latin ordinal names; if both are rendered by the established terms, the gloss becomes a tautology. How is the double naming kept visible?
   - **A**: Greek names rendered literally, Xylander's Latin gloss by the established terms
     - A: ««через все» с «через четыре», «через все» с «через пять», «дважды через все», то есть октава с квартой, октава с квинтой и две октавы» / “the ‘through all’ with the ‘through four’, the ‘through all’ with the ‘through five’, the ‘twice through all’, that is, the octave with a fourth, the octave with a fifth, and two octaves”
   - **B**: both name-sets rendered by the established terms (tautological gloss); explanation only in the note
     - B: «октава с квартой, октава с квинтой и двойная октава, то есть октава вместе с квартой, октава вместе с квинтой и две октавы» / “the octave and a fourth, the octave and a fifth, and the double octave, that is, an octave with a fourth, an octave with a fifth, and two octaves”
   - **C**: Greek names transliterated in the running text, the Latin gloss by the established terms
     - C: «диапазон с диатессароном, диапазон с диапенте, дисдиапазон, то есть октава с квартой, октава с квинтой и две октавы» / “diapason with diatessaron, diapason with diapente, disdiapason, that is, the octave and a fourth, the octave and a fifth, and two octaves”
   - **D**: Greek names by the established terms, Xylander's Latin ordinals rendered literally in quotation marks
     - D: «октава с квартой, октава с квинтой, двойная октава, то есть «восьмая с четвёртой», «восьмая с пятой» и «две восьмых»» / “the octave and a fourth, the octave and a fifth, and the double octave, that is, ‘an eighth with a fourth’, ‘an eighth with a fifth’, and ‘two eighths’”
   - *Verdict* (medium confidence; to the editor; second pass: modified): about.md § 4.1 asks that the explanation of a name stay readable: the established term and the literal rendering side by side, or an explanation in a note. A does this in the running text, and its solution fits the function of the Latin best: Xylander glosses the Greek names by the names current in his own Latin (octava, quarta, quinta), which are exactly the modern established terms. D does the reverse (established terms for the Greek names, Xylander's Latin ordinals literally): acceptable, but «две восьмых» / 'two eighths' reads as the fraction 2/8. B renders both sets by the established terms; this follows § 4.2 but turns the 'hoc est' gloss into a tautology, explained only in its notes, and it departs from B's own solution in X28 and X35, where the Latin ordinals are rendered literally («четвёртой», «восьмая»). C keeps transliterations in the running text, a departure from § 4.2 for which it gives its reason in its X28 note (to keep Xylander's double naming visible); but transliterations are not terms a modern reader understands (§ 4.1), and Russian «диапазон» is the everyday word for 'range'.
   - *Recommended*: «через все» с «через четыре», «через все» с «через пять», «дважды через все», то есть октава с квартой, октава с квинтой и две октавы / ‘through all’ with ‘through four’, ‘through all’ with ‘through five’, ‘twice through all’, that is, the octave with a fourth, the octave with a fifth, and two octaves
   - *Evidence*: about.md § 4.1 and § 4.2 [checked]; C's note at X28 (group 1) stating why the Greek names are transliterated in X28–X30 and X35 [checked]; B's renderings at X28 («четвёртой», «пятой», «восьмая») and X35 («кварты, или «четвёртой»») [checked]

**Errors**

- **C** (ru; language; minor; second pass: modified): «называется диапазоном … диапазон с диатессароном, диапазон с диапенте, дисдиапазон / it is called diapason … diapason with diatessaron, diapason with diapente, disdiapason». C states its reason for transliterating the Greek names in its X28 note, so the departure from about.md § 4.2 is reasoned and not a fault in itself (the first pass said no reason was given). But Russian «диапазон» is the everyday word for 'range', so a reader who has not seen the X28 note reads 'is called a range, because it passes through eight strings'. (English 'diapason' keeps its historical sense of the octave and does not mislead in the same way.) *Fix:* mark the Greek name as a name: the literal rendering beside the established term (октавой [«через все»] … «через все» с «через четыре» …, as A), or the transliteration in quotation marks with a note
- **B** (both; consistency; minor; second pass: modified): «октава с квартой, октава с квинтой и двойная октава, то есть октава вместе с квартой, октава вместе с квинтой и две октавы». Rendering both name-sets by the established terms follows about.md § 4.2, but the 'hoc est' gloss becomes a tautology in the running text; and it is inconsistent with B's own solution for the same device in X28 (Latin ordinals rendered literally: «четвёртой», «пятой», «восьмая») and X35 («кварты, или «четвёртой»»). The first pass listed this as a language fault; the point is rather the inconsistency, since following the convention is not itself an error. *Fix:* keep the gloss visible as in B's X28 and X35 (Xylander's Latin names in quotation marks, avoiding the fractional «две восьмых»), or render the Greek names literally beside the established terms (A)
- **B** (en; construal; minor; second pass: confirmed): «For all the intervals are comprised in the octachord and from there are repeated. The octave is called “through all” because it passes through eight strings.». The quia-clause is made an independent sentence, so the causal link between 'all intervals are completed in the octachord' and the name 'through all' is loosened (the Russian keeps it: 'Поскольку …'). *Fix:* For since all the intervals are completed in the octachord and from there are repeated, it is called ‘through all’, because it passes through eight strings.
- **D** (both; language; minor; second pass: confirmed): ««две восьмых» / ‘two eighths’». As a literal rendering of 'duae octavae' this reads as the fraction 2/8 in both languages. *Fix:* «две октавы» (with the literal sense in a note) / ‘two octaves’

**Best base.** Russian: A (Keeps the causal structure and makes Xylander's Greek-to-Latin gloss visible without tautology; D close second.) English: A (Same reasons; D is smooth but its literal 'two eighths' misleads.)

**Notes to keep**

- A: Xylander renders the Greek names by the ordinary Latin ones (octava cum quarta …); why the Greek names are given literally; 'through all' = through all eight; marginal 'Vide Annotationes' at 44.17–18 (also in C).

**For the editor**

- How to render Xylander's double naming (Greek names glossed by 'octava cum quarta' etc.): literal Greek names plus established terms (A), or established terms plus literal Latin ordinals (D)? The same choice recurs in X42 ('decimamquintam hodie vocant').


### X31 (Xyl. 44.21–45.2)

> Consonant autem diatessaron dictum intervallum, et diapente, secundum id genus consonantiae quod Paraphonum Graeci dicunt, quasi iuxta sonans. Diapason autem, diapason cum diatessaron, diapason cum diapente, et disdiapason, secundum id genus consonantiae quod Antiphonum appellant, quasi contra sonans.

**Sense.** The interval called diatessaron (the fourth) and the diapente (the fifth) are consonant according to the kind of consonance the Greeks call 'paraphonon', as if 'sounding beside'. The octave, the octave and a fourth, the octave and a fifth and the double octave are consonant according to the kind they call 'antiphonon', as if 'sounding against'.

**Divergences**

1. *diatessaron dictum intervallum*: dictum: 'called (diatessaron)' or 'the aforesaid'; and does it cover the fifth too?
   - **A**: 'the interval called the fourth', referring to the fourth only
     - A: «интервал, называемый квартой, и квинта» / “the interval called the fourth, and the fifth”
   - **B, D**: 'called', extended to both intervals (B's Russian drops the phrase altogether)
     - B: «Кварта и квинта» / “The intervals called the fourth and the fifth”
     - D: «Интервалы, именуемые квартой и квинтой» / “The intervals called the fourth and the fifth”
   - **C**: 'the aforesaid interval of the fourth' (after dicta intervalla, X29)
     - C: «названный интервал кварты и квинта» / “The aforesaid interval of the fourth, and the fifth”
   - *Verdict* (medium confidence; second pass: confirmed): With the name standing before the participle ('diatessaron dictum intervallum'), 'the interval called diatessaron' is the natural construal, parallel to 'quod Paraphonum Graeci dicunt' in the same sentence; 'aforesaid' is possible but would more naturally be 'dictum diatessaron intervallum'. Dictum is singular and belongs to the fourth only; extending it to the fifth (B en, D) is harmless but not the Latin. C's Russian 'названный' covers both senses; its English commits to 'aforesaid'.
   - *Recommended*: Консонируют же интервал, называемый квартой, и квинта / The interval called the fourth, and the fifth, are consonant
   - *Evidence*: Lewis & Short s.v. dico II (dictus, 'called', 'aforesaid') [from memory]; X29 'per media omnia dicta intervalla' [checked]
2. *quod Paraphonum Graeci dicunt … quod Antiphonum appellant*: Form of the term: neuter adjective used as a noun ('paraphonon', «парафонный») or abstract noun ('paraphony', «парафония»)
   - **A, C**: adjectival term, matching the participial glosses quasi iuxta / contra sonans
     - A: «который греки называют парафонным, как бы «звучащим рядом»» / “which the Greeks call paraphonon, as it were ‘sounding beside’”
     - C: «который греки называют парафонным, как бы «звучащим рядом»» / “which the Greeks call paraphonon, as it were ‘sounding beside’”
   - **B, D**: abstract noun, with the gloss turned into a verbal noun
     - B: «который греки называют парафонией, то есть как бы звучанием рядом» / “the Greeks call paraphony, as if sounding alongside”
     - D: «который греки называют парафонией, то есть как бы «звучанием рядом»» / “the Greeks call paraphony, meaning something like ‘sounding alongside’”
   - *Verdict* (medium confidence; to the editor; second pass: confirmed): Both convey the term. The adjectival form keeps the Latin word class, fits the participial glosses (iuxta sonans, contra sonans) and the participles assonans / obsonans and the neuter 'illud … consonat' of X32; C's note also points out that Russian «антифон» is a liturgical genre, a reason to prefer «антифонный». The noun forms are legitimate but must then be carried through X32 consistently. Editor's choice for the concordance.
   - *Recommended*: который греки называют парафонным, как бы «звучащим рядом» … антифонным, как бы «звучащим против» / which the Greeks call paraphonon, as it were ‘sounding beside’ … antiphonon, as it were ‘sounding against’
   - *Evidence*: LSJ s.vv. παράφωνος, ἀντίφωνος [from memory]; X32 'illud quidem consonat' (neuter) [checked]
3. *quasi contra sonans*: contra: 'against, opposite' or 'in answer'
   - **A, C, D**: 'against' / 'opposite'
     - A: «как бы «звучащим против»» / “as it were ‘sounding against’”
     - C: «как бы «звучащим напротив»» / “as it were ‘sounding opposite’”
     - D: «как бы «звучанием напротив»» / “something like ‘sounding opposite’”
   - **B**: 'in answer' / 'навстречу'
     - B: «как бы звучанием навстречу» / “as if sounding in answer”
   - *Verdict* (medium confidence; second pass: confirmed): The gloss calques the prefix ἀντί-, so 'against / opposite' is the expected rendering. B's 'in answer' is lexically possible (L&S contra, adv. I.A.3, reciprocal 'in return, back, counter-') and anticipates 'respondeant' in X32, but it is less literal; the Russian 'навстречу' is weaker still. Not an error; A–C–D preferable.
   - *Recommended*: как бы «звучащим против» / as it were ‘sounding against’
   - *Evidence*: Lewis & Short s.v. contra, adv. I.A, I.A.3, I.B [checked]

**Errors**

- **B** (ru; omission; minor; second pass: confirmed): «Кварта и квинта консонируют». 'dictum intervallum' is left out in the Russian (the English has 'The intervals called …'); the two versions of B also disagree. *Fix:* Консонируют же интервал, называемый квартой, и квинта

**Text proposals and doubts**

- **C**, doubt: printed «Consonant autem diatessaron dictum inter-|uallum (44.21–22)»; proposal: Консонируют же интервал, называемый квартой, и квинта… / The interval called the fourth and the fifth are consonant…. *Verdict: sound*; second pass: confirmed. The alternative is in fact the more natural construal (see divergence); C's chosen Russian «названный» is ambiguous enough to stand, but its English 'aforesaid' should yield to 'called'. *Evidence*: Lewis & Short s.v. dico II [from memory]

**Best base.** Russian: A (Exact construal of dictum, adjectival terms matching the glosses, nothing omitted; C equally exact except for 'названный интервал кварты'.) English: A (Construal of dictum and bracketed '[are consonant]' exact; C's 'aforesaid' and B/D's extension of 'called' are weaker.)

**Notes to keep**

- C: Two different Greek uses: Thrasyllus in Theon (antiphonic = octave, double octave; paraphonic = fifth, fourth) vs. Gaudentius (paraphonic = intermediate between consonant and dissonant, e.g. ditone, tritone); Xylander's list adds the octave and a fourth and the octave and a fifth to the antiphonic. References to be re-verified: the Theon page (Hiller pp. 48–49) and Gaudentius are not checked here.
- C: Why the Russian uses the adjectives «парафонный», «антифонный» (Russian «антифон» is a liturgical genre).
- D: Plato, Laws 812d–e uses ἀντίφωνον of high against low.

**For the editor**

- Term form for Paraphonum / Antiphonum (here and X32): adjective-term ('paraphonon', «парафонный») or abstract noun ('paraphony', «парафония»)?


### X32 (Xyl. 45.2–13)

> Inter paraphonum et antiphonum, hoc est assonans et obsonans, hoc differt, quod illud quidem consonat inaequali tempore, quando soni mutuas suas proportionalitates atque rationes aequalitate quadam leniter et concinne sibi invicem succedunt. Antiphonum autem est, cum simul eodem tempore acutum cum gravi consonat: ut cum octavus primo, undecimus quarto, duodecimus quinto, decimusquintus octavo aequaliter ascendit vel descendit in intensionibus remissionibusve, ita ut acutis graves, vel contra, respondeant proportionaliter.

**Sense.** Paraphonon and antiphonon, that is 'assonans' and 'obsonans', differ in this: the former is consonant at unequal (i.e. different) times, when the notes, in respect of their mutual proportionalities and ratios, succeed one another gently and harmoniously with a certain evenness. Antiphonon is when high and low are consonant together at the same moment: as when the 8th note with the 1st, the 11th with the 4th, the 12th with the 5th, the 15th with the 8th rises or falls equally in raisings or lowerings, so that low notes answer to high, or vice versa, proportionally. (All four example pairs are octaves.)

**Divergences**

1. *hoc est assonans et obsonans*: How to render Xylander's second, Latin pair of glosses on paraphonon / antiphonon
   - **A, C**: prefix calques: ad- for παρα- ('sounding to / beside'), ob- for ἀντι- ('sounding against')
     - A: «то есть «звучащим при» и «звучащим навстречу»» / “that is, the ‘sounding to’ and the ‘sounding over against’”
     - C: «то есть «призвучным» и «противозвучным»» / “that is, the ‘sounding-to’ and the ‘sounding-against’”
   - **D**: assonans in its classical sense 'answering with sound', obsonans as 'sounding opposite'
     - D: «то есть «звучание в ответ» и «встречное звучание»» / “that is, ‘sounding in response’ and ‘sounding opposite’”
   - **B**: interpretive: 'in accompaniment' and 'in answer'
     - B: «то есть созвучие вторящее и ответное» / “that is, sounding in accompaniment and sounding in answer”
   - *Verdict* (medium confidence; to the editor; second pass: modified): The pair repeats in Latin the etymological glosses of X31 (iuxta sonans, contra sonans), so the prefixes carry the meaning: ad- for παρα-, ob- for ἀντι-. A and C render it so, and 'to sound to' is in fact the first gloss of assono in Lewis & Short. D follows the other attested sense of assono ('respond to', of Echo): lexically sound, but the paraphonic class then receives the notion of 'response' that the same segment attaches to antiphony ('acutis graves … respondeant'), and the contrast is weakened. B's Russian pair «вторящее / ответное» ('echoing / answering') is nearly synonymous, so the contrast vanishes; its English 'in accompaniment' is not a sense of assono, and 'in answer' is not a sense of obsono ('to interrupt by a sound'). A's Russian «звучащим при» is unidiomatic; C's calques are clear but are coinages and need the quotation marks and a note (about.md § 4.1).
   - *Recommended*: то есть «призвучное» и «противозвучное» (with a note on assono / obsono) / that is, the ‘sounding-to’ and the ‘sounding-against’
   - *Evidence*: Lewis & Short s.v. assono (adsono): 'to sound to, respond to (rare)', Ov. Met. 3.507, Pers. 1.102 [checked]; Gaffiot s.v. adsono: 'répondre par un son' [checked]; Lewis & Short s.v. ob-sŏno: 'to interrupt by a sound', Plaut. Ps. 205; Gaffiot s.v. 1 obsono: 'interrompre par un bruit' [checked]; X31 quasi iuxta sonans / quasi contra sonans [checked]
2. *quando soni mutuas suas proportionalitates atque rationes aequalitate quadam leniter et concinne sibi invicem succedunt*: The accusative 'mutuas suas proportionalitates atque rationes' has no governing word: supplied participle, accusative of respect with the verb, or respect attached to aequalitate?
   - **A**: a participle supplied in brackets: '[keeping] their mutual proportionalities and ratios'
     - A: «когда звуки, [храня] взаимные свои пропорциональности и отношения, с некоторой равномерностью мягко и складно сменяют друг друга» / “when the notes, [keeping] their mutual proportionalities and ratios, succeed one another gently and neatly with a certain evenness”
   - **C**: accusative of respect with succedunt: 'in their mutual proportionalities and ratios'
     - C: «когда звуки по своим взаимным пропорциональностям и отношениям с некоторой равномерностью мягко и слаженно сменяют друг друга» / “when the notes, in their mutual proportionalities and ratios, succeed one another gently and harmoniously with a certain evenness”
   - **B, D**: respect attached to the evenness: 'with a certain evenness in their mutual proportions and ratios'
     - B: «когда они плавно и стройно следуют друг за другом, соблюдая некоторую равномерность во взаимных пропорциях и отношениях» / “succeeding one another smoothly and fittingly with a certain evenness in their mutual proportions and ratios”
     - D: «соблюдая известную равномерность в своих взаимных пропорциях и отношениях, они плавно и стройно следуют друг за другом» / “succeeding one another smoothly and harmoniously with a certain evenness in their mutual proportions and ratios”
   - *Verdict* (medium confidence; to the editor; second pass: confirmed): The printed text is sound (page image) and needs no supplement: a Grecizing accusative of respect is the simplest account, and it may go with the whole clause (C) or be felt with aequalitate (B, D); the sense is the same in all three, namely that the successive notes stand in their mutual ratios and follow one another evenly. C stays closest to the Latin; A's bracketed '[keeping]' is honest but adds a verb the Latin lacks; B and D are acceptable. Note that in the Boethian vocabulary proportionalitas is the 'proportion' (ἀναλογία) and ratio / proportio the 'ratio', so B's and D's 'proportions … ratios' is a defensible rendering of the pair; A's and C's 'proportionalities' is a literal calque.
   - *Recommended*: когда звуки по своим взаимным пропорциональностям и отношениям с некоторой равномерностью мягко и слаженно сменяют друг друга / when the notes, in respect of their mutual proportionalities and ratios, succeed one another gently and harmoniously with a certain evenness
   - *Evidence*: Allen and Greenough §397b (synecdochical or Greek accusative, 'in poetry and later Latin'; also called accusative of specification), DCC edition [checked]; Boethius, De institutione arithmetica II.40 (proportionalitas = ἀναλογία, as distinct from proportio) [from memory]; page image xylander-p45, lines 5–7: the reading is as printed, no trace of a lost word [checked]

**Errors**

- **B** (en; term; minor; second pass: confirmed): «rise or fall by equal changes in pitch». 'in intensionibus remissionibusve' is a pair of pitch and should be 'in raisings or lowerings' (about.md § 4.5); 'changes in pitch' collapses the pair (the Russian keeps «повышении или понижении»). *Fix:* rise or fall equally in raisings or lowerings
- **B** (both; mistranslation; minor; second pass: modified): «созвучие вторящее и ответное / sounding in accompaniment and sounding in answer». Xylander's two glosses repeat the prefix contrast of X31 (ad- 'to, beside' / ob- 'against'). B's Russian «вторящее» ('echoing') is close to the attested sense of assono ('respond to'), but paired with «ответное» ('answering') the two words are near-synonyms and the contrast disappears; the English 'in accompaniment' is not a sense of assono, and 'in answer' is not a sense of obsono ('to interrupt by a sound'); 'in answer' also anticipates the 'respondeant' that the text reserves for antiphony. *Fix:* «призвучное» и «противозвучное» / the ‘sounding-to’ and the ‘sounding-against’
- **D** (ru; construal; minor; second pass: confirmed): «и низкие звуки соразмерно отвечают высоким или наоборот». The result clause 'ita ut … respondeant' becomes a coordinate clause ('и'); the English keeps 'so that'. *Fix:* так что низкие звуки соразмерно отвечают высоким или наоборот
- **A** (ru; language; minor; second pass: confirmed): ««звучащим при»». Unidiomatic Russian for the calque of assonans; the reader cannot parse 'звучащее при'. *Fix:* «призвучным» (with a note) or «звучащим рядом»

**Text proposals and doubts**

- **A**, text issue: printed «quando soni mutuas suas proportionali|tates atque rationes æqualitate quadam leniter et | concinnè sibi inuicem succedunt (45.5–7)»; proposal: No change; possibly a participle (servantes, retinentes) has dropped out after rationes. *Verdict: open*; second pass: confirmed. Nothing on the page suggests a loss, and a Grecizing accusative of respect explains the construction without change; A rightly translates the printed text and marks the supplement in brackets. The lacuna hypothesis is unnecessary but cannot be excluded. *Evidence*: Allen and Greenough §397b [from memory]
- **A**, doubt: printed «mutuas suas proportionalitates atque rationes … succedunt»; proposal: когда звуки в отношении взаимных своих пропорциональностей и отношений с некоторой ровностью мягко и слаженно сменяют друг друга / when the notes, in respect of their mutual proportionalities and ratios, succeed one another gently and harmoniously with a certain evenness. *Verdict: sound*; second pass: confirmed. The accusative of respect needs no supplement and is preferable to the bracketed participle; the sense is the same. *Evidence*: Allen and Greenough §397b [from memory]
- **A**, doubt: printed «aequalitate quadam … aequaliter»; proposal: с неким равенством … равно восходит или нисходит / with a certain equality … rises or falls equally. *Verdict: open*; second pass: confirmed. Both 'evenness' and 'equality' are within aequalitas; 'evenness' suits the smooth succession, 'equally' suits the parallel motion. A legitimate alternative, not a change of doctrine. *Evidence*: Lewis & Short s.v. aequalitas [from memory]
- **D**, doubt: printed «mutuas suas proportionalitates atque rationes … sibi invicem succedunt»; proposal: звуки плавно и стройно сменяют друг друга, [следуя] своим взаимным пропорциям и отношениям с известной равномерностью / the notes succeed one another smoothly and harmoniously, [following] their mutual proportions and ratios with a certain evenness. *Verdict: open*; second pass: confirmed. Like A's '[keeping]', a supplied participle; possible, but the accusative of respect makes it unnecessary. *Evidence*: Allen and Greenough §397b [from memory]
- **D**, doubt: printed «illud quidem consonat inæquali | tempore»; proposal: через неодинаковые промежутки времени / at unequal intervals of time. *Verdict: unsound*; second pass: modified. The antithesis with 'simul eodem tempore' in the next sentence, and 'sibi invicem succedunt' in this one, fix the sense 'not at the same time, in succession'. D's alternative brings in rhythmic irregularity (unequal intervals between the successive notes), which Xylander's Latin does not express. (Vinet's version speaks of 'impari temporis spatio', but Xylander is to be translated as he stands.) *Evidence*: X32, 'Antiphonum autem est, cum simul eodem tempore …' [checked]; Vinet V37 'impari temporis spatio … aequo … temporis spacio' (latin-texts.json) [checked]
- **B**, doubt: printed «illud quidem consonat inæquali | tempore»; proposal: При парафонии звуки консонируют в течение неодинаковых промежутков времени. / In paraphony the notes are consonant for unequal spans of time.. *Verdict: unsound*; second pass: confirmed. Same reason: the contrast is with simultaneity (simul eodem tempore). *Evidence*: X32 [checked]
- **C**, doubt: printed «illud quidem consonat inæquali | tempore»; proposal: …первое консонирует в неравное по длительности время… / …the former is consonant in an unequal span of time…. *Verdict: unsound*; second pass: confirmed. Same reason; C itself rightly rejects it. *Evidence*: X32 [checked]

**Best base.** Russian: C (Closest to the Latin construction (accusative of respect), clear calques for assonans / obsonans, neuter 'высокое … с низким' kept, conventions followed.) English: C (Same; A equally exact but 'at unequal time' and the bracketed participle make it less clean.)

**Notes to keep**

- A: assonans / obsonans as fresh Latin glosses (classical senses of assono, obsono); succession vs. simultaneity; the three aequ- words; all four examples are octaves, while 11, 12, 15 are the note-counts of the octave and a fourth, the octave and a fifth, the double octave, which the text does not pair with the first note (with A's checks).
- C: Checks naming the notes of each pair on the fifteen-note list (8 mese : 1 proslambanomenos; 11 diatonos diezeugmene : 4 hypate diatonos; 12 nete diezeugmene : 5 hypate meson; 15 nete hyperbolaion : 8 mese).

**For the editor**

- Rendering of assonans / obsonans: prefix calques in quotation marks («призвучное» / «противозвучное»; 'sounding-to' / 'sounding-against') or descriptive participles? Calques are coinages (about.md § 4.1) but render Xylander's own coinage-like use.
- Accusative 'mutuas suas proportionalitates atque rationes': accusative of respect (no supplement) or a bracketed participle as A? And 'proportionalitates': literal 'proportionalities' or Boethian 'proportions'?
- Should a note say that the examples of antiphony are all octaves, although X31 lists four antiphonic intervals?


### X33 (Xyl. 45.13–16)

> Caeterum tonus, semitonium, atque diesis per se considerata, initium quidem seu principium sunt consonantiae, non tamen consonantia.

**Sense.** Moreover, the tone, the semitone and the diesis, considered in themselves, are indeed the beginning or principle of consonance, yet not a consonance.

**Divergences**

1. *non tamen consonantia*: 'yet not consonance' (non tamen) or 'not yet consonance' (as if nondum)
   - **A, C**: adversative 'but / yet not'
     - A: «но не консонанс» / “yet not a consonance”
     - C: «но не консонанс» / “yet not consonance”
   - **B, D**: Russian adds the temporal 'ещё' ('not yet'); the English is adversative
     - B: «но консонанса ещё не образуют» / “though they do not constitute consonance”
     - D: «но ещё не консонанс» / “though they are not themselves consonance”
   - *Verdict* (high confidence; second pass: confirmed): Xylander writes 'non tamen', 'yet not'. 'Not yet' (οὔπω, Vinet's nondum, as C's note points out) is the wording of the parallel tradition, not of this Latin; B's and D's Russian import it. A and C are right; the English of B and D is right.
   - *Recommended*: но не консонанс / yet not a consonance
   - *Evidence*: Lewis & Short s.v. tamen [from memory]; Vinet V38 'principium quidem consonantiae sunt, sed nondum tamen consonantia' (latin-texts.json) [checked]; page image xylander-p45, lines 15–16 ('nō tamen consonantia') [checked]

**Errors**

- **B** (ru; mistranslation; minor; second pass: confirmed): «но консонанса ещё не образуют». 'ещё не' renders nondum, not Xylander's 'non tamen'. *Fix:* но консонанса не образуют
- **D** (ru; mistranslation; minor; second pass: confirmed): «но ещё не консонанс». Same: 'not yet' instead of 'yet not'. *Fix:* но не консонанс
- **D** (both; omission; minor; second pass: confirmed): «Тон, полутон и диеса … / The tone, the semitone and the diesis …». The transition 'Caeterum' is left out in both languages (B leaves it out in English only). *Fix:* Впрочем, тон … / Moreover (For the rest), the tone …
- **B** (en; omission; minor; second pass: added): «The tone, semitone, and diesis, considered in themselves, are the start or beginning of consonance». 'Caeterum', the transition to the elements of consonance, is left out in the English (the Russian keeps it as «А»). Counted for consistency with D's omission of the same word, which the first pass listed while noting B's only in passing. *Fix:* For the rest (Moreover), the tone, the semitone and the diesis …

**Best base.** Russian: A = C (Identical, exact Russian, with 'Caeterum' and 'non tamen' rendered.) English: A (Renders quidem ('indeed') and caeterum; C equally exact otherwise.)

**Notes to keep**

- C: Parallel in Thrasyllus (Theon): tone and diesis are a beginning of consonance but 'not yet' consonance; Vinet nondum vs. Xylander non tamen (Theon page to be verified).
- A: In the usual doctrine the tone is not a consonance but the difference of fifth and fourth (Boethius I.16–18).


### X34 (Xyl. 45.16–24)

> Conficitur autem diatessaron duobus ex tonis, unoque, semitonio, et praeterea quopiam exiguo: quod quia minus diesi est, appellationem peculiarem non invenit. Diapente vero ex tribus tonis, semitonioque constat. Diapason autem ex sex tonis conficitur. Diapason autem cum diatessaron, ex octo tonis et semitonio conflatur. Diapason cum diapente, ex novem tonis et semitonio. Disdiapason, ex duodecim tonis.

**Sense.** The fourth is made up of two tones, one semitone and, besides, some small amount which, being less than a diesis, has found no name of its own. The fifth consists of three tones and a semitone; the octave of six tones; the octave and a fourth of eight tones and a semitone; the octave and a fifth of nine tones and a semitone; the double octave of twelve tones.

**Errors**

- **B** (ru; language; minor; second pass: confirmed): «Кварта образуется из двух тонов, одного полутона и ещё какой-то малой добавки. Она меньше диесы, и потому особого названия для неё не нашлось.». After the sentence break, 'Она' can be read as referring to the subject 'Кварта' (the fourth smaller than a diesis); the Latin relative quod refers unambiguously to quopiam exiguo. *Fix:* …и ещё какой-то малой добавки, которая меньше диесы и потому не получила особого названия.

**Text proposals and doubts**

- **A**, text issue: printed «duobus ex tonis, unoqúe semitonio, et præterea quo- (45.17)»; proposal: unoque semitonio (no comma), as printed. *Verdict: sound*; second pass: confirmed. The diplomatic line has no comma after unoqúe, and the corrections list does not record one; uno agrees with semitonio. No effect on the sense; the reading text should drop the comma. *Evidence*: printed line 45.17 in group-2.json [checked]; corrections list of group-2.json (no entry for 45.17) [checked]; page image xylander-p45, line 17: 'unoq; ſemitonio', the -que abbreviation and no comma [checked]
- **C**, text issue: printed «duobus ex tonis, unoqúe semitonio, et præterea quo- (45.17)»; proposal: duobus ex tonis, unoque semitonio. *Verdict: sound*; second pass: confirmed. Same point as A's; no effect on the sense. *Evidence*: printed line 45.17 [checked]; page image xylander-p45, line 17: 'unoq; ſemitonio', the -que abbreviation and no comma [checked]

**Best base.** Russian: A = C (Both exact, the relative clause kept unambiguous; D equally exact with a bracketed '[интервала]'.) English: A = D (Exact; A's 'some small thing' is closest to quopiam exiguo, D's bracketed '[interval]' is a fair clarification; C's '[remainder]' slightly suggests subtraction.)

**Notes to keep**

- A: The added 'small thing' contradicts the ratios (by 4:3 the fourth is smaller than 2½ tones; remainder 256:243) and Boethius I.21 'duorum tonorum … ac semitonii, sed non pleni'; X47 gives the fourth as simply two tones and a semitone; translated as the Latin stands (with A's or C's cent checks).
- D: With a 9:8 tone the remainder of the fourth after two tones is 256:243, already less than half a tone, so nothing added to a half-tone gives 4:3.

**For the editor**

- The reading text has a comma after 'unoque' not in the print (45.17) and not listed among the corrections: remove it?


### X35 (Xyl. 45.24–46.3)

> Proportio autem diatessaron seu quartae est sesquitertia, diapente sesquialteram constituit, diapason duplam. Diapason cum diatessaron, duplam superpartientem: diapason cum diapente, triplam.

**Sense.** The ratio (proportio) of the diatessaron, or fourth, is sesquitertian [4:3]; the fifth makes the sesquialter [3:2], the octave the duple [2:1]; the octave and a fourth the duple superpartient [8:3]; the octave and a fifth the triple [3:1].

**Divergences**

1. *Proportio autem diatessaron seu quartae*: How to keep the Greek name glossed by the Latin 'quarta' visible (as in X30)
   - **A**: literal Greek name beside the established term, in both languages
     - A: «Пропорция же интервала «через четыре», или кварты» / “The proportion of the ‘through four’, or fourth”
   - **B, D**: Russian: established term plus Xylander's Latin ordinal rendered literally («четвёртой»); English: D literal Greek name + established term, B a paraphrase ('commonly called')
     - B: «Пропорция кварты, или «четвёртой»» / “The proportion of the interval commonly called the fourth”
     - D: «Отношение кварты, или «четвёртой»» / “The ratio of the interval ‘through four’, or fourth”
   - **C**: transliterated Greek name beside the established term
     - C: «Пропорция же диатессарона, или кварты» / “The proportion of the diatessaron, or fourth”
   - *Verdict* (medium confidence; to the editor; second pass: confirmed): All except B's English keep the double naming. A is consistent across languages and with its X30 solution, and fits the function of the Latin (Greek name explained by the current Latin term). D's Russian and English use opposite strategies. C gives its reason in the note (Greek name with Latin gloss), so its transliteration is a reasoned departure from about.md § 4.2, but a transliterated 'диатессарон' is not a term a modern reader understands; the convention prefers term plus literal rendering. B's English 'commonly called' drops the Greek name and adds words not in the Latin.
   - *Recommended*: Пропорция же интервала «через четыре», или кварты, — сверхтретная [4:3] / The proportion of the ‘through four’, or fourth, is sesquitertian [4:3]
   - *Evidence*: about.md § 4.1–4.2 [checked]; X30 (same device) [checked]

**Errors**

- **B** (en; mistranslation; minor; second pass: confirmed): «The proportion of the interval commonly called the fourth». 'diatessaron seu quartae' becomes 'commonly called the fourth': the Greek name is dropped and 'commonly called' is added without brackets. *Fix:* The proportion of the ‘through four’, or fourth

**Best base.** Russian: A (All ratio names and brackets correct; the double naming rendered readably and consistently with X30.) English: A (Same; D equally correct in the English.)

**Notes to keep**

- A: 8:3 is multiple superpartient, neither multiple nor superparticular, so the Pythagoreans did not count the octave and a fourth as a consonance while Ptolemy did (Boethius II.27, V.9).
- C: Boethius' example of the duple superbipartient, 3 to 8 (De inst. mus. I.4; page to be verified).

**For the editor**

- Proportio: 'proportion' / «пропорция» (A, B, C) or 'ratio' / «отношение» (D)? In Boethian usage proportio is the ratio (λόγος); the choice should be fixed in the concordance.


### X36 (Xyl. 46.3–9)

> Id quo melius intelligatur, exempli causa constituatur in numeris primus terminus senarius: ad eum sesquitertius est octonarius, sesquialter novenarius, duplus duodenarius, duplus superpartiens (nimirum super bipartiens tertias) sedecim. Triplus denique, octodecim: quadruplus, viginti quatuor.

**Sense.** To make this clearer, let the number six be set as the first term of a numerical example: to it eight is sesquitertian [4:3], nine sesquialter [3:2], twelve duple [2:1], sixteen duple superpartient [8:3] (namely [duple] superbipartient thirds); finally eighteen is triple [3:1], twenty-four quadruple [4:1].

**Divergences**

1. *(nimirum super bipartiens tertias)*: Is Xylander's Boethian specification rendered as a ratio name (formed on the pattern of about.md § 4.3) or by a description; and does it take a bracket?
   - **A, C**: ratio name formed on the Boethian pattern ('superbipartient'), no bracket of its own (explained in the note)
     - A: «(а именно сверхдвучастное третей)» / “(that is to say, superbipartient thirds)”
     - C: «(а именно сверхдвучастным по третям)» / “(namely superbipartient by thirds)”
   - **D**: description of the excess, '[duple]' supplied, bracket [8:3]
     - D: «(точнее, [двукратное] с избытком в две трети [8:3])» / “(specifically, [the double] plus two thirds [8:3])”
   - **B**: description of the excess, no bracket
     - B: «(с избытком именно в две трети)» / “(with precisely two thirds in excess)”
   - *Verdict* (medium confidence; to the editor; second pass: confirmed): All four understand the arithmetic rightly (16 = 2 × 6 + 4, i.e. 8:3). But 'superbipartiens tertias' is a Boethian ratio name, and about.md § 4.3 asks that a ratio name not in the table be formed on the same pattern; A and C do so and so keep Xylander's Boethian precision visible; B and D replace the term by a paraphrase. On the bracket: A and C argue, rightly, that a bare [5:3] would mislead and that the parenthesis names the same 8:3; D's '[двукратное] … [8:3]' is a defensible strict application. Recommend the term (A/C) and either no bracket or '[duple] superbipartient thirds [8:3]'; editor's choice.
   - *Recommended*: (а именно [двукратное] сверхдвучастное третей [8:3]) / (that is to say, [duple] superbipartient thirds [8:3])
   - *Evidence*: about.md § 4.3 [checked]; Gaffiot s.v. superbipartiens: 'qui contient un nombre et les deux tiers de ce nombre (5/3 …)', Boeth. Arithm. 1.28 [checked]; DMLBS s.v. superbipartiens: n + 2 : n; b. with duplex, xn + 2 : n ('duplex superbipartiens, ut sunt octo ad tria', Odington) [checked]; Boethius, De institutione arithmetica I.28–31 on superpartient and multiple superpartient ratios [from memory]

**Errors**

- **B** (both; term; minor; second pass: confirmed): «(с избытком именно в две трети) / (with precisely two thirds in excess)». The Boethian ratio name 'super bipartiens tertias' is paraphrased instead of formed on the pattern of about.md § 4.3, with no reason given; the term disappears from the text. *Fix:* (а именно сверхдвучастное третей) / (that is to say, superbipartient thirds), explanation in a note
- **D** (both; term; minor; second pass: confirmed): «(точнее, [двукратное] с избытком в две трети [8:3]) / (specifically, [the double] plus two thirds [8:3])». Same: the ratio name is replaced by a description. *Fix:* (точнее, [двукратное] сверхдвучастное третей [8:3]) / (specifically, [duple] superbipartient thirds [8:3])

**Best base.** Russian: A = C (All numbers and brackets correct, the Boethian term kept and explained in the note; A's phrasing slightly lighter.) English: A = C (Same.)

**Notes to keep**

- C: Super bipartiens tertias = Boethius' superbipartient by thirds: excess over the duple is two thirds of the smaller term (16 = 2 × 6 + 4); alone it would be 5:3, hence one bracket only (A's note makes the same point).
- A: Quadruple [4:1] is the ratio of the double octave, not named in X35; 6, 8, 9, 12 as in Boethius' hammers (I.10).

**For the editor**

- Bracket for 'super bipartiens tertias': none (A, C), or '[duple] … [8:3]' (D)? And the Russian form of the name: «сверхдвучастное третей» (A) or «сверхдвучастное по третям» (C)?


### X37 (Xyl. 46.9–13)

> At enim scire hoc oportet, proportiones intervallorum nequaquam iudicari ad numeros sonorum vel fidium facta collatione, sed ad sonitus ipsius intensionem a gravi ad acutum.

**Sense.** But one must know this: the ratios of the intervals are by no means judged by making a comparison with the numbers of the notes or strings, but by the intensio (tension, rise) of the sound itself from low to high.

**Errors**

- **B** (en; language; minor; second pass: confirmed): «the proportions of intervals are assessed by the rise in pitch of the sound itself from low to high. Comparing the numbers of notes or strings plays no part in that assessment.». The 'nequaquam … sed' antithesis is reordered into two sentences and the first member paraphrased ('plays no part'); the sense survives but the brief asks for no paraphrase (the Russian keeps the order). *Fix:* the proportions of intervals are by no means judged by a comparison with the numbers of notes or strings, but by the rise in pitch of the sound itself from low to high.

**Best base.** Russian: A (Exact, keeps 'hoc' and the antithesis; C equally exact with a bracketed '[сопоставлением]'.) English: A (Exact and clear; D also good.)

**Notes to keep**

- D: The 'numbers of the notes or strings' are their ordinal positions, as the example of the fifth in X38 shows, not numbers expressing pitch.
- A: Intensio here and in X39 is tension in the sense of pitch (L&S s.v. intensio); sonitus = the sounding, cf. X29.
- C: Xylander uses chorda, fides and nervus as synonyms, all rendered 'string'.

**For the editor**

- intensio (alone) here and in X39: 'tension' / «напряжение» (A, C) or 'rise in pitch' / «повышение» (B, D)? about.md § 4.5 leaves it to context; one rendering should be fixed for X37–X39.


### X38 (Xyl. 46.13–21)

> Nam quia intervallum diapente diximus habere sesquialteram proportionem, equidem id non intelligendum est ad excessum quantitatis respiciendo, qua in respectu se praecedentium sonorum habet. Ea namque ratione ad primum esset quincuplum, ad secundum duplum sesquialterum: at tertio collatum, eum, eiusque bessem continet: sesquiquartum vero est ad quartum, nulli autem horum omnium sesquialtera proportione quadrat.

**Sense.** For when we said that the interval of the fifth has the sesquialter ratio, this is certainly not to be understood by looking at the excess of quantity, i.e. how it (the fifth note, the number 5) stands in relation to the notes preceding it. For on that reckoning it would be quintuple [5:1] to the first, duple sesquialter [5:2] to the second; compared with the third, it contains it and its two thirds (5:3); to the fourth it is sesquiquartan [5:4]; with none of all these does it fit in the sesquialter ratio.

**Divergences**

1. *ad excessum quantitatis respiciendo, qua in respectu se praecedentium sonorum habet*: Construal of 'qua … habet': qua as object-substitute (= quam, 'the excess which it has'), as adverb ('in so far as / how') or with 'se habet' ('how it stands')
   - **A**: translated as if 'quam … habet', se going with praecedentium (A's own declared choice, see its text issue)
     - A: «не с оглядкой на то количественное превышение, какое у него есть по отношению к предшествующим ему звукам» / “not … with an eye to the excess of quantity which it has with respect to the notes preceding it”
   - **C**: qua adverbial 'in so far as', habet with an understood object, se with praecedentium
     - C: «глядя на избыток количества — в той мере, в какой он имеет его по отношению к предшествующим ему звукам» / “by looking at the excess of quantity, in so far as it has it in respect of the notes preceding it”
   - **B, D**: free rendering of the clause as 'how much [the fifth note] exceeds the preceding notes', with a bracketed subject
     - B: «речь, конечно, не о том, насколько её [порядковый номер] превосходит номера предшествующих звуков» / “we certainly did not mean this in terms of its numerical excess over the notes that precede it”
     - D: «мы, конечно, не имели в виду, на сколько по количеству [пятый звук] превосходит предшествующие ему звуки» / “we certainly did not mean the numerical excess of [the fifth note] over the notes preceding it”
   - *Verdict* (medium confidence; to the editor; second pass: confirmed): All four give the same sense, which the following arithmetic fixes: the number 5 compared with 1–4. The printed qua (confirmed by A and C on the page) needs no emendation: it can be the adverb 'how, in the way in which' with 'se habet' ('how it stands in relation to the preceding notes'), or C's 'in so far as'. A translates its conjecture quam rather than the reading text (harmless here, but against the brief); C translates the printed text; B and D paraphrase acceptably with bracketed supplements.
   - *Recommended*: глядя на избыток количества — на то, как он соотносится с предшествующими звуками / by looking at the excess of quantity, that is, how it stands in relation to the preceding notes
   - *Evidence*: Lewis & Short s.v. quā, adv., II.B 'as far as, in so far as', II.C 'in what manner, how' [checked]; Lewis & Short s.v. habeo (se habere, 'to be, be situated', with adverbs) [from memory]; page image xylander-p46, line 16: 'qua' with no abbreviation stroke [checked]

**Errors**

- **A** (both; construal; minor; second pass: confirmed): «то количественное превышение, какое у него есть … / the excess of quantity which it has …». Translates the conjecture 'quam … habet' (A's text issue) instead of the reading text 'qua … habet'; the sense is unaffected, but the brief requires the reading text. *Fix:* глядя на количественное превышение — на то, как он соотносится с предшествующими ему звуками / with an eye to the excess of quantity, how it stands with respect to the notes preceding it
- **B** (ru; mistranslation; minor; second pass: confirmed): «Когда мы говорим, что у квинты полуторная [3:2] пропорция». 'diximus' (we said, referring back to X35) becomes present 'говорим'; 'intervallum' is also dropped. The English has 'we said'. *Fix:* Когда мы сказали, что интервал квинты имеет полуторную [3:2] пропорцию

**Text proposals and doubts**

- **A**, text issue: printed «spiciendo, qua in respectu se præcedentium sonorum | habet (46.16–17)»; proposal: perhaps quam … habet. *Verdict: unsound*; second pass: confirmed. qua is construable as printed (adverb 'how' with 'se habet', or 'in so far as'), the page shows no trace of a lost abbreviation stroke (A and C both read qua on the image), and the sense is the same; no emendation is needed. *Evidence*: Lewis & Short s.v. quā, adv., II.B–C [checked]; page image xylander-p46, line 16 [checked]
- **C**, text issue: printed «spiciendo, qua in respectu se præcedentium sonorum (46.16)»; proposal: Keep qua (adverbial, 'in so far as'); quam is possible but not necessary. *Verdict: sound*; second pass: confirmed. Keeps the printed text and gives a workable construal; the 'se habet' construal (C's option c) is equally available. *Evidence*: Lewis & Short s.v. quā, adv., II.B–C [checked]; page image xylander-p46, line 16 [checked]

**Best base.** Russian: C (Translates the printed qua-clause, all ratio names and brackets right ([5:1], [5:2], [5:4], [3:2]), bes rendered exactly.) English: A = C (A reads best ('square in the sesquialter proportion' for quadrat) but renders the conjecture; C follows the printed text.)

**Notes to keep**

- C: The argument: diapente counts five notes, but 3:2 is not the ratio of 5 to any of 1–4; ratios belong to pitch, not to the count of notes.
- A: 'Interval of the fifth' here = the fifth note, the number 5; 5:3 is described ('it and its bes'), not named (it would be superbipartiens tertias, cf. X36), hence no bracket; bes = two thirds (L&S).

**For the editor**

- Construal of 'qua … habet' (46.16): keep qua ('how it stands' / 'in so far as') as printed; A's quam is unnecessary.


### X39 (Xyl. 46.21–23)

> Ergo ad intensionem acuitatis, non ad numerum sonorum respiciendum. eo enim tandem modo sesquialterum erit,

**Sense.** Therefore one must look to the intensio of acuteness (the rise in pitch), not to the number of the notes; for only in this way, in the end, will it (the interval) be sesquialter [3:2], (continued in X40)

**Divergences**

1. *eo enim tandem modo sesquialterum erit*: Subject of the neuter 'sesquialterum erit': the interval (intervallum, neuter) or 'the ratio'
   - **A, C**: the interval (implied, or bracketed) will be sesquialter
     - A: «ибо лишь таким образом он и окажется полуторным [3:2]» / “for only in that way will it be sesquialter [3:2]”
     - C: «ведь таким-то образом [интервал] в конце концов и будет полуторным [3:2]» / “for it is in this way, in the end, that it will be sesquialter [3:2]”
   - **B, D**: 'the ratio' made the subject (unbracketed in B and in D's Russian)
     - B: «только тогда отношение будет полуторным [3:2]» / “Only in that way will the ratio be sesquialter [3:2]”
     - D: «Только тогда и получится полуторное [3:2] отношение» / “Only then will the [ratio] be sesquialter [3:2]”
   - *Verdict* (high confidence; second pass: confirmed): The neuter sesquialterum agrees with 'intervallum diapente' of X38 (proportio would require sesquialtera), so the subject is the interval; A and C are right. B and D's 'ratio' gives the same doctrine but supplies a word without brackets (D brackets it only in English).
   - *Recommended*: ибо лишь таким образом [интервал] и окажется полуторным [3:2] / for only in that way will it be sesquialter [3:2]
   - *Evidence*: X38 'intervallum diapente … habere sesquialteram proportionem' [checked]; Allen and Greenough §286 (agreement of adjectives) [from memory]

**Errors**

- **B** (both; sign; minor; second pass: confirmed): «отношение будет полуторным [3:2] / will the ratio be sesquialter [3:2]». 'отношение' / 'the ratio' is not in the Latin (the subject is the neuter interval) and is added without square brackets. *Fix:* [интервал] будет полуторным [3:2] / it will be sesquialter [3:2]
- **D** (ru; sign; minor; second pass: confirmed): «Только тогда и получится полуторное [3:2] отношение». 'отношение' added without brackets (the English has '[ratio]'); the Latin subject is the interval. *Fix:* Только так [интервал] и окажется полуторным [3:2]

**Best base.** Russian: A (Exact subject, idiomatic 'лишь таким образом' for 'eo tandem modo', acuitas kept as «высоты»; C equally exact.) English: A = C (Both exact; A's 'only' and C's 'in the end' are both defensible for tandem.)

**Notes to keep**

- A: acuitas is a medieval / Neo-Latin word ('sharpness of pitch'), absent from classical Latin (DMLBS, Du Cange); C adds that the sentence runs on into X40.


### X40 (Xyl. 46.23–24)

> quod idem de reliquis existimandum est.

**Sense.** (continuing X39) and the same must be judged of the remaining [intervals].

**Best base.** Russian: A = C (Exact relative continuation ('и то же самое следует полагать / думать об остальных').) English: A = C (Identical and exact; B equally good with a bracketed '[intervals]'.)


### X41 (Xyl. 46.24–47.5)

> Porro hae proportiones intervallorum, aut per differentiam longitudinis, crassitieive nervorum, aut per tensionem, quae fit conversione clavorum, deprehensae sunt, aut a ponderum tractione forte manifestius: in illis vero quae inflantur, vel per cavitatis amplitudinem, vel ab intensione et remissione spiritus.

**Sense.** Further, these ratios of the intervals were found either through the difference in length or thickness of the strings, or through the tension produced by turning the pegs, or, perhaps more plainly, by the pull of weights; in blown instruments, either through the width of the cavity (bore) or by the tensing and slackening of the breath.

**Errors**

- **B** (both; omission; minor; second pass: confirmed): «Эти пропорции интервалов обнаружили … / These proportions of intervals were discovered …». The connective 'Porro' ('further') is left out (also by D). *Fix:* Далее, эти пропорции … / Further, these proportions …
- **B** (both; sign; minor; second pass: confirmed): «В духовых инструментах … / In instruments that are blown …». 'instruments' is supplied (the Latin has only 'in illis … quae inflantur') but not bracketed. *Fix:* В духовых [инструментах] … / In [instruments] that are blown …
- **D** (both; omission; minor; second pass: confirmed): «Эти отношения интервалов обнаружили … / These interval ratios were discovered …». 'Porro' left out. *Fix:* Далее, эти отношения … / Further, these ratios …

**Best base.** Russian: A = C (Complete (Porro, aut … aut … aut), supplied word bracketed, intensio et remissio spiritus rendered with a reasoned note.) English: A = C (Same; C's 'width of the bore' is the most exact for cavitatis amplitudinem.)

**Notes to keep**

- A: The traditional list of Pythagoras' ways of finding the ratios (strings, weights, pipes); intensio et remissio spiritus refers to breath, not pitch, hence not 'raising and lowering'; tensio vs. intensio; clavus = tuning peg is medieval (DMLBS).
- C: Parallel in Boethius I.11 (equal weights on strings, pipe lengths, cups, length and thickness of strings); and the physical note that pitch rises as the square root of tension, so weights 2:1 do not give an octave (4:1 needed) — the claim is traditional (D makes the same point).

**For the editor**

- 'intensione et remissione spiritus': the about.md pair 'raising and lowering' is for pitch; here of breath. Fix one rendering ('tension and slackening' A/C, 'strengthening and weakening' B/D).


### X42 (Xyl. 47.5–17)

> Porro systematum aliud est tetrachordum, quod quatuor componitur nervis, omnem vim intervalli diatessaron complectens: aliud pentachordum, quinque ex nervis constitutum, diapente intervalli omnem potestatem in se continens: aliud octachordum, octo ex nervis constructum, omnem octavae materiem ambiens. Est et quod continentibus sedecim nervis conficitur, omnem disdiapason (decimamquintam hodie vocant) intervalli ambitum comprehendens, eoque ad duplices usus habile, quod et praedicta intervalla, et ex his ipsis composita complectitur.

**Sense.** Further, of the systems one is the tetrachord, composed of four strings and embracing the whole force (vis) of the interval of the fourth; another the pentachord, made of five strings and containing in itself the whole power (potestas) of the interval of the fifth; another the octachord, built of eight strings and encompassing the whole matter (materies) of the octave. There is also one made of sixteen continuous strings, comprising the whole compass (ambitus) of the interval of the double octave (which today they call the fifteenth), and for that reason suited to twofold uses, because it embraces both the aforesaid intervals and those composed of them.

**Divergences**

1. *(decimamquintam hodie vocant)*: Xylander's contemporary name for the double octave: literal 'fifteenth' or the established Russian term «квинтдецима»
   - **A, D**: Russian established term «квинтдецима»; English 'the fifteenth'
     - A: «(ныне её называют квинтдецимой)» / “(today they call it the fifteenth)”
     - D: «(сегодня её называют квинтдецимой)» / “(called the fifteenth today)”
   - **B, C**: Russian literal «пятнадцатая»; English 'the fifteenth'
     - B: «(ныне её называют «пятнадцатой»)» / “(today they call it the “fifteenth”)”
     - C: «(ныне его называют пятнадцатой)» / “(today they call it the fifteenth)”
   - *Verdict* (medium confidence; to the editor; second pass: confirmed): Both are correct in sense. «Квинтдецима» is the established Russian name of the interval of two octaves (as нона, децима … квинтдецима), and so fits about.md § 4.1 and Xylander's point that this is the name current 'today'; English 'the fifteenth' is likewise the established English name. The literal «пятнадцатая» needs the quotation marks (B) to be readable; C's unmarked 'его называют пятнадцатой' is awkward.
   - *Recommended*: (ныне её называют квинтдецимой) / (today they call it the fifteenth)
   - *Evidence*: about.md § 4.1 [checked]; Russian interval nomenclature (нона … квинтдецима) [from memory]

**Errors**

- **D** (both; omission; minor; second pass: confirmed): «Одна из систем — тетрахорд. / One system is the tetrachord». 'Porro' is left out (B leaves it out in the English only). *Fix:* Далее, одна из систем — тетрахорд / Further, one system is the tetrachord
- **D** (both; mistranslation; minor; second pass: confirmed): «всё содержание октавы / the entire content of the octave». 'materiem' is rendered 'content'; the word anticipates the contrast of X43 between systems seen 'in materia' and intervals understood by thought, which 'content' hides. *Fix:* всю материю октавы / the whole matter of the octave
- **B** (en; omission; minor; second pass: added): «One system is the tetrachord». 'Porro' is left out in the English (the Russian has «Далее»); counted for consistency with D's omission of the same word. *Fix:* Further, one system is the tetrachord

**Best base.** Russian: A (Complete, keeps Xylander's four words (сила, мощь, материя, объём) apart, uses the established «квинтдецима», construes 'eo … quod' exactly.) English: A = C (Both complete and literal; C's 'contiguous' and A's 'successive' are both right for continentibus.)

**Notes to keep**

- A: vis, potestas, materies, ambitus: four words for the same thing, kept apart; 'decimamquintam hodie vocant' is Xylander's gloss with the current name; sixteen strings vs. the fifteen notes of X14–15 (two octachords counted separately).
- C: Decima quinta as the name of the double octave in the theory and organ practice of Xylander's time (inclusive count of fifteen notes).

**For the editor**

- Russian for 'decimamquintam': «квинтдецима» (established) or «пятнадцатая» (literal, in quotation marks)?


### X43 (Xyl. 47.17–21)

> Inter systemata proinde, ac in medio eorum concepta intervalla hoc interest, quod systematum omnis constitutio in materia spectatur: cogitatione autem separata materia, intervalla intelliguntur.

**Sense.** Accordingly, between the systems and the intervals contained (conceived) in their midst there is this difference: the whole constitution of systems is viewed in matter, whereas intervals are understood when the matter has been separated off in thought.

**Divergences**

1. *in medio eorum concepta intervalla*: concepta: 'contained, comprised' or 'conceived (in the mind)'
   - **A, B, C**: 'contained / comprised in their midst'
     - A: «интервалами, заключёнными внутри них» / “the intervals contained in their midst”
     - B: «заключённых в них интервалов» / “the intervals comprised within them”
     - C: «интервалами, заключёнными внутри них» / “the intervals comprised in their midst”
   - **D**: 'conceived (by us) within them'
     - D: «интервалами, которые мы мыслим внутри них» / “the intervals conceived within them”
   - *Verdict* (medium confidence; second pass: confirmed): Concipio covers both 'take in, contain' and 'conceive in the mind'. With 'in medio eorum' the local sense 'contained in their midst' is the more natural, and the mental act is expressed separately in the second clause (cogitatione … intelliguntur); D's 'conceived' anticipates that clause and adds 'мы'. Not wrong, but A–C are closer to the construction.
   - *Recommended*: интервалами, заключёнными внутри них / the intervals comprised in their midst
   - *Evidence*: Lewis & Short s.v. concipio I.A (to take in, hold; pass. 'to be collected or held') [checked]; Lewis & Short s.v. concipio, the transferred sense 'to conceive in the mind' [from memory]

**Errors**

- **D** (both; omission; minor; second pass: confirmed): «Различие между системами и интервалами … / Systems differ from the intervals …». 'proinde' ('accordingly'), which ties the distinction to the preceding list of systems, is left out. *Fix:* Итак, различие … / Systems, then, differ …

**Text proposals and doubts**

- **C**, doubt: printed «ac in me-|dio eorum concepta interualla (47.17–18)»; proposal: …между системами и интервалами, мыслимыми внутри них… / …between systems and the intervals conceived within them…. *Verdict: open*; second pass: confirmed. Lexically possible (D chose it); C's own choice 'comprised' is preferable with in medio eorum. *Evidence*: Lewis & Short s.v. concipio [from memory]

**Best base.** Russian: C (Exact and idiomatic; A equally exact but repeats 'мыслятся … мыслью'.) English: A = C (Both exact.)

**Notes to keep**

- A: A system is material (the strings or notes of an instrument); an interval is a relation thought of in abstraction from them; cf. X09, X19 (D: materia points back to the strings just listed).


### X44 (Xyl. 47.21–48.1)

> Harmoniae proprium est, ut ea systemata construat atque concinnet ad horum melodiae (eam cantus seu cantilenae modulationem fere dixeris) generum aliquod, quae sunt diatonon, chromaticum, et harmonicum, cui ab ipsa Harmonia nomen obtigit.

**Sense.** It is proper to harmony to construct and fit together those systems according to some one of these genera of melody (which you might almost call the modulatio of song or cantilena): the diatonon, the chromatic and the harmonic, which last received its name from Harmony itself.

**Errors**

- **B** (both; omission; minor; second pass: confirmed): «сообразно одному из родов мелодии / according to one of the genera of melody». 'horum' ('these', pointing forward to the list of genera) is left out. *Fix:* сообразно одному из этих родов мелодии / according to one of these genera of melody

**Best base.** Russian: A = C (Both complete and exact (horum, fere, the gloss on melodia, the name of the harmonic genus).) English: A (Exact, and 'measured shaping' avoids the modern sense of 'modulation' that C's English needs a note to exclude.)

**Notes to keep**

- A: The parenthesis is Xylander's gloss on the Greek word melodia; modulatio = measured, regulated singing (L&S, Quintilian); diatonon in Greek form, the other two in Latin form; 'harmonic' = the enharmonic genus, name kept by convention.
- C: Diatonon (τὸ διάτονον) only here; Xylander writes diatonicum in X45, X48, X55; Boethius I.21 calls the third genus enarmonium.

**For the editor**

- modulatio (here and later): 'measured shaping' / «мерное ведение» (A), 'melodic movement' / «мелодическое движение» (B, C ru), 'modulation' (C en), «мелодический строй» (D)? To be fixed in the concordance with cantus, cantilena, melodia.


### X45 (Xyl. 48.1–7)

> Nam siquidem harmonia ipsa seu consonantiarum coaptatio potissimum per tonos progrediatur, eo modo diatonicum conficitur genus melodiae: sin per semitonia, chromaticum, quasi coloratum dicas: sin ipsa harmonia diesibus maiore sui parte absolvatur, harmonicus eo modo cantus conflatur.

**Sense.** For if harmony itself, or the fitting together (coaptatio) of consonances, proceeds chiefly by tones, the diatonic genus of melody is produced in that way; if by semitones, the chromatic, as if you said 'coloured'; but if harmony itself is completed for the greater part by dieses, harmonic song (cantus) is formed in that way.

**Errors**

- **D** (both; omission; minor; second pass: confirmed): «Если сама гармония … / If harmony itself …». 'Nam', which makes this sentence the explanation of the three genera just named in X44, is left out (D also drops 'ipsa' in the last clause). *Fix:* Ибо если сама гармония … / For if harmony itself …

**Best base.** Russian: A = C (Both complete; keep 'Nam', 'ipsa', 'eo modo' and the gloss on chromaticum.) English: A = C (Both exact.)

**Notes to keep**

- A: coaptatio is the word Augustine coined for ἁρμονία (L&S s.v. coaptatio); quasi coloratum = etymological gloss on chromaticum, cf. Boethius I.21 'Chroma autem, quod dicitur color'.
- B: 'Chiefly' = two of the three steps of the tetrachord are of the named kind in each genus (T T S; S S trihemitone; D D ditone), as the following examples show.


### X46 (Xyl. 48.7–10)

> Neque tamen aut solis ex tonis universa harmonia componitur, aut ex solis semitoniis, solisve diesibus: id enim fieri nequit.

**Sense.** Yet the whole harmony is not composed of tones alone, or of semitones alone, or of dieses alone: for that cannot be.

**Errors**

- **D** (ru; language; minor; second pass: confirmed): «При этом гармония в целом не складывается …». 'При этом' ('at the same time') does not render the adversative 'Neque tamen' ('yet … not'); the English has 'Yet'. *Fix:* Однако гармония в целом не складывается …

**Best base.** Russian: A = C (Exact, with 'tamen' and 'enim' rendered; B equally good but drops 'enim'.) English: A = C (Exact; B's 'cannot be composed' modalises the indicative 'componitur'.)


### X47 (Xyl. 48.10–14)

> Sed quoniam quidem tribus ex intervallis constat diatessaron, comprehensum videlicet duobus tonis et semitonio: in harmoniam coaptatis duobus tonis, opus est reliquum semitonio perfici.

**Sense.** But since the fourth consists of three intervals, being comprised, namely, by two tones and a semitone: when two tones have been fitted into the harmony, the remainder must be completed by a semitone; (continued in X48)

**Errors**

- **D** (both; omission; minor; second pass: confirmed): «Кварта ведь состоит … / Since the fourth consists …». 'Sed', which opposes this to X46 ('not of tones alone … but since …'), is left out. *Fix:* Но так как кварта состоит … / But since the fourth consists …

**Best base.** Russian: A = C (Both exact; A also keeps the run-on into X48 with its semicolon, as the lower-case 'hocque' requires.) English: A = C (Both exact.)

**Notes to keep**

- C: Here the fourth is exactly two tones and a semitone, without the 'small remainder' of X34 (A, B, D note the same); comprehensum (neuter) agrees with diatessaron.
- D: With 4:3 and 9:8 the actual remainder is 256/243.


### X48 (Xyl. 48.14–16)

> hocque genus diatessaron, diatonicum vocatur: quanquam ad eius constitutionem, praeter duos tonos, semitonium quoque accesserit.

**Sense.** (continuing X47) and this genus of the fourth is called diatonic, although a semitone too has entered into its constitution besides the two tones.

**Best base.** Russian: A = C (Both exact, keep 'hocque' as the continuation of X47 and the perfect 'accesserit'; D equally clear.) English: A = C (Both exact; D's present 'enters' is an acceptable idiom.)

**Notes to keep**

- C: The concession is explained by the name ('proceeding through tones'): Boethius I.21 'vocatur diatonum, quasi quod per tonum ac per tonum progrediatur' (A makes the same point by cross-reference to X45–46).


### X49 (Xyl. 48.16–18)

> Iam si duo sint semitonia, quod superest, ex coalitu utique conflatur intervallum, trisemitonium incompositum:

**Sense.** Now if there are two semitones, what remains is assuredly formed by coalescence into one interval, the incomposite trihemitone; (continued in X50)

**Errors**

- **B** (both; omission; minor; second pass: confirmed): «то из остатка путём слияния образуется интервал / the remainder coalesces into an interval». 'utique' ('assuredly, necessarily'), which marks the result as forced by the size of the fourth, is left out. *Fix:* то остаток непременно путём слияния образует интервал / the remainder necessarily coalesces into an interval

**Best base.** Russian: A = C (Complete (Iam, utique, ex coalitu) and exact.) English: A = C (Same.)

**Notes to keep**

- A: coalitus is a late word (Arnobius, L&S); its sense here answers to 'coalescentibus' in X27: three semitones fused into one incomposite interval (C says the same).
- D: Why 'incomposite trihemitone': three semitones in size, taken as one step in melody.


### X50 (Xyl. 48.19–22)

> atque ita ex duobus semitoniis et trisemitonio confundetur genus diatessaron, quod Chromaticum vocatur, ad movendos affectus et luctum ciendum efficacius.

**Sense.** (continuing X49) and so from two semitones and a trihemitone will be blended the genus of the fourth called chromatic, more effective for moving the affections and arousing grief.

**Best base.** Russian: A = C (Both keep the future 'confundetur' ('сольётся') and the comparative as an attribute of the genus.) English: A = C (Both exact.)

**Notes to keep**

- C: confundetur = 'will be blended' (confundo, pour together); affectus = the affections; Vinet's different wording ('moestius luctuosiusque'); printed diatesseron corrected to diatessaron (A and D also record the correction).
- A: Boethius I.21 on the chromatic 'in mollius decidens'.


### X51 (Xyl. 48.22–49.1)

> Sin melodia per duas dieses procedat, alterum intervallorum erit ditonus ex coalitu: idque genus diatessaron, Harmonicum dicetur, quod eo quia sit optimum, a communi harmoniae vocabulo nomen invenit.

**Sense.** But if the melody proceeds by two dieses, the other of the intervals will be a ditone by coalescence; and this genus of the fourth will be called Harmonic, which, because it is the best, found its name from the common word 'harmony'.

**Text proposals and doubts**

- **B**, text issue: printed «cedat, alterum interuallorum erit ditonus ex (48.23)»; proposal: Retain alterum and understand the remaining interval in context ('the remaining interval will be a ditone'). *Verdict: sound*; second pass: confirmed. No emendation is proposed; alterum is printed and makes sense as 'the other (kind) of the intervals' besides the two dieses (C's note), so 'the other of the intervals' should stay in the text; B's 'remaining interval' is a fair gloss for a note, not a needed change. *Evidence*: Lewis & Short s.v. alter ('the other of two; the second') [from memory]; printed line 48.23 [checked]

**Best base.** Russian: A = C (Exact, future 'будет называться' kept, the causal 'eo quia' rendered, supplied word bracketed (A).) English: A = C (Both exact.)

**Notes to keep**

- A: Enharmonic tetrachord: diesis, diesis, incomposite ditone (Boethius I.21 'per diesin et diesin et ditonum'); alterum intervallorum = the third interval left after the two dieses; with 'quia sit optimum' cf. Boethius 'enarmonium vero optime atque apte coniunctum'.


### X52 (Xyl. 49.1–3)

> Neque alia ratione etiam ab harmonia reliqua systematum genera conficiuntur.

**Sense.** Nor in any other way are the remaining genera of systems, too, produced by harmony.

**Best base.** Russian: A = C (Both keep the litotes 'neque alia ratione', 'etiam' and the agent 'ab harmonia'; B and D ('Таким же образом гармония образует') are accurate positive equivalents.) English: A = C (Same.)


### X53 (Xyl. 49.3–6)

> Verum quia exemplo tetrachordi ostendimus, quomodo intervalla ipsa inter se contexerentur, facile studiosus lector etiam de reliquis systematibus coniecturam faciet.

**Sense.** But since we have shown by the example of the tetrachord how the intervals themselves are woven together, the studious reader will easily form a conjecture about the remaining systems too.

**Errors**

- **B** (both; omission; minor; second pass: confirmed): «На примере тетрахорда мы показали … / We have shown by the example of the tetrachord …». 'Verum' ('but'), which turns from the doctrine to the reader, is left out (the causal 'quia' is kept as 'поэтому / so'). *Fix:* Но поскольку на примере тетрахорда мы показали … / But since we have shown …

**Best base.** Russian: A (Exact and idiomatic; C and D equally exact ('составит себе представление' slightly freer for 'coniecturam faciet').) English: A = C (Both exact ('form a conjecture').)


### X54 (Xyl. 49.6–10)

> Sane ad cantum difficilime omnium genus melodiae harmonicum applicari potest, multumque ea res usum ad consuetudinem requirit: quare neque in usum fere venit.

**Sense.** Certainly the harmonic genus of melody can be applied to song with the greatest difficulty of all, and the matter requires much practice to become habitual; therefore it has hardly come (or: hardly comes) into use.

**Text proposals and doubts**

- **A**, doubt: printed «quare neque in | usum ferè uenit (49.9–10)»; proposal: потому он почти и не вошёл в употребление / wherefore it has scarcely come into use either. *Verdict: open*; second pass: confirmed. venit can be present or perfect; the perfect (C's choice) suits a historical statement, the present (A, B, D) the parallel with potest and requirit. Both legitimate; editor's choice. *Evidence*: form identical in present and perfect (vĕnit / vēnit) [from memory]

**Best base.** Russian: A = C (Both render 'applicari potest', 'usum ad consuetudinem' and 'neque … fere' exactly; D compresses 'applicari potest'.) English: A = C (Both exact.)

**Notes to keep**

- A: On the difficulty of the enharmonic and its falling out of use compare the Greek theorists (Aristoxenus, Aristides Quintilianus, from memory); Boethius I.21 says nothing of it.
- C: difficilime is the printed spelling for difficillime.


### X55 (Xyl. 49.10–12)

> Contra diatonicum simplex est, egregium, naturaeque aptum: quo factum est, ut Plato quoque id receperit.

**Sense.** The diatonic, on the contrary, is simple, excellent and suited to nature: whence it came about that Plato too accepted it.

**Best base.** Russian: A = C (Both keep 'Contra' and 'quo factum est, ut' ('оттого и вышло, что'); B and D condense the consecutive clause acceptably.) English: A = C (Both exact.)

**Notes to keep**

- C: Plato: the division of the world-soul in the Timaeus (35b–36b) with tones 9:8 and remainders 256:243, i.e. a diatonic series; Boethius I.21: the diatonic is 'aliquanto durius et naturalius'.
- D: The Republic (398e–399c) concerns the choice of Dorian and Phrygian harmoniai, not the genus; the Latin names no particular dialogue (B makes the same caution).


### X56 (Xyl. 49.12–18)

> Verum horum praeterea unumquodque variatur, inque haud paucas cantuum divisiones, differentias atque species diducitur. Modo enim Lydia, modo Phrygia, mox etiam Dorica cantilena concinnatur: ad quarum unamquamque Paean, Hymenaeus, Exodius, et alia quaecunque melodiarum genera aptantur.

**Sense.** But each of these is moreover varied and divided into not a few divisions, differences and species of songs. For now a Lydian, now a Phrygian, then also a Dorian cantilena is composed; to each of these the paean, the hymenaeus, the exodius and whatever other genera of melodies are fitted.

**Divergences**

1. *Paean, Hymenaeus, Exodius*: Names of the song genres in the running text: Latinized Greek names kept, or described
   - **A, C**: names kept (пеан, гименей, эксодий / paean, hymenaeus, exodius), explained in notes
     - A: «пеан, гименей, эксодий» / “the paean, the hymenaeus, the exodius”
     - C: «пеан, гименей, эксодий» / “the paean, the hymenaeus, the exodius”
   - **B**: paean and hymenaeus kept, Exodius described as the closing / exit song
     - B: «пеан, гименей, заключительную песнь» / “the paean, the hymenaeus, the exit song”
   - **D**: Hymenaeus and Exodius described ('wedding song', 'song of departure'); a funeral sense raised in the doubt
     - D: «пеан, свадебную песнь, прощальную песнь» / “the paean, the wedding song, the song of departure”
   - *Verdict* (low confidence; to the editor; second pass: confirmed): Paean and hymenaeus are established names that a reader of classics understands, so keeping them (A, B, C) is right; D's 'wedding song' is also correct. Exodius is the real problem: Xylander only Latinizes the Greek word and gives no hint of its sense, which may be the closing song (ἐξόδιον, Pollux) or, in Byzantine usage, a funeral hymn (D's doubt). The transliteration (A, C) keeps Xylander's indeterminacy but Russian «эксодий» also names the Roman afterpiece (exodium); B's 'exit song' and D's 'song of departure' choose a sense. Recommend the name with a note giving both possible senses; editor's choice.
   - *Recommended*: пеан, гименей, эксодий (with a note: 'исходная / заключительная песнь'; in Byzantine usage also a funeral hymn) / the paean, the hymenaeus, the exodius (with the same note)
   - *Evidence*: Lewis & Short s.v. exodium (afterpiece) [from memory]; LSJ s.v. ἐξόδιος (closing, of the exodos) [from memory]; Pollux, Onomasticon IV.108 (cited by B and D as checked) [not verified here]; Lampe, Patristic Greek Lexicon s.v. ἐξόδιος ('funeral') [from memory]; Canon of Holy Saturday, ode 1, first troparion: 'ἐξόδιον ὕμνον καὶ ἐπιτάφιον ᾠδήν', the funeral sense D cites [from memory]

**Errors**

- **D** (both; omission; minor; second pass: confirmed): «Каждый из этих [родов], в свою очередь, допускает изменения … Складывается то лидийский … / Each of these [genera] in turn admits of variation … A tune is fashioned now as Lydian …». 'Verum' and 'enim' are left out, so the turn to the subject of variation and the explanatory link of the second sentence are lost; 'praeterea' ('moreover') becomes 'in turn'. *Fix:* Но, кроме того, каждый из них видоизменяется … Ибо слаживается то лидийский … / But each of these is moreover varied … For now a Lydian …

**Text proposals and doubts**

- **D**, doubt: printed «Pæan, Hymenæus, Exodius (49.17)»; proposal: погребальная песнь / funeral song. *Verdict: open* (to the editor); second pass: confirmed. In Byzantine Greek ἐξόδιος (ὕμνος) is used of funeral song (D cites the Holy Saturday canon), which suits a Greek text of 1008; but whether Xylander meant it is unknowable from his Latin. Best kept in a note, not in the running text. *Evidence*: Lampe s.v. ἐξόδιος [from memory]; Canon of Holy Saturday, ode 1, first troparion ('ἐξόδιον ὕμνον καὶ ἐπιτάφιον ᾠδήν'), agreeing with D's citation [from memory]

**Best base.** Russian: A = C (Complete (Verum, praeterea, enim, quaecunque), the plural 'melodiarum' kept, the genre names consistent with the Latin.) English: A = C (Same.)

**Notes to keep**

- D: Exodius: song of departure (Pollux IV.108, exit song of the chorus); in the Byzantine Holy Saturday canon the cognate epithet marks a burial hymn (to be verified).
- C: Latin exodium = afterpiece following a tragedy, Greek ἐξόδιον = closing song of a tragedy; Xylander's masculine Exodius agrees with Paean and Hymenaeus; 'Vide Annot.' in the margin at 49.17 (also D, B).
- A: unamquamque and melodiarum are corrections of the printed text (unamquan- with -que sign; meliodiarum).

**For the editor**

- Exodius: keep the name (эксодий / exodius) with a note on its two possible senses, or describe ('exit song' B, 'song of departure' D)?


### X57 (Xyl. 49.18–24)

> Neque enim sola intervallorum mutatione, aut eorum discrimine, sed praeterea etiam obliquioribus aut celerioribus transitibus, mansionibus, fluxibus, tum tactuum ipsorum pulsationumque variis administrationibus, innumera, atque plane varia cantilenarum genera comparantur.

**Sense.** For not by the change of intervals alone, or by their distinction, but besides also by more oblique or swifter transitions, by stays (on a note), by flowings, and then by the various handlings of the touches and strokes themselves, innumerable and quite varied kinds of cantilenae are produced.

**Errors**
