- **B** (en; omission; minor; second pass: confirmed): «Innumerable and quite varied kinds of tune are obtained by changing or varying the intervals, and also by more oblique or swifter transitions». 'Neque enim sola … sed praeterea etiam' loses 'not … alone' and 'for'; 'eorum discrimine' (their distinction) becomes 'varying'. The Russian keeps 'не только … но также'. *Fix:* For innumerable and quite varied kinds of tune are obtained not only by the change of intervals or their distinction, but also by …
- **D** (both; construal; minor; second pass: confirmed): «как от смены интервалов и различий между ними, так и от … / both from changes of interval … and from …». 'not by … alone, but also' is flattened into 'both … and', losing 'sola'; in the Russian 'variis administrationibus' becomes 'от того, как исполняются', dropping 'variis'. *Fix:* не только сменой интервалов или их различием, но кроме того … и разнообразным обращением с самими касаниями и ударами / not by the change of intervals alone … but also … and by the various handlings of the touches and strokes
- **A** (en; language; minor; second pass: confirmed): «by dwellings, by flowings». 'dwellings' for mansionibus reads as 'houses' in English; the musical sense is stays or rests on a note (the Russian 'остановками' is right). *Fix:* by stays, by flowings

**Text proposals and doubts**

- **A**, doubt: printed «tum tactuum ipso-|rum pulsationumqúe uarijs administrationibus (49.21–22)»; proposal: разнообразным управлением самими тактами и ударами / by the varied handling of the very beats and strokes. *Verdict: open* (to the editor); second pass: confirmed. A's choice 'touches' is supported by classical usage of tactus of strings beside pulsatio; but tactus is also the standard sixteenth-century term for the musical beat, and the context (swifter transitions, stays, flowings) is partly temporal, so a Renaissance reader may have heard 'beats'. Both are possible for Xylander's Latin. *Evidence*: Lewis & Short s.v. tactus I: 'quae (chordae) ad quemque tactum respondeant', Cic. de Or. 3.57.216 [checked]; tactus as the measure-beat in sixteenth-century theory (e.g. Gaffurius, Practica musicae) [from memory]

**Best base.** Russian: A = C (Both complete ('не одной лишь … но сверх того и …', 'разнообразным управлением / обращением с самими касаниями и ударами').) English: C (Complete and exact, with 'stays' for mansionibus; A equally exact but 'dwellings' misleads.)

**Notes to keep**

- A: A list of means of melodic composition (transitus, mansiones, fluxus, tactus, pulsationes); compare the parts of melopoeia in Cleonides; tactus also 'beat' in the sixteenth century, here rather 'touch'.
- C: obliquioribus 'more slanting, roundabout' against celerioribus; Vinet has lentioribus 'slower' here — the versions differ (D notes the same); mansio cf. Aristoxenus' μονή τις καὶ στάσις τῆς φωνῆς (LSJ s.v. μονή).

**For the editor**

- tactus: 'touch' (of strings) or the Renaissance 'beat'? A note giving both seems advisable.


### X58 (Xyl. 49.25)

> Finis Musicae.

**Sense.** The end of the Music (the music section).

**Best base.** Russian: C = D («Конец «Музыки».» marks Musica as the title of the section without adding words; B's bracketed '[раздела]' is also acceptable.) English: C = D ('End of the Music', with the capital marking the section title.)

**Notes to keep**

- C: Closing formula of the music section; Musica names the section (cf. X01 and the running head DE MVSICA); catchword GE[OMETRIA] below.


## Vinet (Tournon 1591)


### V01 (Vinet 41.1–2)

> Michaelis Pselli Musica.

**Sense.** Title: “The Music of Michael Psellus.”

**Best base.** Russian: A = C (All four are correct; A and C mark Musica as the title of a work.) English: A = C (All four are correct; “The Music of Michael Psellus” is the plainest.)

**Notes to keep**

- C: The ascription to Psellus is impossible: the compendium dates itself to AM 6516 (1007/1008), Psellus was born in 1018; the name is kept because the Latin gives it.


### V02 (Vinet 41.3–4)

> Caput I. De Musica in commune.

**Sense.** Chapter heading: “Chapter I. On music in general.”

**Best base.** Russian: A = C (All four are identical in substance.) English: A = C (All four are identical in substance.)

**Notes to keep**

- A: The division into five headed chapters and numbered paragraphs belongs to Vinet's printing; in commune = “in general” (L&S communis).


### V03 (Vinet 41.5–8)

> Musica Universitatem contineri veteres dixerunt. Rerum enim naturalium nulla est, quae vacet symmetria, et proportione:

**Sense.** The ancients said that the universe is held together by music. For of natural things there is none that lacks symmetria (commensurability) and proportion:

**Divergences**

1. *symmetria*: English rendering of the Grecism symmetria (paired with proportio here and in V04–V05); all four Russian versions agree on «соразмерность».
   - **A**: “due measure”
     - A: «соразмерности и пропорции» / “due measure and proportion”
   - **C**: “commensurability”
     - C: «соразмерности и пропорции» / “commensurability and proportion”
   - **B, D**: “symmetry”
     - B: «соразмерности и пропорции» / “symmetry and proportion”
     - D: «соразмерности и пропорции» / “symmetry and proportion”
   - *Verdict* (medium confidence; to the editor; second pass: modified): All construe the word alike (Vitruvian ‘commensurability of the parts of a whole’). Lewis & Short gloss symmetria ‘proportion, symmetry’ and Gaffiot ‘symétrie’, so B's and D's ‘symmetry’ is dictionary-supported and not an error. But modern English ‘symmetry’ suggests mirror symmetry and may mislead a modern reader (about.md § 4.1), and the dictionary's other gloss, ‘proportion’, is already taken by proportio. ‘Commensurability’ renders συμμετρία in its first LSJ sense and matches the Russian «соразмерность»; ‘due measure’ (A) is acceptable but paraphrastic. Recommend C's word, with the Latin and the Vitruvian sense in a note; the choice is the editor's.
   - *Recommended*: соразмерность и пропорция / commensurability and proportion
   - *Evidence*: Lewis & Short s.v. symmetria (‘proportion, symmetry’; Vitr. 1.2; Plin. 34.58, 65) [checked]; Gaffiot 2016 s.v. symmetria (‘symétrie’; Vitr. Arch. 1.3; Plin. 34.58) [checked]; LSJ s.v. συμμετρία (I commensurability; II due proportion, symmetry) [from memory]; Vitruvius, De architectura 1.2.4 (symmetria est ex ipsius operis membris conveniens consensus) [from memory]; about.md § 4.1 [checked]

**Best base.** Russian: C = A (Exact and natural, colon kept so that the sentence runs on into V04.) English: C (Exact; “commensurability” avoids the modern sense of “symmetry”.)

**Notes to keep**

- C: Symmetria: Greek loan, Vitruvius' ‘commensurability of the parts of a whole’ (De arch. 1.2.4); rendered so as to avoid modern ‘symmetry’; the pair symmetria et proportio recurs three times in I.1.
- C: Universitas = the universe (Cic. N.D. 1.39, 1.120); Musica contineri = ‘is held together by music’.
- D: Xylander has commensuratio for symmetria; compare Boethius' musica mundana (De inst. mus. I.2).

**For the editor**

- English for symmetria: “commensurability” (C), “due measure” (A) or “symmetry” (B, D)?


### V04 (Vinet 41.8–13)

> quinimo eorum quae fiunt, si quid non cum apta symmetria et proportione efficitur, nunquam pulchre factum fuerit, sive artis id opus sit, sive naturae: sive sensu id percipiatur aliquo, sive sola mente comprehendi possit.

**Sense.** Nay rather, of the things that come to be, if anything is produced without fitting symmetria and proportion, it will never have been beautifully made, whether it is a work of art or of nature, whether it is perceived by some sense or can be grasped by the mind alone.

**Best base.** Russian: A = C (Both follow the Latin construction (si quid … nunquam … fuerit) exactly and read well.) English: A = C (Both exact; C uses the term recommended in V03.)


### V05 (Vinet 41.13–17)

> Musica autem est ipsa per se symmetria et proportio, ipsaque adeo Universitas, utpote quae universitatis harmonia est ac convenientia.

**Sense.** Music is in itself symmetria and proportion, and indeed the universe itself, inasmuch as it is the harmony and accord (convenientia) of the universe.

**Errors**

- **A** (en; consistency; minor; second pass: confirmed): «and indeed the Universe itself, inasmuch as it is the harmony and accord of the universe». The same word is capitalised and lower-cased within one sentence (mirroring the printer's capitals), while A's Russian capitalises it throughout; the English capital carries no meaning. *Fix:* the universe itself … of the universe

**Text proposals and doubts**

- **A**, doubt: printed «ipsaque adeo Vniuersitas»; proposal: …и такова же сама Вселенная, поскольку музыка является гармонией и согласием Вселенной / …and so indeed is the Universe itself, since music is the harmony and accord of the universe.. *Verdict: unsound*; second pass: confirmed. ipsaque adeo is the emphatic addition of a second predicate of Musica (‘and indeed the universe itself’); the following utpote quae clause explains exactly that identification. A itself rejects the alternative. *Evidence*: Lewis & Short s.v. adeo II.B.1 (ipse adeo, for emphasis) and II.B.5 (atque adeo, adding a stronger point) [checked]
- **B**, doubt: printed «ipsaque adeo Vniuersitas»; proposal: и тем самым сама вселенная / and thus the universe itself. *Verdict: unsound*; second pass: modified. Lewis & Short record a consequential ‘so, thus’ (adeo III.B), so B's alternative is lexically possible. But after a pronoun (ipsaque adeo) the emphatic use applies (II.B.1 ‘just, precisely’, ‘most freq. with pronouns’; cf. II.B.5 atque adeo ‘or even’), and ‘thus’ would turn the climax into an inference that the following utpote clause makes unnecessary. The difference in sense is slight; B itself keeps ‘indeed’. *Evidence*: Lewis & Short s.v. adeo II.B.1, II.B.5, III.B [checked]

**Best base.** Russian: C (Literal and natural; keeps «согласие» for convenientia, the cognate chain Vinet builds on.) English: C (Exact, consistent term, no stray capitals.)

**Notes to keep**

- C: Convenientia (Ciceronian ‘agreement, accord’) begins a chain of cognates: convenientia, conveniens in the definition of the note (V11), convenire (V15); render consistently (согласие – согласующийся – согласоваться / accord – accordant – accord).
- C: Ipsaque adeo Universitas identifies music outright with the universe, not only with its harmony (cf. Xylander's harmonia universi).

**For the editor**

- Musica autem est ipsa per se symmetria et proportio, ipsaque adeo Universitas: all four take the first ipsa with Musica (‘music in itself is …’); the parallel ipsa … ipsaque would also allow ‘music is symmetria and proportion itself … and indeed the universe itself’. The sense hardly changes; keep the translators' reading unless the editor prefers the parallel.


### V06 (Vinet 41.17–42.1)

> Quin et Deum ipsum quoque non inepte fortasse tum sui ipsius, tum universitatis convenientiam dixeris: in quo ita apte consistit universitas, ut perpulchre optimeque habere conspiciatur:

**Sense.** Moreover, you might perhaps not inaptly call God himself too the accord both of himself and of the universe; in him the universe stands so fittingly that it is seen to be in the most beautiful and best state:

**Errors**

- **D** (en; omission; minor; second pass: confirmed): «One might perhaps aptly call God himself». Quin et … quoque (‘moreover … too’) is not rendered in the English; the step from music to God loses its connective. D's Russian keeps «и». *Fix:* Indeed, one might perhaps aptly call God himself too …
- **D** (both; addition; minor; second pass: confirmed): «согласие и Своего собственного существа / the accord both of his own being». sui ipsius is ‘of himself’; ‘being’ / «существа» is added without brackets. *Fix:* согласие и Его самого, и вселенной / the accord both of himself and of the universe

**Best base.** Russian: A = C (Both keep quin et … quoque, the litotes non inepte and the run-on colon.) English: C (Exact and idiomatic (“you might perhaps not inaptly call God himself …”).)

**Notes to keep**

- C: Perpulchre optimeque habere: intransitive habere ‘to be in a state’ (bene habet).


### V07 (Vinet 42.1–2)

> tanta dignam admiratione Musicam existimarunt.

**Sense.** So great was the admiration of which they [the ancients] judged music worthy.

**Errors**

- **A** (ru; language; minor; second pass: confirmed): «столь великого восхищения считали они достойной музыку.». The inversion «считали они достойной музыку» is clumsy Russian. *Fix:* столь великого восхищения считали они музыку достойной.

**Best base.** Russian: B = D (Natural and exact (“Вот какого восхищения они считали музыку достойной”).) English: C (Exact, and supplies the subject [the ancients] in brackets, which the reader needs after V06.)

**Notes to keep**

- C: The subject of existimarunt is veteres from V03.


### V08 (Vinet 42.2–7)

> Musica itaque in rebus ad hunc modum omnibus deprehenditur, omnia componit ornatque, et voluptatem quidem sensui, convenientiam vero naturae, et beatitudinem menti praestat.

**Sense.** Music, then, is in this way found in all things; it orders and adorns all things, and affords pleasure to sense, accord to nature and blessedness to the mind.

**Best base.** Russian: A = C (Both render itaque and ad hunc modum separately and keep the tricolon.) English: A = C (Both exact.)


### V09 (Vinet 42.8–11)

> Nos autem hic de Musica illa, quam sensus aut potius sensuum unus auditus, percipit contemplaturque, breviter disceptabimus.

**Sense.** We, however, shall here briefly discuss that music which sense, or rather one of the senses, hearing, perceives and contemplates.

**Errors**

- **B** (ru; construal; minor; second pass: confirmed): «которую воспринимают и рассматривают посредством чувств, точнее, одного из них — слуха». sensus is the subject (verbs singular: percipit contemplaturque), not a plural instrument; the Russian makes the sentence impersonal and plural, unlike B's own English. *Fix:* которую воспринимает и созерцает чувство, или, вернее, одно из чувств — слух

**Text proposals and doubts**

- **C**, doubt: printed «quam sensus aut potius sensuum vnus auditus, percipit contemplaturque»; proposal: которую воспринимают и созерцают чувства, а вернее — одно из чувств, слух / which the senses, or rather one of the senses, hearing, perceive and contemplate. *Verdict: unsound*; second pass: confirmed. The singular verbs and the correction aut potius (general faculty narrowed to one sense) favour singular sensus, as C itself concludes. *Evidence*: Allen and Greenough § 317 (agreement with disjunctive subjects) [from memory]

**Best base.** Russian: A = D (Exact, singular subject kept, natural word order.) English: A = C (Exact.)


### V10 (Vinet 42.12–14)

> Caput II. De Soni natura, et variis appellationibus.

**Sense.** Chapter heading: “Chapter II. On the nature of the note and its various names.”

**Best base.** Russian: B = D (All correct; B and D need no bracket.) English: B = D (All correct.)

**Notes to keep**

- D: Sonus here is the musical note of definite pitch, opposed at the end of the definition to noise (crepitus); cf. Boethius I.8.


### V11 (Vinet 42.15–16)

> Sonus est vocis intervallo carentis conveniens intensio.

**Sense.** A note is the accordant (conveniens) tension or pitch (intensio) of a voice that has no interval.

**Divergences**

1. *intensio*: Render intensio literally (‘tension’, the stretching of the voice) or by its musical sense (‘pitch’)?
   - **A, C**: ‘tension’, pitch conceived as tautness
     - A: «согласное напряжение голоса» / “an accordant tension of a voice”
     - C: «согласующееся напряжение голоса» / “the accordant tension of a voice”
   - **B, D**: ‘pitch’
     - B: «пригодная для согласования высота голосового звучания» / “a fitting pitch of vocal sound”
     - D: «высота, на которой голос … может согласоваться» / “the pitch of a voice … a pitch capable of accord”
   - *Verdict* (medium confidence; to the editor; second pass: modified): Both are legitimate (about.md § 4.5 leaves intensio on its own to the translator). Lewis & Short record a musical sense (II ‘the tune’: gravis, media, acuta, Censor. 12), and the definition reflects the Greek τάσις (Thrasyllus in Theon: φθόγγος = φωνῆς ἐναρμονίου τάσις), usually rendered ‘pitch’. Vinet's other uses pull both ways: V37 in intensionibus remissionibusve (the raising/lowering pair fixed by § 4.5), V45 ab intensione acuti (pitch of the higher note), V47 torsione claviculorum … intensione and intensione spiritus ac remissione (physical tension, and the pair), V49 bis Dia-pason universam comprehendens intensionem (the whole compass). ‘Tension’ keeps the Latin image and the link with V47; ‘pitch’ is what a modern reader understands (§ 4.1). Whichever is chosen, the other belongs in a note, and the choice must be applied with V37, V45, V47 and V49 in view.
   - *Recommended*: Звук есть согласующаяся высота голоса, не имеющего интервала (in a note: intensio, букв. «напряжение»); if the editor keeps the image, «согласующееся напряжение голоса» with «высота» in the note / A note is the accordant pitch of a voice that has no interval (note: intensio, literally ‘tension’)
   - *Evidence*: Lewis & Short s.v. intensio II (‘the tune’: gravis, media, acuta, Censor. 12) [checked]; Theon, ed. Hiller (1878) p. 47.18–20 (Thrasyllus: φθόγγον … εἶναι φωνῆς ἐναρμονίου τάσιν) [checked]; Boethius, De inst. mus. I.8 (Sonus … vocis casus emmeles … in unam intensionem) [from memory]; latin-texts.json, Vinet V37, V45, V47, V49 [checked]
2. *conveniens*: Whether conveniens is rendered so that Vinet's own gloss in V15 (conveniens dicitur, quod … cum alio sono convenire possit) remains intelligible.
   - **A, C, D**: ‘accordant’, able to accord with another note (cognate of convenire kept)
     - A: «согласное напряжение» / “an accordant tension”
     - C: «согласующееся напряжение» / “the accordant tension”
     - D: «может согласоваться [с другим звуком]» / “a pitch capable of accord”
   - **B**: Russian ‘fit for accord’, English ‘fitting’ (the generic sense)
     - B: «пригодная для согласования высота» / “a fitting pitch”
   - *Verdict* (high confidence; second pass: confirmed): Vinet himself explains the word in V15 by convenire cum alio sono, so the rendering should carry the idea of according with another note; A, C and D do, and so does B's Russian. B's English ‘fitting’ is a dictionary sense but hides Vinet's gloss (B is consistent with it in V15, where the explanation then loses its point). A's «согласное» is acceptable, though «согласный» also evokes the phonetic ‘consonant’; C's «согласующееся» is unambiguous.
   - *Recommended*: согласующееся / accordant
   - *Evidence*: latin-texts.json, V15 (Vinet's gloss) [checked]; Lewis & Short s.v. convenio / convenientia [from memory]

**Errors**

- **D** (ru; construal; minor; second pass: confirmed): «Звук — это высота, на которой голос, не охватывая никакого интервала, может согласоваться [с другим звуком].». Paraphrase: the definition (noun + genitive + adjective) is recast as a relative clause and imports V15's explanation; vocis intervallo carentis becomes an adverbial participle. The sense is close but the form of a definition, parallel to V12–V14, is lost. *Fix:* Звук есть согласующаяся высота голоса, не имеющего интервала.

**Text proposals and doubts**

- **C**, doubt: printed «Sonus est vocis interuallo carentis conueniens intensio.»; proposal: Звук есть подобающее напряжение голоса, не имеющего интервала / A note is the fitting tension of a voice that has no interval.. *Verdict: unsound*; second pass: confirmed. ‘Fitting’ is a possible dictionary sense, but Vinet's explicit gloss in V15 (convenire cum alio sono) fixes the sense ‘accordant’; C rightly rejects the alternative. *Evidence*: latin-texts.json, V15 [checked]

**Best base.** Russian: C (Literal definition, unambiguous «согласующееся», cognate chain kept; only the intensio choice is open.) English: C (Literal and keeps ‘accordant’; replace ‘tension’ by ‘pitch’ if the editor so decides.)

**Notes to keep**

- C: Parallel: Thrasyllus in Theon (Hiller p. 47.18–24) defines the note as the τάσις of a voice ἐναρμόνιος; Xylander here writes harmoniae particeps intensio, seu tenor.
- A: Compare Boethius I.8 (vocis casus emmeles … in unam intensionem): Vinet has no casus, and conveniens stands where emmeles would; intensio = pitch as a degree of tautness (L&S intensio II).

**For the editor**

- intensio in the definition of the note: «напряжение»/“tension” (A, C) or «высота»/“pitch” (B, D)? Decide together with Vinet's other uses in group 4: V37 (intensionibus remissionibusve), V45 (ab intensione acuti), V47 (string tension; intensione spiritus ac remissione) and V49 (universam comprehendens intensionem).


### V12 (Vinet 42.16–17)

> Intervallum, quaedam sonorum inter se habitudo.

**Sense.** An interval is a certain relation (habitudo) of notes to one another.

**Best base.** Russian: A = C («соотношение» keeps habitudo distinct from ratio («отношение»).) English: A = C (All four are identical; A and C have the useful notes.)

**Notes to keep**

- C: Habitudo is classically ‘condition, appearance’; the sense ‘relation’ is Boethian (De inst. arith. II.40: proportio est duorum terminorum ad se invicem quaedam habitudo); cf. Thrasyllus in Theon (Hiller p. 48.8–9): interval = ποιὰ σχέσις of notes.


### V13 (Vinet 42.17–18)

> Systema, quaedam intervallorum complexio.

**Sense.** A system is a certain combination (complexio) of intervals.

**Best base.** Russian: A = C (All four correct.) English: A = C (All four correct; ‘combination’ preferable to D's ‘grouping’ only marginally.)

**Notes to keep**

- C: Cf. Thrasyllus in Theon (Hiller p. 48.10–11): system = ποιὰ περιοχή of intervals; complexio ‘combination’ (Cic. Fin. 1.19).


### V14 (Vinet 42.19)

> Harmonia, systematum compositio.

**Sense.** Harmony is the composition (compositio) of systems.

**Best base.** Russian: A = C («составление» matches compositio best; B «построение» and D «соединение» are acceptable.) English: B = C (All correct.)

**Notes to keep**

- C: Cf. Theon (Hiller p. 48.12–13): harmony = σύνταξις of systems.
- A: The four definitions form a ladder: note, interval, system, harmony; harmony returns in chapter V.


### V15 (Vinet 42.19–23)

> Sonus itaque intervallo dimensioneque vacat, ut punctum in Geometria. Conveniens vero dicitur, quod ita efferri oportet, ut cum alio sono convenire possit,

**Sense.** So the note has no interval and no dimension, like the point in geometry. It is called conveniens (accordant) because it must be uttered so that it can accord with another note,

**Divergences**

1. *Conveniens vero dicitur, quod … cum alio sono convenire possit*: Rendering of conveniens where Vinet explains it by the cognate convenire.
   - **A, C**: ‘accordant’ … ‘accord’ (cognates kept)
     - A: «Согласным же он называется потому, что … мог согласоваться с другим звуком» / “it is called accordant because … it can accord with another note”
     - C: «Согласующимся же он называется потому, что … мог согласоваться с другим звуком» / “It is called accordant because … it can accord with another note”
   - **D**: ‘capable of accord’ … ‘accord’ (cognate kept, periphrastic)
     - D: «Способным к согласию он называется потому, что … мог согласоваться с другим звуком» / “It is called capable of accord because … it can accord with another note”
   - **B**: Russian ‘fit for accord’; English ‘fitting’ (cognate lost in English)
     - B: «Его называют пригодным для согласования, потому что … мог согласоваться с другим звуком» / “It is called fitting because … it can be in accord with another note”
   - *Verdict* (high confidence; second pass: confirmed): The sentence is an etymological gloss: the name must echo the verb. A and C do this most directly; D's periphrasis works; B's Russian works, B's English ‘fitting … be in accord’ breaks the link Vinet makes.
   - *Recommended*: Согласующимся же он называется потому, что его следует издавать так, чтобы он мог согласоваться с другим звуком, / It is called accordant because it must be uttered in such a way that it can accord with another note,
   - *Evidence*: latin-texts.json, V11 and V15 [checked]

**Best base.** Russian: C (Literal, clear, keeps the cognate pair and the run-on comma.) English: C = A (Exact; keeps ‘accordant … accord’.)

**Notes to keep**

- C: Vinet explains conveniens (V11) himself by convenire cum alio sono; hence the cognates in both languages; the sentence continues in V16.
- D: The comparison with the point: Euclid, Elements I def. 1 (a point has no part).


### V16 (Vinet 42.23–43.4)

> quoniam nisi sic elatus sit, absurdus et absonus sonus est, non conveniens: imo vero ne sonus quidem omnino vocandus, sed crepitus quidam temere editus, et ad convenientiam concentumque ineptus.

**Sense.** since, unless it has been so uttered, it is an out-of-tune and ill-sounding note, not an accordant one; nay rather it should not be called a note at all, but a kind of noise (crepitus) uttered at random, unfit for accord and concord.

**Best base.** Russian: C = A (Both keep ‘not accordant’ as a contrast with the definition; C's «треск» renders crepitus most exactly.) English: A = C (Both exact; imo vero correctly as ‘or rather / indeed’.)

**Notes to keep**

- A: Absurdus et absonus is a Ciceronian pair (vox absona atque absurda, De or. 3.41); crepitus ‘rattling, clatter’, an unmusical sound; two words for one notion, as again convenientiam concentumque.
- B: Xylander uses the more technical dissonus / consonus and strepitus here.


### V17 (Vinet 43.5–22)

> Porro autem sonorum appellationes variae sunt, qui ut a gravi ad acutum ordine progrediuntur, sic nominari solent, Proslambanomenos, Hypate hypaton, Parhypate hypaton, Hypate diatonos, Hypate meson, Parhypate meson, Diatonos meson, Mese solum, Paramese, Trite diezeugmene, Diatonos diezeugmene, Nete diezeugmene, Trite hyperbolaeon, Diatonos hyperbolaeon, Nete hyperbolaeon.

**Sense.** Further, the names of the notes are various; as they proceed in order from low to high, they are usually named thus: proslambanomenos, hypate hypaton, parhypate hypaton, hypate diatonos, hypate meson, parhypate meson, diatonos meson, mese solum (‘just mese’), paramese, trite diezeugmene, diatonos diezeugmene, nete diezeugmene, trite hyperbolaeon, diatonos hyperbolaeon, nete hyperbolaeon.

**Divergences**

1. *Mese solum*: Sense of the adverb solum (printed solùm): a remark that the name is just ‘mese’, or ‘mese alone’ (the note standing by itself)?
   - **B, D**: ‘simply mese’ (the name has no second word)
     - B: «просто меса» / “simply mese”
     - D: «просто меса» / “simply mese”
   - **A**: same sense in Russian; English ‘mese only’
     - A: «просто меса» / “mese only”
   - **C**: ‘mese alone’, deliberately keeping the ambiguity between the name and the note
     - C: «меса одна» / “mese alone”
   - *Verdict* (medium confidence; second pass: confirmed): solum is the adverb (the grave accent marks adverbs throughout this printing: Porrò, verò, continuò), and in a list of names it says that this note bears a one-word name, the only one in the list without a tetrachord qualifier. «просто меса» / ‘simply mese’ says this clearly. A's ‘mese only’ is ambiguous in English; C's «меса одна» reads like an adjective agreeing with меса (Latin would then have sola) and like part of the name. Xylander's mese monos shows that both translators found such a word in their original.
   - *Recommended*: просто меса / simply mese
   - *Evidence*: scan vinet-p43 and printed line 43.15 (Mése solùm) [checked]; printed lines 43.5, 43.26, 44.6 (Porrò, continuò, verò) [checked]; latin-texts.json, X14 (mese monos) [checked]

**Errors**

- **B** (both; omission; minor; second pass: confirmed): «У звуков разные названия / The notes have various names». Porro autem (‘further’), which opens the new paragraph II.2, is not rendered. *Fix:* Далее, у звуков разные названия / Further, the notes have various names
- **D** (both; omission; minor; second pass: confirmed): «Названия звуков различны. / The notes have various names.». Porro autem not rendered. *Fix:* Далее, названия звуков различны. / Further, the notes have various names.

**Text proposals and doubts**

- **A**, text issue: printed «Mése solùm (43.15)»; proposal: Keep the reading; solum is the adverb ‘only’, a remark on the name.. *Verdict: sound*; second pass: confirmed. The printed grave accent marks the adverb, as on other adverbs in this printing; the reading text needs no change. *Evidence*: scan vinet-p43 [checked]; printed lines of V17–V22 (Porrò, continuò, verò) [checked]
- **C**, text issue: printed «Hypáte diátonos,»; proposal: No change; the name is Vinet's; explain in a note that the usual name is lichanos (diatonos) hypaton.. *Verdict: sound*; second pass: confirmed. The print is unambiguous and the conventions keep the version's names (about.md § 4.4); the departure belongs in a note. Theon (Thrasyllus' canon) calls this note ὑπερυπάτη ἡ καὶ διάτονος ὑπατῶν, which makes the confusion with ‘hypate’ intelligible. *Evidence*: about.md § 4.4 [checked]; Theon, ed. Hiller p. 88.17–18 [checked]
- **B**, doubt: printed «Mése solùm»; proposal: одна лишь меса / mese alone. *Verdict: open*; second pass: confirmed. Possible, but in a list of names ‘simply mese’ states the point (a one-word name) more clearly; ‘mese alone’ may suggest the note belongs to no tetrachord, whereas mese is the top of the tetrachord meson. *Evidence*: scan vinet-p43 [checked]
- **C**, doubt: printed «Mése solùm»; proposal: просто меса / simply mese. *Verdict: sound*; second pass: confirmed. This is the clearer rendering of the adverb in a list of names (see the divergence). *Evidence*: scan vinet-p43 [checked]

**Best base.** Russian: A (Complete (keeps Porro autem), conventional names, «просто меса».) English: A (Complete and conventional; replace ‘mese only’ by ‘simply mese’.)

**Notes to keep**

- A: Departures from the usual nomenclature: hypate diatonos for lichanos (diatonos) hypaton; the movable notes all called diatonos; diezeugmene in the nominative singular instead of the genitive plural diezeugmenon; mese with a one-word name. The names are the 15 notes of the two-octave system without the conjunct tetrachord.
- C: Theon (Hiller p. 88.17–18, Thrasyllus' canon) calls the fourth note ὑπερυπάτη ἡ καὶ διάτονος ὑπατῶν; Vinet writes Paramese, Xylander paramesos.
- D: Boethius names the fourth diatonic note lichanos hypaton diatonos (De inst. mus. I.20, I.22).

**For the editor**

- Mese solum: «просто меса» / ‘simply mese’ (A ru, B, D) or «меса одна» / ‘mese alone’ (C)?


### V18 (Vinet 43.23–26)

> Ex his conficitur, quod in Pentekaedecachordo est Heckaedecachordum: quando finis Octachordi, principium deinceps continuo sequentibus constituitur.

**Sense.** From these [notes] is made up the sixteen-stringed [system] that is in the fifteen-stringed one (or: from this it follows that in the fifteen-stringed there is a sixteen-stringed), since the end of the octachord is made the beginning for those that follow next in unbroken succession.

**Divergences**

1. *Ex his conficitur, quod in Pentekaedecachordo est Heckaedecachordum*: Is conficitur ‘is made up, composed’ with quod as a relative (antecedent Heckaedecachordum drawn into the clause), or the logical ‘it follows’ with quod as a conjunction?
   - **A, B, D**: composition: from these [notes] the sixteen-string [system] in the fifteen-string one is formed
     - A: «Из них составляется та шестнадцатиструнная [система], что находится в пятнадцатиструнной» / “From these is made up the sixteen-stringed [system] that is in the fifteen-stringed one”
     - B: «Из них образуется шестнадцатиструнная система в пятнадцатиструнной» / “They form the sixteen-string system within the fifteen-string system”
     - D: «Из них образуется шестнадцатиструнная система, заключённая в пятнадцатиструнном инструменте» / “From these is formed the sixteen-string system contained in the fifteen-string instrument”
   - **C**: logical consequence: ‘from these it follows that …’
     - C: «Отсюда следует, что в пятнадцатиструнном [инструменте] заключена шестнадцатиструнная [система]» / “From these it follows that in the fifteen-string [instrument] there is a sixteen-string [system]”
   - *Verdict* (medium confidence; to the editor; second pass: modified): Both are grammatically possible. The logical sense is lexicalised (L&S conficio I.B.3, passive ‘to follow logically, be deduced’, with ex; Gaffiot: ex quo conficitur ut, Cic. Inv. 2.145; ex eo hoc conficitur … esse, Cic. Inv. 1.63), and humanist Latin often has quod for ut or for the accusative and infinitive. The composition reading has the stronger support inside this text: Vinet uses conficere for making up an interval from its parts (V39 Conficitur autem Dia-tessaron duobus tonis et hemitonio …; V55 ex tribus intervallis Dia-tessaron intervallum confectum est) and for melos and modulationes (V32, V65); ex his naturally means the fifteen notes just listed (cf. L&S I.A.1 unde pallium conficiatur, making from a material); and Xylander's parallel (Per has sonorum species heccaedecachordum … absolvitur) expresses composition. Prefer composition; C's reading is a defensible minority construal, not an error. The doctrine is the same either way, so the final choice is the editor's.
   - *Recommended*: Из них составляется шестнадцатиструнная [система], заключённая в пятнадцатиструнной, / From these is made up the sixteen-stringed [system] that is contained in the fifteen-stringed one,
   - *Evidence*: Lewis & Short s.v. conficio I.A.1 (eme lanam, unde pallium conficiatur, Plaut. Mil. 3.1.93) and I.B.3 (pass. ‘to follow logically, to be deduced’, with ex) [checked]; Gaffiot s.v. conficio (ex quo conficitur ut, Cic. Inv. 2.145; ex eo hoc conficitur … esse, Cic. Inv. 1.63) [checked]; latin-texts.json, V32, V39, V55, V65, X15 [checked]
2. *in Pentekaedecachordo … Heckaedecachordum*: Which noun is understood with the two substantivised adjectives, and is it marked as an addition?
   - **A**: [system] in both places, bracketed
     - A: «шестнадцатиструнная [система], что находится в пятнадцатиструнной» / “the sixteen-stringed [system] that is in the fifteen-stringed one”
   - **C**: fifteen-string [instrument], sixteen-string [system], bracketed
     - C: «в пятнадцатиструнном [инструменте] заключена шестнадцатиструнная [система]» / “in the fifteen-string [instrument] there is a sixteen-string [system]”
   - **B**: system in both places, unbracketed
     - B: «шестнадцатиструнная система в пятнадцатиструнной» / “the sixteen-string system within the fifteen-string system”
   - **D**: sixteen-string system in a fifteen-string instrument, unbracketed
     - D: «шестнадцатиструнная система, заключённая в пятнадцатиструнном инструменте» / “the sixteen-string system contained in the fifteen-string instrument”
   - *Verdict* (medium confidence; to the editor; second pass: confirmed): Vinet supplies no noun. Heckaedecachordum is one of the systems in V49 (Systematum … aliud deinceps Heckaedecachordum), so [system] is well founded for it; for Pentekaedecachordum both [system] and [instrument] are possible (Xylander glosses with instrumentum … organo). Any supplied noun must be in brackets (about.md § 4.6); B and D add it silently.
   - *Recommended*: шестнадцатиструнная [система] … в пятнадцатиструнной / the sixteen-stringed [system] … in the fifteen-stringed one
   - *Evidence*: latin-texts.json, V49, X15 [checked]; about.md § 4.6 [checked]

**Errors**

- **B** (both; sign; minor; second pass: confirmed): «шестнадцатиструнная система в пятнадцатиструнной / the sixteen-string system within the fifteen-string system … the notes that follow». ‘system’ (both languages, twice in English) and English ‘the notes’ are supplied without square brackets. *Fix:* шестнадцатиструнная [система] / the sixteen-string [system] within the fifteen-string [one] … the [notes] that follow
- **D** (both; sign; minor; second pass: confirmed): «шестнадцатиструнная система, заключённая в пятнадцатиструнном инструменте / the sixteen-string system contained in the fifteen-string instrument». ‘system’ and ‘instrument’ are supplied without brackets. *Fix:* шестнадцатиструнная [система] … в пятнадцатиструнном [инструменте] / the sixteen-string [system] … in the fifteen-string [instrument]

**Text proposals and doubts**

- **A**, doubt: printed «Ex his conficitur, quod in Pentekædecachordo est Heckædecachordum»; proposal: Из этого следует, что в пятнадцатиструнной [системе] есть шестнадцатиструнная… / From this it follows that in the fifteen-stringed [system] there is a sixteen-stringed one…. *Verdict: open*; second pass: confirmed. Grammatically possible (C adopts it), but the composition reading fits Vinet's use of conficere and ex his better; the doctrine is the same. *Evidence*: Lewis & Short s.v. conficio I.B.3 [checked]
- **D**, doubt: printed «Ex his conficitur, quod in Pentekædecachordo est Heckædecachordum»; proposal: Отсюда следует, что в пятнадцатиструнном инструменте заключена шестнадцатиструнная система. / From this it follows that the fifteen-string instrument contains a sixteen-string system.. *Verdict: open*; second pass: confirmed. As for A's doubt. *Evidence*: Lewis & Short s.v. conficio I.B.3 [checked]
- **C**, doubt: printed «in Pentekædecachordo est Heckædecachordum»; proposal: в пятнадцатиструнной [системе] заключена шестнадцатиструнная / in the fifteen-string [system] there is a sixteen-string [one]. *Verdict: open* (to the editor); second pass: confirmed. The Latin supplies no noun; [system] for both is as defensible as [instrument] for the first. *Evidence*: latin-texts.json, V49 [checked]

**Best base.** Russian: A (Composition reading, supplied nouns bracketed, close to the Latin.) English: A (Same reasons.)

**Notes to keep**

- A: Pentekaedecachordum and Heckaedecachordum are Greek words kept by Vinet (k / ck as printed); two octachords would give 16 notes, but the shared eighth note is counted once, so there are 15 strings.
- C: πεντεκαιδεκάχορδος is attested (LSJ, Adrastus in Theon); no established modern term, hence a descriptive rendering.

**For the editor**

- Which noun to supply: [system] for both (A) or [instrument] for Pentekaedecachordum (C)?
- Ex his conficitur, quod …: composition (A, B, D) or logical consequence (C)?


### V19 (Vinet 43.26–44.2)

> Quam enim rationem primus sonus ad octavum habere cernitur, eandem et octavus quoque ipse ad quindecimum habet.

**Sense.** For the ratio which the first note is seen to have to the eighth, the eighth itself also has to the fifteenth.

**Best base.** Russian: C = A (Both keep the correlative structure and need no bracket (no ratio name).) English: B = D (Exact and the most natural English word order.)


### V20 (Vinet 44.2–3)

> Atque de sonis quidem hactenus.

**Sense.** And so much for the notes.

**Best base.** Russian: C (All correct; C is the most neutral.) English: A = C (All correct.)


### V21 (Vinet 44.4–5)

> Caput III. De intervallis.

**Sense.** Chapter heading: “Chapter III. On intervals.”

**Best base.** Russian: A = C (All four identical in substance.) English: A = C (All four identical in substance.)


### V22 (Vinet 44.6–14)

> Iam vero intervallorum minimum quidem est Diesis. Deinde hemitonium, quod Diesi duplo acutius est: Postremo Tonus, ex duobus constans hemitoniis: cuius quidem intervalli ratio sesquioctava est, quomodo novem numerus ad octo habet: ut sit Hemitonium, veluti octo cum semisse: et Diesis, ut, octo cum quadrante:

**Sense.** Now, the smallest of the intervals is the diesis; next the semitone, which is twice as high (i.e. twice as large a step upward) as the diesis; last the tone, consisting of two semitones, whose ratio is sesquioctave [9:8], as nine is to eight; so that the semitone is as it were eight and a half, and the diesis eight and a quarter [to eight]:

**Divergences**

1. *hemitonium, quod Diesi duplo acutius est*: Translate the comparative literally (‘twice as high as the diesis’) or by its sense (the semitone raises the pitch twice as far)?
   - **A, C, D**: literal ‘twice as high as the diesis’, explained in a note
     - A: «полутон, который вдвое выше диесы» / “the semitone, which is twice as high as the diesis”
     - C: «полутон, который вдвое выше диесы» / “the semitone, which is twice as high as the diesis”
     - D: «полутон, который вдвое выше диесы» / “the semitone, which is twice as high as the diesis”
   - **B**: interpretive: the semitone raises the pitch twice as much as the diesis
     - B: «полутон, который даёт вдвое большее повышение, чем диеса» / “the semitone, which raises the pitch twice as much as the diesis”
   - *Verdict* (medium confidence; to the editor; second pass: confirmed): Both readings understand the Latin alike: Diesi (ablative of comparison) duplo acutius, ‘higher by double than the diesis’, i.e. a step upward twice the size. The brief asks for the Latin as it stands with the difficulty explained in a note, which A, C and D do; B's rendering is correct in sense but turns the comparative into an explanation not in the words (cf. Xylander's cuius dupla ad acutum est accessio, which is closer to B). Recommend the literal rendering with a note.
   - *Recommended*: затем полутон, который вдвое выше диесы (note: т. е. вдвое больший шаг вверх) / next the semitone, which is twice as high as the diesis (note: i.e. a step upward twice as large)
   - *Evidence*: Lewis & Short s.v. duplus (duplo major) [from memory]; latin-texts.json, X18 [checked]; translation-brief.md, The task (render what the Latin says, explain in a note) [checked]

**Text proposals and doubts**

- **C**, text issue: printed «p. 44, between lines 44.12 and 44.13: a figure (line AB, numbers 9 … 1, letters C … L, and I, M between 8 and 9)»; proposal: Record the figure, which belongs to this segment, in the notes.. *Verdict: sound*; second pass: confirmed. The woodcut is on the page inside this sentence: A at the left end under 9, L under 8, then K, H, G, F, E, a blurred letter (G-like; D expected) and C under 7 … 1, B at the right end without numeral; I and M lie between L and A, I nearer L (about a quarter to a third of the unit) and M about halfway, i.e. 8¼ and 8½ measured from B. It confirms that ‘eight and a half / a quarter’ are lengths from one end. No change to the reading text. *Evidence*: scan vinet-p44, figure enlarged [checked]

**Best base.** Russian: C = A (Literal, all numbers and the bracket right, notes explain the arithmetic.) English: C = A (Literal, exact numbers, ‘as it were’ for veluti/ut.)

**Notes to keep**

- C: Description of the figure on p. 44 (line AB, 9 … 1, letters, points I and M between 8 and 9 standing for 8¼ and 8½; the woodcut is approximate).
- A: ‘Eight and a half’ and ‘eight and a quarter’ to eight give 17:16 and 33:32: the excess of 9 over 8 is halved, not the interval; (17/16)² = 289/256 > 288/256; a superparticular ratio has no rational mean (Sectio canonis 3); in Boethius 17:16 is the larger part of the tone 16:17:18.
- C: Xylander here says the semitone is ‘as it were half of the sesquioctave’, the diesis ‘its quarter’; Vinet gives numbers instead.
- A: The diesis here is the quarter-tone, half the semitone (Boethius I.21: diesis … semitonii dimidium); hemitonium is the Greek form where Xylander has semitonium.

**For the editor**

- duplo acutius: literal «вдвое выше» / ‘twice as high’ with a note (A, C, D) or B's explanatory rendering?


### V23 (Vinet 44.14–17)

> quandoquidem licet monas non possit dividi per numeros, illa tamen geometrico modo dividi potest, ubi in materia consideratur.

**Sense.** for although the unit (monas) cannot be divided by numbers, it can nevertheless be divided geometrically when it is considered in matter.

**Best base.** Russian: A = C (Both exact and natural.) English: A = C (Both exact.)

**Notes to keep**

- A: Monas (printed monàs) is Greek μονάς, kept by Vinet (Boethius would say unitas); the unit meant is that by which 9 exceeds 8: indivisible arithmetically, divisible as a length on a string.


### V24 (Vinet 44.17–45.1)

> Chordam enim, quae sesquioctavam continet rationem, potes partiri in semisesquioctavam, quod hemitonium est: et eandem rursus dividere in diesin:

**Sense.** For you can divide the string that contains the sesquioctave [9:8] ratio into the semisesquioctave, which is the semitone, and divide the same again into the diesis:

**Divergences**

1. *et eandem rursus dividere in diesin*: Reference of eandem: the string (chordam) or the semisesquioctave (feminine, with rationem understood)?
   - **A, C**: ambiguity kept in English (‘the same’); in Russian «её же» can only resume the feminine «струну», since their «полусверхосминное» is neuter
     - A: «и её же снова разделить на диесу» / “and divide the same again into the diesis”
     - C: «и её же снова разделить на диесу» / “and divide the same again into the diesis”
   - **B, D**: the string (B with a bracket; D by English ‘it’ and the Russian feminine «её»)
     - B: «а затем вновь разделить ту же [струну], получив диесу» / “and divide the same [string] in turn to obtain a diesis”
     - D: «и её же снова разделить, чтобы получить диесу» / “and divide it again to obtain the diesis”
   - *Verdict* (medium confidence; second pass: modified): Grammatically open: eandem can resume chordam or semisesquioctavam (rationem); the neuter hemitonium is excluded. Xylander's parallel (et hoc denuo dividi in diesin, hoc = semitonium) shows that the doctrine is the halving of the semitone, which either reference yields. In English, ‘the same’ (A, C) keeps the ambiguity and is closest; B's bracket is a legitimate, marked decision. In Russian all four in effect choose the string, because «её» agrees only with «струну»; that is a legitimate reading, so the Russian renderings are not at fault, but A's doubt and C's note wrongly say that their Russian keeps the ambiguity (see errors). The other reference belongs in a note.
   - *Recommended*: и её же снова разделить на диесу (note: eandem may also resume semisesquioctavam: «и это полусверхосминное снова разделить на диесу») / and divide the same again into the diesis
   - *Evidence*: the Latin of the segment [checked]; latin-texts.json, X20 (et hoc denuo dividi in diesin) [checked]

**Errors**

- **A** (notes; note-claim; minor; second pass: added): «Left open: 'the same' / «её же» keeps the ambiguity of the Latin.». In A's Russian «её же» can only resume the feminine «струну» («полусверхосминное» is neuter, «полутон» masculine), so the Russian chooses the string; only the English keeps the ambiguity. *Fix:* Say that the English keeps the ambiguity, while the Russian follows the string reading and the alternative is given in the note.
- **C** (notes; note-claim; minor; second pass: added): «Eandem «её же» может относиться и к струне, и к полусверхосминному; двусмысленность сохранена.». The Russian pronoun «её» is feminine and cannot refer to the neuter «полусверхосминное»; C's Russian text resolves eandem to the string, so the claim that the ambiguity is kept holds only for the English. *Fix:* Eandem может относиться и к струне, и к полусверхосминному [отношению]; в русском тексте «её же» указывает на струну. / … the ambiguity is kept in the English; the Russian follows the string reading.

**Text proposals and doubts**

- **A**, doubt: printed «et eandem rursus diuidere in diesin»; proposal: …и это же [полусверхосминное отношение] снова разделить на диесу / …and divide that same [semisesquioctave] again into the diesis. *Verdict: open*; second pass: confirmed. Equally possible grammatically; same doctrine. *Evidence*: the Latin of the segment [checked]
- **B**, doubt: printed «et eandem rursus diuidere in diesin»; proposal: а затем вновь разделить то же полусверхосминное [отношение], получив диесу / and then divide that same semisesquioctave [ratio] again to yield a diesis. *Verdict: open*; second pass: confirmed. As for A's doubt. *Evidence*: the Latin of the segment [checked]
- **D**, doubt: printed «et eandem rursus diuidere in diesin»; proposal: и снова разделить полученное полусверхосминное [отношение], чтобы получить диесу / and subdivide that same semisesquioctave [ratio] to obtain a diesis. *Verdict: open*; second pass: confirmed. As for A's doubt. *Evidence*: the Latin of the segment [checked]

**Best base.** Russian: C (Exact, idiomatic impersonal «можно», no bracket on semisesquioctava (the text says what it is); «её же» follows the string reading, which is legitimate.) English: C = A (Exact; ambiguity kept.)

**Notes to keep**

- C: Semisesquioctava ‘half of the ratio 9:8’, formed on the Boethian pattern; eandem may refer to the string or to the semisesquioctave.
- A: The true half of 9:8 is the mean proportional √72 (≈ 101.96 cents), irrational, hence constructible only geometrically, which is the point of V25–V30.


### V25 (Vinet 45.1–5)

> quoniam Geometria tum minorem a maiore omni modo abscindit lineam, tum minori addit maiorem: indeque fit, ut quaelibet figura augeri quantumvis et minui possit.

**Sense.** since geometry both cuts off, in any way you like, a lesser line from a greater and adds a greater to a lesser; and hence any figure can be increased and diminished as much as one pleases.

**Divergences**

1. *omni modo abscindit*: Sense of omni modo: ‘in every way, in any manner’ or ‘always’?
   - **A, B, C**: ‘in every way / in any manner / without restriction’
     - A: «отнимает любым способом» / “cuts off in every way”
     - B: «можно без всяких ограничений отсечь» / “can cut off … without restriction”
     - C: «отсекает любым образом» / “cuts off, in any manner”
   - **D**: ‘always’
     - D: «всегда можно отсечь» / “can always cut”
   - *Verdict* (medium confidence; second pass: confirmed): L&S gives omnimodo ‘by all means, in all ways, entirely’; ‘in every way / in any manner’ is the direct sense. D's ‘always’ is an interpretation (‘in every case’), close to Xylander's semper here, but not what the Latin word says; acceptable, not an error.
   - *Recommended*: отсекает любым образом меньшую линию от большей / cuts off, in any manner, a lesser line from a greater
   - *Evidence*: Lewis & Short s.v. omnimodo (‘by all means, in all ways, entirely, altogether, wholly’) [checked]; Gaffiot 2016 s.v. omnimodo (‘de toute façon, de toute manière’); Georges (‘auf alle Weise, in jeder Hinsicht’) [checked]; latin-texts.json, X21 (semper … posse) [checked]

**Text proposals and doubts**

- **C**, doubt: printed «omni modo abscindit»; proposal: поскольку геометрия во всяком случае отсекает меньшую линию от большей / since geometry at all events cuts off a smaller line from a greater. *Verdict: unsound*; second pass: modified. Lexically not impossible (L&S omnimodo ‘by all means … entirely’; Gaffiot ‘de toute façon’), but here omni modo qualifies abscindit in a contrast with arithmetic: geometry can cut off any lesser length whatever (V26–V30), so ‘in any manner’ is the sense, as C itself argues; ‘at all events’ would reduce the adverb to an asseveration and lose the point. *Evidence*: Lewis & Short s.v. omnimodo [checked]; Gaffiot 2016 and Georges s.v. omnimodo [checked]; latin-texts.json, X21 [checked]

**Best base.** Russian: C = A (Both keep the tum … tum construction and render omni modo directly.) English: C = A (Exact.)

**Notes to keep**

- C: Cf. Euclid, Elements I.2–3 (placing a given length at a point; cutting off from the greater of two unequal lines a line equal to the less).


### V26 (Vinet 45.6)

> Arithmetica vero id facere nequit:

**Sense.** Arithmetic, however, cannot do this:

**Best base.** Russian: A = C (All correct; A and C keep the colon that leads into V27.) English: A = C (All correct.)


### V27 (Vinet 45.6–14)

> haec enim datum quadratum distribuere in quadrata aequalia non potest, nec duplicare datum. Sedecim namque numerum quadratum, neque in paria dividi quadrata est possibile (octo enim quadrari non possunt) neque in quadratum aliud duplicari: quoniam triginta duo numerus quadrari nullo modo potest.

**Sense.** for it cannot divide a given square into equal squares, nor double a given one. For the square number sixteen can neither be divided into equal squares (for eight cannot be made a square) nor doubled into another square, since the number thirty-two can in no way be made a square.

**Errors**

- **A** (en; language; minor; second pass: confirmed): «for eight cannot be squared … the number thirty-two can in no way be squared». In modern English ‘to square a number’ means to multiply it by itself, so ‘eight cannot be squared’ reads as false; quadrari here means ‘to be made (laid out as) a square number’. A's note explains it, but the running text misleads. *Fix:* for eight cannot be made a square … thirty-two can in no way be made a square

**Text proposals and doubts**

- **C**, text issue: printed «p. 46, top of the page: dot figures labelled 16, 8, 32 and a geometric figure with points A B C D E F»; proposal: Record the figures in the notes to V27 and V29.. *Verdict: sound*; second pass: confirmed. The scan shows 16 as four rows of four dots, 8 as four rows of two, 32 as eight rows of four divided by a rule after the fourth row (two squares of 16 making an oblong); to the right, square ABDC with diagonals AD, BC meeting at E and a point F above BD joined to B and D (square BFDE on BD as diagonal). No change to the reading text. *Evidence*: scan vinet-p46 [checked]

**Best base.** Russian: C (Exact; «из восьми нельзя составить квадрат» gives the figurate sense of quadrari.) English: C (Exact; ‘cannot be made into a square’ avoids the modern sense of ‘squared’.)

**Notes to keep**

- C: Quadrari is figurate: 8 or 32 units cannot be laid out as a square; description of the dot figures on p. 46.
- D: The example of eight shows that division into two equal squares is meant; ‘into equal squares’ alone would be wider (16 = 4 + 4 + 4 + 4). Xylander says in duo … quadrata.

**For the editor**

- in paria … quadrata: all render ‘into equal squares’; the parenthesis (octo …) shows that two equal squares are meant. A note (D, A) suffices, or ‘into two equal squares’ with [two] bracketed?


### V28 (Vinet 45.15)

> At Geometriae facillimum hoc:

**Sense.** But for geometry this is very easy:

**Best base.** Russian: A = C (All correct; A and C keep the colon.) English: A = C (All correct.)


### V29 (Vinet 45.15–21)

> Quadrati enim latus Geometra si in diametrum traduxerit, et ei quadratum circumiecerit diametro, dimidium dati quadrati habebit, atque ex quadrati dimetiente rursus si latus Geometra effecerit, datum duplicaverit quadratum.

**Sense.** For if the geometer turns the side of a square into a diagonal and puts a square around that diagonal, he will have half the given square; and if in turn he makes a side out of the square's diagonal, he will have doubled the given square.

**Best base.** Russian: C = A (Both follow circumiecerit (‘опишет около/вокруг’) and the future perfects closely.) English: C = A (Exact.)

**Notes to keep**

- C: Description of the geometric figure on p. 46: square ABDC, diagonals AD and BC meeting at E, F above BD; BFDE, with BD as diagonal, is half of ABDC; the doubled square is not drawn.
- D: Read the other way, the same figure also shows the doubling: taking BFDE as given, ABDC stands on its diagonal and is twice its area.
- A: The diagonal is named twice, diametrus (Greek) and dimetiens (its Latin calque, Pliny); the doubling of the square on its diagonal is the construction of Plato's Meno 82b–85b. Xylander has diameter in both places.


### V30 (Vinet 45.21–23)

> Ad hunc sane modum et sesquioctava toni ratio in hemitonium dividitur, rursusque in diesin.

**Sense.** In just this way the sesquioctave [9:8] ratio of the tone too is divided into the semitone, and again into the diesis.

**Best base.** Russian: A = C (Both render et (‘too’) and rursus, bracket present.) English: A = C (Exact.)

**Notes to keep**

- C: The analogy is exact: the side of the half-square is to the given side as 1 : √2, a mean proportional; half the tone 9:8 is the mean proportional √72 between 9 and 8, which no number expresses.
- A: The argument defends the halving of the tone, against which Boethius argues in De inst. mus. III.1; Vinet's numbers in V22 (17:16, 33:32) are only approximations of the geometric half and quarter.


### V31 (Vinet 46.1–5)

> Horum autem adauctu, alia quoque simplicia intervalla fiunt, Tridiesis, Trihemitonium, Ditonium, et Tritonium; et ut volueris, et rythmus tulerit, quomodo ex sequentibus cognoscetur.

**Sense.** By the increase of these, other simple intervals too arise: the tridiesis, the trihemitone, the ditone and the tritone; and [others] as you wish and as the rhythm allows, as will be learned from what follows.

**Divergences**

1. *alia quoque simplicia intervalla*: Render Vinet's simplicia as ‘simple’ or by the conventional term for incomposite intervals?
   - **A, C, D**: ‘simple’, Vinet's word, with a note that it means incomposite
     - A: «другие простые интервалы» / “other simple intervals”
     - C: «другие простые интервалы» / “other simple intervals”
     - D: «другие простые интервалы» / “other simple intervals”
   - **B**: ‘incomposite’ (the convention for incompositum)
     - B: «другие несоставные интервалы» / “other incomposite intervals”
   - *Verdict* (medium confidence; to the editor; second pass: confirmed): about.md § 4.2 fixes the term for compositum / incompositum (non compositum), not for simplex. Vinet uses simplicia here and in V32 (where he explains the name: Simplicia autem ideo dicta sunt …) and non-composita in V34; Xylander has incomposita. Keeping ‘simple’ keeps both Vinet's explanation of the name and the difference from non-composita and from Xylander visible, as the brief asks. B's choice is defensible under § 4.1 (B notes the Latin) but erases that difference. One caution: in modern theory a ‘simple interval’ is one within the octave, so a note is needed.
   - *Recommended*: другие простые интервалы (note: simplicia = несоставные) / other simple intervals (note: simplicia = incomposite)
   - *Evidence*: about.md § 4.2 [checked]; latin-texts.json, V32, V34, X26–X27 [checked]; translation-brief.md, Translation principles (keep the translators' different words visible) [checked]
2. *et ut volueris, et rythmus tulerit*: The clause is elliptical: what arises ‘as you wish and as the rhythm allows’?
   - **A**: literal, ellipsis left unfilled
     - A: «и как тебе будет угодно и как допустит ритм» / “and as you wish and as the rhythm allows”
   - **C, D**: further intervals, supplied as [others] in brackets
     - C: «и [иные] — как будет угодно и как позволит ритм» / “and [others], as you will and as the rhythm allows”
     - D: «и [другие] — какие пожелаешь и какие допустит ритм» / “and [others] as one wishes and as rhythm will allow”
   - **B**: a new sentence ‘you may proceed as you wish’, supplied without brackets
     - B: «Продолжать можно как угодно, насколько позволяет ритм» / “You may proceed as you wish and as rhythm permits”
   - *Verdict* (medium confidence; second pass: confirmed): The clause extends the list: further intervals may be formed by increase as one wishes and the rhythm allows (cf. Xylander: et quo quis modo voluerit, idque rhythmus admiserit). C and D make this clear with a marked addition; A's literal rendering is acceptable but obscure; B's ‘proceed’ is a plausible sense but an unmarked addition.
   - *Recommended*: и [другие] — как пожелаешь и как позволит ритм, что станет ясно из дальнейшего / and [others], as you wish and as the rhythm allows, as will be learned from what follows
   - *Evidence*: latin-texts.json, X26 [checked]; Lewis & Short s.v. fero (si res feret, ‘if circumstances allow’) [from memory]

**Errors**

- **B** (both; sign; minor; second pass: confirmed): «Продолжать можно как угодно / You may proceed as you wish». ‘Proceed’ / «продолжать» is supplied without brackets, and the clause is detached from the list it extends. *Fix:* и [другие] — как угодно и насколько позволит ритм / and [others], as you wish and as rhythm permits

**Text proposals and doubts**

- **C**, doubt: printed «& vt volueris, & rythmus tulerit»; proposal: and [they are formed] as you will and as the rhythm allows. *Verdict: open*; second pass: confirmed. Possible; it differs from C's chosen ‘[others]’ only in whether the listed intervals or further ones are meant. Xylander's parallel favours further intervals. *Evidence*: latin-texts.json, X26 [checked]

**Best base.** Russian: C (Keeps ‘simple’, brackets the supplied [иные], all four interval names per convention.) English: C = D (Exact, with the ellipsis supplied in brackets.)

**Notes to keep**

- C: Simplicia intervalla = incomposite intervals (contrasted in V34 with the neque non-composita fourth, fifth, octave); Xylander has incomposita; cf. Theon's τριημιτόνιον ἀσύνθετον of the chromatic tetrachord (Hiller pp. 54–55).
- A: Adauctus is a rare Lucretian word (2.1122); Vinet's forms trihemitonium, ditonium, tritonium (Xylander trisemitonium, ditonus, tritonus); rythmus printed without h.

**For the editor**

- simplicia intervalla: «простые»/‘simple’ (A, C, D) or «несоставные»/‘incomposite’ (B)? Decide together with V32 and V34.


### V32 (Vinet 46.5–10)

> Simplicia autem ideo dicta sunt, quod veluti unum ex coniunctis confusisque tribus diesibus, seu tribus hemitoniis seu multis etiam tonis conficiatur melos, quod licet multorum vim habeat intervallorum, tamen per unicum resonet intervallum.

**Sense.** They are called simple because from three dieses joined and fused together, or from three semitones, or even from many tones, as it were one melos is made, which, although it has the force of many intervals, nevertheless sounds through a single interval.

**Divergences**

1. *melos*: Rendering of the Greek loan melos (used by Vinet only here), meaning a single melodic step or movement.
   - **A, C**: transliterated «мелос» / ‘melos’, explained in a note
     - A: «образуется как бы единый мелос» / “there is made as it were a single melos”
     - C: «составляется как бы один мелос» / “a single melos is made”
   - **B**: ‘melody’
     - B: «образуется как бы единая мелодия» / “what is virtually one melody is formed”
   - **D**: ‘melodic strain’ / «напев»
     - D: «слагается как бы один напев» / “form as it were a single melodic strain”
   - *Verdict* (medium confidence; to the editor; second pass: confirmed): What is meant is one melodic move heard as a single interval. ‘A melody’ (B) suggests a whole tune and may collide with Vinet's modulatio (‘melody’) elsewhere; «напев» / ‘melodic strain’ (D) has the same drawback, though less. «Мелос» is established in Russian musicology and ‘melos’ is used in English writing on Greek music, so A and C keep Vinet's Grecism without coining; the note should say it is a single melodic step. The editor may prefer a descriptive rendering (§ 4.1).
   - *Recommended*: как бы один мелос (note: единый мелодический ход) / as it were a single melos (note: one melodic move)
   - *Evidence*: Lewis & Short s.v. melos (‘a tune, air, strain, song’) [from memory]; LSJ s.v. μέλος [from memory]; latin-texts.json, V52–V65 (modulatio) [checked]
2. *Simplicia autem ideo dicta sunt*: The name explained here: ‘simple’ or ‘incomposite’ (continuation of the divergence in V31).
   - **A, C, D**: ‘simple’
     - A: «Простыми же они названы потому» / “They are called simple because”
     - C: «Простыми же они названы потому» / “They are called simple because”
     - D: «Простыми же они названы потому» / “They are called simple because”
   - **B**: ‘incomposite’
     - B: «Несоставными же они зовутся потому» / “They are called incomposite because”
   - *Verdict* (medium confidence; to the editor; second pass: confirmed): Here Vinet explains the name itself (veluti unum … melos … per unicum resonet intervallum), so his word should stand: ‘simple’. See V31.
   - *Recommended*: Простыми же они названы потому, что / They are called simple because
   - *Evidence*: latin-texts.json, V31, V34 [checked]

**Errors**

- **C** (ru; language; minor; second pass: confirmed): «звучит, однако, через один-единственный интервал». Calque of per unicum intervallum; in Russian «через интервал» suggests ‘after an interval (of time)’ or ‘skipping a step’. *Fix:* звучит, однако, одним-единственным интервалом

**Best base.** Russian: A (Exact, keeps ‘simple’ and melos, and «звучит … одним-единственным интервалом» is idiomatic.) English: A = C (Exact.)

**Notes to keep**

- A: An interval is incomposite not by its size but because no notes lie between its extremes in the genus concerned (the trihemitone in the chromatic, the ditone in the harmonic genus).
- C: Melos is used by Vinet only here (elsewhere modulatio); Xylander has cantilena; multis tonis is loose: two and three tones (ditone, tritone) are meant.

**For the editor**

- melos: «мелос»/‘melos’ (A, C), ‘melody’ (B) or «напев»/‘melodic strain’ (D)?


### V33 (Vinet 46.11–13)

> Caeterum alia praeter illa sunt intervalla, Dia-tessaron, Dia-pente, et Dia-pason nuncupata:

**Sense.** But besides those there are other intervals, called Dia-tessaron, Dia-pente and Dia-pason (the fourth, the fifth and the octave):

**Best base.** Russian: A = C (Both render Caeterum and keep the run-on into V34; names per convention.) English: A = C (Exact.)

**Notes to keep**

- C: Vinet hyphenates the Greek names (Dia-tessaron ‘through four’, Dia-pente ‘through five’, Dia-pason ‘through all [the strings]’); the literal sense is explained in V34.
- B: Unlike Xylander (quartam vulgo dicunt …), Vinet gives no vernacular Latin equivalents.


### V34 (Vinet 46.13–47.7)

> sed haec non sine medio, neque non-composita sunt, quando per media omnia supradicta intervalla incedunt: quae ratione quidem vocis primi soni ad ultimum, intervalla appellantur: sed a numero tamen ordineque, qui in medio sunt, sonorum, haec nomina receperunt: Dia-tessaron, ut quod per quatuor: Dia-pente, per quinque: Dia-pason per octo sonos aut chordas ingrediatur termineturque.

**Sense.** But these [the fourth, fifth and octave] are not without a middle, nor are they incomposite, since they proceed through all the intervals named above as intermediate ones. They are called 'intervals' by reason of the voice of the first note [in relation] to the last, but they received these names from the number and order of the notes that lie in the middle: Dia-tessaron as that which proceeds through four, Dia-pente through five, Dia-pason through eight notes or strings, and is bounded by them.

**Divergences**

1. *ratione quidem vocis primi soni ad ultimum*: Is ad ultimum the second term of a relation (ratio ... ad: 'the relation/ratio of the first note's voice to the last') or the end of a span ('the sound [running] from the first note to the last')?
   - **A, C, D**: A relation between the bounding notes: ratio ... primi soni ad ultimum (A, C: 'the ratio of the voice of the first note to the last'; D: 'the sound of the first note in relation to the last').
     - A: «по отношению голоса первого звука к последнему» / “by virtue of the ratio of the voice of the first note to the last”
     - C: «по отношению голоса первого звука к последнему» / “with respect to the ratio of the voice of the first note to the last”
     - D: «по звучанию первого звука в отношении к последнему» / “with reference to the sound of the first note in relation to the last”
   - **B**: A span: ratione = 'with reference to', vocis = 'the sounding', ad ultimum = 'to the last', with a 'from' supplied.
     - B: «по звучанию от первого звука до последнего» / “with reference to the sounding from the first note to the last”
   - *Verdict* (medium confidence; second pass: confirmed): The relation reading (A, C, D) keeps closer to the Latin as printed: Vinet has no a/ab before primi soni, and ratio ... ad is his normal construction for the relation of one note to another (V19 'quam ... rationem primus sonus ad octavum'; V22 'quomodo novem numerus ad octo'). B's span reading supplies a 'from' that the Latin lacks; it gives a defensible sense (Xylander's Latin, X29, has 'a primo sono ad extremum'), but that is the other version, not Vinet's wording. Russian «по отношению голоса первого звука к последнему» keeps both the word ratio and its 'respect' sense.
   - *Recommended*: интервалами они называются по отношению голоса первого звука к последнему / they are called intervals by reason of the relation of the voice of the first note to the last
   - *Evidence*: Vinet's own usage, V19 and V22 (latin-texts.json) [checked]; Xylander X29, 'ratione sonitus qui est a primo sono ad extremum' (latin-texts.json) [checked]; Lewis & Short s.v. ratio: I 'a reckoning, account, calculation'; II.B.1 'relation, reference, respect to a thing'; 'proportion' (Cic. Rep. 6.18) [checked]
2. *Dia-tessaron, ut quod per quatuor ... ingrediatur termineturque*: Whether the etymological explanation of the names is made visible in the running text (literal 'through four' beside the established name) or left to the note at V33.
   - **C**: Gives the established name and the literal sense side by side in the text, and keeps ut quod as 'as that which'.
     - C: «кварта — «через четыре», как [интервал], который проходит через четыре [звука] и ими ограничивается» / “the fourth, “through four”, as that which proceeds through four [notes] and is bounded by them”
   - **A, B, D**: Established name only; the literal sense is in the note at V33 (A keeps 'as that which', B renders ut quod as 'since', D as a plain statement).
     - A: «кварта — как идущая и завершающаяся через четыре» / “the fourth, as that which proceeds and terminates through four”
     - B: «Ведь кварта проходит через четыре, квинта — через пять, октава — через восемь звуков или струн и ими ограничена.» / “since the fourth extends through and is bounded by four notes or strings”
     - D: «Так, кварта проходит через четыре звука или струны и на том заканчивается» / “the fourth runs through four notes or strings and ends there”
   - *Verdict* (medium confidence; to the editor; second pass: confirmed): Both are within about.md § 4.1 (term and literal rendering side by side, or a note). Because the sentence exists to explain the names, C's solution reads best here; A, B, D are acceptable given their note at V33. Not an error either way.
   - *Recommended*: кварта — «через четыре», как [интервал], который проходит через четыре [звука] и ими ограничивается; квинта — «через пять»; октава — «через восемь» звуков или струн / the fourth, “through four”, as that which proceeds through four [notes] and is bounded by them; the fifth, “through five”; the octave, “through eight” notes or strings
   - *Evidence*: about.md § 4.1 [checked]

**Errors**

- **A** (both; language; minor; second pass: confirmed): «as that which proceeds and terminates through four / как идущая и завершающаяся через четыре». 'Terminates through four' / «завершающаяся через четыре» is unidiomatic: termineturque means 'is bounded (by them)', not 'ends through'. *Fix:* as that which proceeds through four [notes] and is bounded by them / как [интервал], проходящий через четыре [звука] и ими ограниченный
- **B** (both; sign; minor; second pass: confirmed): «neither without intervening steps / не лишены промежуточных ступеней». sine medio is 'without a middle'; 'steps/ступеней' is an explanatory addition not marked with brackets (D, with the same gloss, brackets it). *Fix:* not without a middle (or: intermediate [steps]); не лишены середины (или: промежуточных [ступеней])

**Best base.** Russian: C (Keeps ratio ... ad, ut quod, both verbs and the visible etymology in correct Russian.) English: C (Most complete and exact; D is a close, more fluent second but flattens ut quod.)

**Notes to keep**

- C: Vinet explains Dia-pason as per octo 'through eight', not 'through all' (διὰ πασῶν); Xylander keeps both (per omnes, hoc est, per octo); the verbs ingrediatur termineturque belong to all three members.
- A: The counts 4, 5, 8 include both bounding notes, although the text speaks of the notes 'in the middle' (3, 4, 7 steps).

**For the editor**

- Should the running text show the literal names ('through four' etc.) beside кварта/квинта/октава where Vinet explains the names (C), or leave them to a note (A, B, D)?


### V35 (Vinet 47.7–14)

> Dia-pason autem per octo dicitur, tanquam in Octachordo intervallis omnibus comprehensis completisque, et hinc rursus repetitis, quemadmodum ex ipsa liquet appellationum ratione. Nam Dia-pason cum Dia-tessaron, et Dia-pason cum Dia-pente, et bis Dia-pason posteriora intervalla vocantur.

**Sense.** Dia-pason is said [to be] 'through eight', as though all the intervals were comprised and completed in the octachord and from there repeated again, as is clear from the very rationale of the names. For the subsequent intervals are called Dia-pason with Dia-tessaron, Dia-pason with Dia-pente, and twice Dia-pason.

**Best base.** Russian: C (Keeps the 'as though' of tanquam («как бы») and the full sentence; A is equally exact but renders tanquam as a plain 'since'.) English: C = A (Both exact and complete; B and D are correct but slightly freer (B 'the way the intervals themselves are named').)

**Notes to keep**

- A: Greek διὰ πασῶν means 'through all [the strings]'; the argument here (all intervals completed in the octachord) explains that 'all', yet Vinet writes per octo.
- B: Vinet writes bis Dia-pason 'twice the octave' where Xylander has the single word disdiapason; spans of 11, 12 and 15 notes (10, 11, 14 steps) for the three compounds (B, D checks).


### V36 (Vinet 47.15–20)

> Consonat autem intervallum Dia-tessaron, et Dia-pente secundum paraphonum: Dia-pason vero, et Dia-pason cum Dia-tessaron, et Dia-pason cum Dia-pente, et bis Dia-pason secundum antiphonum.

**Sense.** The interval Dia-tessaron and Dia-pente are consonant according to the paraphonic [kind]; Dia-pason, Dia-pason with Dia-tessaron, Dia-pason with Dia-pente and twice Dia-pason according to the antiphonic.

**Divergences**

1. *secundum paraphonum ... secundum antiphonum*: paraphonum / antiphonum are Greek adjectives (τὸ παράφωνον, τὸ ἀντίφωνον) in Latin dress: render as abstract nouns ('paraphony', 'antiphony') or as substantivised adjectives ('the paraphonic [kind]')?
   - **A, B, D**: Abstract nouns: 'by/according to paraphony ... antiphony' (D's Russian adds «по виду»).
     - A: «по парафонии ... по антифонии» / “by paraphony ... by antiphony”
     - B: «по парафонии ... по антифонии» / “by paraphony ... by antiphony”
     - D: «по виду парафонии ... по виду антифонии» / “according to paraphony ... according to antiphony”
   - **C**: Substantivised adjectives with the noun supplied in brackets: 'according to the paraphonic [kind] ... the antiphonic'.
     - C: «по парафонному [типу] ... по антифонному» / “according to the paraphonic [kind] ... according to the antiphonic”
   - *Verdict* (medium confidence; to the editor; second pass: confirmed): Same sense in both. C keeps the grammatical form of the Latin (an adjective, as in Theon's κατὰ τὸ ἀντίφωνον / παράφωνον) and brackets the supplied noun; A, B, D give the readable abstract nouns, which are current in Russian and English writing on Greek harmonics. A terminological choice for the editor; whichever is chosen should be used in V37 too.
   - *Recommended*: консонируют по парафонному [типу] ... по антифонному / are consonant according to the paraphonic [kind] ... according to the antiphonic
   - *Evidence*: LSJ s.v. ἀντίφωνος III (τὸ ἀντίφωνον, concord in the octave, Arist. Pr. 918b30) and s.v. παράφωνος (I 'sounding beside'; II sounds midway between consonance and dissonance, Gaud. Harm. 8) [checked]; LSJ does not record the sense 'fourth and fifth' found in Theon; Theon of Smyrna, ed. Hiller, the Thrasyllan division of consonances into antiphonic and paraphonic [from memory]; Xylander X31, 'secundum id genus consonantiae quod Paraphonum Graeci dicunt' (latin-texts.json): the other version also reads the word as naming a kind of consonance [checked]

**Errors**

- **D** (ru; sign; minor; second pass: confirmed): «по виду парафонии ... по виду антифонии». «виду» is not in the Latin (secundum paraphonum) and is not bracketed; D's English has no such word. *Fix:* по парафонии ... по антифонии (или: по [виду] парафонии)

**Best base.** Russian: C (Exact, keeps intervallum and the adjectival form, supplied words bracketed; A is equally sound with the noun forms.) English: C = A (Both exact; choice depends on the term decision (paraphonic [kind] vs paraphony).)

**Notes to keep**

- A: The octave and a fourth (8:3) is counted a consonance here, as in Ptolemy, against the Pythagorean view reported by Boethius (II.27; Ptolemy's proof V.9).
- C: Paraphonum/antiphonum are untranslated Greek neuters (Xylander glosses quasi iuxta sonans / quasi contra sonans); the division is Theon's (octave, double octave antiphonic; fifth, fourth paraphonic), whereas in Gaudentius 'paraphonic' denotes intervals between consonance and dissonance (tritone, ditone). C's Theon page reference not verified.

**For the editor**

- Term: «парафония/антифония», 'paraphony/antiphony' (A, B, D) or «парафонный/антифонный [тип]», 'the paraphonic/antiphonic [kind]' (C)?


### V37 (Vinet 47.21–48.7)

> Differunt autem inter se Paraphonum et antiphonum, quod Paraphonum impari temporis spatio consonat, leniter quodammodo et apposite excipientibus inter se sonis, secundum proportiones et rationes aequabiliter: antiphonum vero aequo convenit temporis spacio, acuto cum gravi pariter consonante, ut octavo cum primo, undecimo cum quarto, duodecimo cum quinto, quintodecimo cum octavo, gravibus nimirum una cum acutis, aut acutis cum gravibus peraequa proportione simul ascendentibus vel descendentibus, in intensionibus remissionibusve.

**Sense.** The paraphonic and the antiphonic differ from each other in that the paraphonic is consonant in an unequal space of time, the notes taking one another up in a gentle, as it were, and fitting way, evenly, according to proportions and ratios; the antiphonic accords in an equal space of time, the high being consonant together with the low, as the eighth with the first, the eleventh with the fourth, the twelfth with the fifth, the fifteenth with the eighth: the low notes, that is, together with the high, or the high with the low, ascending or descending at the same time in an entirely equal proportion, in raisings or lowerings.

**Divergences**

1. *impari temporis spatio ... aequo ... temporis spacio*: Rendering of the two time phrases: literal 'in an unequal / equal space of time', or interpreted (plural 'unequal spans/intervals'; aequo as 'the same')?
   - **A, C**: Literal and parallel: singular 'an unequal / an equal space (span) of time'.
     - A: «в неравный промежуток времени ... в равный промежуток времени» / “in an unequal span of time ... in an equal span of time”
     - C: «в неравный промежуток времени ... в равный промежуток времени» / “in an unequal space of time ... in an equal space of time”
   - **B**: Plural for impari ('unequal spans'), literal 'equal span' for aequo.
     - B: «в неодинаковые промежутки времени ... в равный промежуток времени» / “over unequal spans of time ... over an equal span of time”
   - **D**: Plural for impari ('unequal intervals of time'); aequo interpreted as 'the same'.
