     - D: «в неодинаковые промежутки времени ... в один и тот же промежуток времени» / “at unequal intervals of time ... in the same interval of time”
   - *Verdict* (medium confidence; second pass: confirmed): A and C keep Vinet's exact, parallel wording (singular; aequus = 'equal'), leaving the sense, succession vs simultaneity, to the participles (excipientibus; pariter, simul), as the Latin does. B's plural is a slight interpretation; D's 'the same' turns aequus into idem and anticipates the explanation, and its English 'intervals of time' is unhappy in a text where 'interval' is a technical term. Neither changes the doctrine, so not counted as errors.
   - *Recommended*: в неравный промежуток времени ... в равный промежуток времени / in an unequal space of time ... in an equal space of time
   - *Evidence*: Lewis & Short s.v. aequus (equal) and s.v. par, pariter (B: 'at the same time, together') [from memory]

**Errors**

- **D** (en; omission; minor; second pass: modified): «In raisings or lowerings [of pitch] the low notes rise or fall together with the high, or the high with the low, at the same time and in exactly equal proportion.». nimirum is not rendered in English, and the clause that explains pariter becomes an independent statement. D's Russian «Именно так» renders nimirum in its classical sense ('doubtless, certainly, truly': Lewis & Short, Gaffiot s.v. nimirum [checked]) and is acceptable, though the humanist 'that is, namely' (A, B, C) fits this explanatory clause better. *Fix:* that is, the low notes together with the high, or the high with the low, rising or falling at the same time in exactly equal proportion, in raisings or lowerings

**Best base.** Russian: A = C (Both keep the ablative absolutes, nimirum and the literal time phrases in natural Russian; choice follows the term decision (парафония / парафонное).) English: A = C (Exact and complete; B is good and more fluent but slightly interpretive.)

**Notes to keep**

- A: Vinet understands the difference as succession (impari temporis spatio, excipientibus) vs simultaneous parallel motion (aequo spatio, pariter, simul); excipere 'to follow after' (L&S).
- C: By the list of V17 the four examples are proslambanomenos–mese, hypate diatonos–diatonos diezeugmene, hypate meson–nete diezeugmene, mese–nete hyperbolaeon: all octaves, although V36 named four antiphonic consonances (A adds that 8, 11, 12, 15 are the upper notes of the octave, octave and fourth, octave and fifth and double octave counted from 1).


### V38 (Vinet 48.8–11)

> Porro tonus et hemitonium et diesis per se ipsa considerata, principium quidem consonantiae sunt, sed nondum tamen consonantia.

**Sense.** Further, the tone, the semitone and the diesis, considered in themselves, are indeed the beginning of consonance, but not yet consonance.

**Best base.** Russian: A = C (Exact, keeps Porro and quidem ... tamen; B and D are equally correct but drop Porro (D) or reword.) English: A = C (Exact and complete.)

**Notes to keep**

- C: Theon has the same formula for the tone and diesis: ἀρχὴ μὲν συμφωνίας, οὔπω δὲ συμφωνία (C's Hiller page reference not verified by the reviewer).


### V39 (Vinet 48.12–20)

> Conficitur autem Dia-tessaron duobus tonis et hemitonio et modica praeterea toni parte, quae diesi minor, nomine vacat: Dia-pente vero tribus tonis et hemitonio: et Dia-pason sex tonis: ac Dia-pason cum Dia-tessaron, tonis octo et hemitonio: Dia-pason cum Dia-pente, novem tonis et hemitonio: bis Dia-pason tonis duodecim.

**Sense.** The fourth is made up of two tones and a semitone and, besides, a small part of a tone which, being smaller than the diesis, has no name; the fifth of three tones and a semitone; the octave of six tones; the octave with the fourth of eight tones and a semitone; the octave with the fifth of nine tones and a semitone; twice the octave of twelve tones.

**Best base.** Russian: A = C (Exact and complete, keeping the participial 'being smaller than the diesis'; B and D equally correct in shorter sentences.) English: A = C (Exact and complete.)

**Notes to keep**

- C: The tone-counting (fourth 2½, fifth 3½, octave 6) is Aristoxenian; the extra 'part of a tone smaller than the diesis' is inconsistent with it (it should reappear in the fifth and octave) and wrong by the ratios: 2½ tones of 9:8 = 509.78 cents exceed 4:3 = 498.04 cents; cf. Sectio canonis props. 14–15 (the octave is less than six tones; the fourth less than two tones and a semitone).
- B: The counts before the increment exceed 4:3, 3:2, 2:1, 8:3, 3:1, 4:1 by 11.73, 11.73, 23.46, 35.19, 35.19, 46.92 cents; Boethius' fourth is two tones and the limma 256:243.

**For the editor**

- Commentary: say once that the extra 'small part of a tone' contradicts both the Pythagorean ratios and Vinet's own round figures (and V55, where the fourth is two tones and a semitone).


### V40 (Vinet 48.21–25)

> Ratio autem intervalli Dia-tessaron, est sesquitertia: Dia-pente, sesquialtera: Dia-pason, duplex: Dia-pason cum Dia-tessaron duplex-superpartiens: Dia-pason cum Dia-pente, triplex:

**Sense.** The ratio of the interval Dia-tessaron is sesquitertian; of Dia-pente, sesquialter; of Dia-pason, duple; of Dia-pason with Dia-tessaron, duple-superpartient; of Dia-pason with Dia-pente, triple; [the sentence continues in V41–V42]

**Best base.** Russian: A = C (All four are correct and follow about.md § 4.3; A and C keep 'of the interval' and the continuing punctuation.) English: A = C (All four correct; A and C closest to the Latin.)

**Notes to keep**

- C: Duplex-superpartiens is a short form of Boethius' duplex superbipartiens (8:3: the greater contains the smaller twice and two thirds of it); the Pythagoreans (Boethius) excluded the octave and a fourth, Ptolemy admitted it; Vinet lists it without comment.
- A: Vinet uses the -plex forms (duplex, triplex, quadruplex) of the Boethian multiples; Xylander adds super bipartiens tertias (X36).


### V41 (Vinet 48.25–26)

> Bis Dia-pason, quadruplex:

**Sense.** of twice Dia-pason, quadruple; [the sentence continues with the numerical example in V42]

**Best base.** Russian: A = C (Correct, and the punctuation lets the sentence run on into V42 as the Latin does (B and D close it with a full stop, acceptable).) English: A = C (Correct and continuous with V42.)


### V42 (Vinet 48.26–49.6)

> ut exempli gratia in numeris, primus proponatur nobis terminus, numerus sex, cuius sesquitertius est plane octo: sesquialter, novem: duplex, duodecim: duplex-superpartiens, sedecim, sex bis continens, et duas eius partes: triplex, octodecim: quadruplex, viginti quatuor.

**Sense.** as, for example, in numbers: let the first term put before us be the number six, whose sesquitertian is plainly eight; sesquialter, nine; duple, twelve; duple-superpartient, sixteen, containing six twice and two parts [= two thirds] of it; triple, eighteen; quadruple, twenty-four.

**Divergences**

1. *sex bis continens, et duas eius partes*: duae partes: literal 'two parts of it' or the classical fraction idiom 'two thirds of it'?
   - **C**: The idiom: duae partes = two thirds (numerator given, denominator one greater understood).
     - C: «содержащее шесть дважды и [ещё] две трети его» / “containing six twice and two thirds of it [besides]”
   - **A, B, D**: Literal 'two parts of it' in the text; all three explain in a note or check that two thirds (= 4) is meant.
     - A: «содержащее шесть дважды и ещё две его части» / “which contains six twice and two parts of it”
     - B: «ибо оно содержит шесть дважды и ещё две его доли» / “for it contains six twice and two of its parts besides”
     - D: «поскольку заключает в себе шесть дважды и ещё две его части» / “containing six twice and two parts of it besides”
   - *Verdict* (high confidence; second pass: confirmed): All four understand the arithmetic (16 = 2 × 6 + 4). duae partes is the classical expression for 2/3, so C's 'two thirds' gives the sense of the Latin in the running text; the literal 'two parts' (A, B, D) is not wrong but leaves the English and Russian reader to guess what parts are meant, and the explanation then has to sit in a note. Prefer C, with the literal wording in a note.
   - *Recommended*: содержащее шесть дважды и [ещё] две трети его / containing six twice and two thirds of it [besides]
   - *Evidence*: Allen and Greenough § 135 e, note 2 ('When the denominator is but one greater than the numerator, the numerator only is given: two-thirds, duae partēs') [checked]; Boethius, De inst. arith. I (multiplex superpartiens: duplex superbipartiens, 8 : 3) [from memory]; Arithmetic: 16 − 12 = 4 = 2/3 × 6 [checked]

**Errors**

- **D** (ru; omission; minor; second pass: confirmed): «Тогда по отношению к нему восемь даёт сверхтретное [4:3]». plane ('plainly') is left out in the Russian (D's English has 'plainly'). *Fix:* сверхтретное [4:3] к нему — очевидно, восемь
- **D** (notes; note-claim; minor; second pass: confirmed): «The numerals read 4 and 3, 3 and 2, 2 and 1». The figure on p. 49 has five numerals, 4, 3, 2, 2, 1: the first pair carries only the 4 (no 3 on its lower line), as A, B and C report and the scan shows. *Fix:* The numerals are 4 (first pair), 3 and 2 (second pair), 2 and 1 (third pair).

**Text proposals and doubts**

- **A**, text issue: printed «uncaptioned diagram of six lines on p. 49 between III.10 and III.11»; proposal: Record the diagram in the description of the text; it is not represented in the segments.. *Verdict: sound*; second pass: confirmed. The scan shows three pairs of lines (AB over a line ending at F; LN over MO; SV over TX) divided into 4 and 3, 3 and 2, 2 and 1 equal parts, with the numerals 4, 3, 2, 2, 1; measured on the image the length ratios are ≈4:3, 3:2, 2:1. The text does not refer to it. A's description is accurate. *Evidence*: scan vinet-p49, figure between lines 49.6 and 49.7 [checked]
- **C**, text issue: printed «p. 49, between 49.6 and 49.7: three pairs of parallel lines (AB/GF with C D E, I K; LN/MO with P Y, R; SV/TX with Z), numerals 4, 3, 2, 2, 1»; proposal: Record the figure (it illustrates the ratios 4:3, 3:2, 2:1 after V42).. *Verdict: sound*; second pass: confirmed. Matches the scan; the same reversed sort stands at the second tick of AB and at the start of the lower line, so the alphabetic sequence (A B C D E F G) makes the reading 'D on the upper line, G expected on the lower' a reasonable observation. *Evidence*: scan vinet-p49 [checked]

**Best base.** Russian: C (Exact, with 'two thirds' and plane; A is equally exact but literal on duae partes.) English: C (Exact and clear; A a close second.)

**Notes to keep**

- C: duae partes = two thirds (Gaffiot s.v. pars; Boethius: with 'two parts' thirds are understood; bis ... duasque eius insuper partes); Xylander glosses the name here super bipartiens tertias.
- C: Description of the figure on p. 49 (three pairs of lines showing 4:3, 3:2, 2:1 as lengths; the text does not refer to it); A adds measured length ratios ≈1.34, 1.47, 1.98.

**For the editor**

- Should the figure on p. 49 be reproduced or described in the edition (A and C propose recording it)?


### V43 (Vinet 49.7–10)

> Verumtamen sciendum, rationes intervallorum, non ex sonorum, aut ex chordarum numero iudicari; sed ex intentione vocis a gravi ad acutius:

**Sense.** But it must be known that the ratios of intervals are judged not from the number of the notes or of the strings, but from the tension (straining) of the voice from low to higher:

**Divergences**

1. *ex intentione vocis a gravi ad acutius*: intentio (here; intensio in V45, V47): render by the physical word 'tension' (напряжение) or by its effect, 'rise (in pitch)' (повышение)?
   - **A, C**: 'Tension / напряжение' of the voice, kept consistently for intentio/intensio outside the pair intensio–remissio.
     - A: «по напряжению голоса от низкого к более высокому» / “from the tension of the voice from low to higher”
     - C: «по напряжению голоса от низкого к более высокому» / “from the tension of the voice from low to higher”
   - **B, D**: 'Rise (in pitch) / повышение' of the voice or sound.
     - B: «по повышению звучания от низкого к более высокому» / “by the rise in pitch of the sound from low to higher”
     - D: «по повышению голоса от низкого звучания к более высокому» / “by the rise of the voice from a low pitch to a higher one”
   - *Verdict* (medium confidence; to the editor; second pass: confirmed): Both are within the dictionary range and about.md § 4.5 leaves intensio on its own to context. intentio vocis is classical for the straining/raising of the voice (L&S s.v. intentio), and Boethius' intentio is the raising of pitch; 'tension' keeps Vinet's word and its link with the stretched string (and with the weights of V47), 'rise' states the effect, which a gravi ad acutius already expresses. Choose one and keep it through V43, V45 and V47. A slight preference for 'tension' (A, C), which does not duplicate a gravi ad acutius and keeps the word visible; B's 'sound' for vocis is a further small shift.
   - *Recommended*: по напряжению голоса от низкого к более высокому / from the tension of the voice from low to higher
   - *Evidence*: Lewis & Short s.v. intentio I ('a stretching out, straining, tension': vocis, Plin. 28.4.14; intentio aëris, Sen. Q. N. 2.6.3; opp. remissio, Gell. 18.10) [checked]; Lewis & Short s.v. intensio (A 'a stretching out, straining'; II 'the tune: gravis, media, acuta', Censor. 12) [checked]; Boethius, De institutione musica I.3 (intendi/laxari of pitch) [from memory]; about.md § 4.5 [checked]

**Text proposals and doubts**

- **A**, text issue: printed «intentio-|ne (49.9–10), against intensione (49.19; 50.6; 50.11; 50.22) and intensio (42.16)»; proposal: Keep intentione as printed; treat it as a spelling variant of intensione.. *Verdict: sound*; second pass: confirmed. The scan confirms intentio-|ne at 49.9–10 and intensione at 49.19. Both forms derive from intendo; intentio is the classical form, intensio the later one, and V45 restates V43 with intensione. No emendation is needed and both may be rendered by one word. (A's line references for V47 are slightly off: intensione stands at 50.7 and 50.10, not 50.6 and 50.11; 50.22 is right.) *Evidence*: scan vinet-p49, lines 9–10 and 19 [checked]; printed lines 50.7, 50.10, 50.22 (latin-texts.json) [checked]; Lewis & Short s.vv. intentio, intensio [checked]
- **B**, text issue: printed «ex intentio- / ne uocis»; proposal: Retain intentione.. *Verdict: sound*; second pass: confirmed. Same as A's proposal; the reading text already has intentione, and the scan confirms it. *Evidence*: scan vinet-p49 [checked]

**Best base.** Russian: A = C (Exact and keep intentio as one rendered term; D equally exact if 'rise' is chosen.) English: A = C (Exact; B reorders the sentence and weakens 'not from ... but from' to 'independently of'.)

**Notes to keep**

- C: intentio here, intensio elsewhere: both from intendo (classical vs later form); rendered by one word.

**For the editor**

- Term for intentio/intensio on its own (V43, V45, V47): «напряжение» / 'tension' (A, C) or «повышение» / 'rise in pitch' (B, D)?


### V44 (Vinet 49.10–18)

> quandoquidem intervallum Dia-pente, cuius est ratio sesquialtera, sesquialterum minime videtur ex ratione numeri sonorum, quo numero superat sonos, qui ipsum praecedunt. Nam primi soni quincuplex invenitur: secundi vero, duplum sesquialterum: tertii, superpartiens: quarti, sesquiquartum: nullius vero sesquialterum demonstrari potest.

**Sense.** since the interval Dia-pente, whose ratio is sesquialter, by no means appears sesquialter from the reckoning (ratio) of the number of the notes, the number by which it surpasses the notes that precede it. For it is found to be the quintuple of the first note, the duple-sesquialter of the second, the superpartient of the third, the sesquiquartan of the fourth; but it cannot be shown to be the sesquialter of any.

**Divergences**

1. *ex ratione numeri sonorum*: ratio here: 'reckoning, account' (as in ex sonorum numero iudicari, V43, and a sonorum numero, V45) or the technical 'ratio' (of the numbers of the notes), as in ratio sesquialtera in the same sentence?
   - **A, B, D**: Reckoning/count: 'on a reckoning by / by the count of / if judged by the number of the notes'.
     - A: «если считать по числу звуков» / “on a reckoning by the number of the notes”
     - B: «по счёту звуков» / “by the count of notes”
     - D: «если исходить из числа звуков» / “if judged by the number of the notes”
   - **C**: Technical ratio: 'from the ratio of the number of notes'.
     - C: «по отношению числа звуков» / “from the ratio of the number of notes”
   - *Verdict* (low confidence; to the editor; second pass: confirmed): Both readings give a coherent argument: the fifth note compared with notes 1–4 yields ratios of numbers (5:1, 5:2, 5:3, 5:4), none 3:2. The reckoning reading (A, B, D) is supported by the parallel phrases of V43 and V45, where only 'number' is in question; C's 'ratio' keeps the word Vinet repeats in this ratio-dense sentence, and its Russian «по отношению» is ambiguous enough to cover both. The Latin does not settle it.
   - *Recommended*: по отношению числа звуков (или: если считать по числу звуков) / on a reckoning by the number of the notes (or: from the ratio of the number of the notes)
   - *Evidence*: Lewis & Short s.v. ratio: I 'a reckoning, account, calculation'; II.B.1 'relation, reference, respect to a thing'; 'proportion' (Cic. Rep. 6.18) [checked]; Vinet V43 and V45 (latin-texts.json) [checked]

**Best base.** Russian: A (Exact, keeps quo numero superat literally and the genitive construction; C equally exact if the 'ratio' reading is preferred.) English: A = C (Exact and complete; B and D drop Nam but are otherwise correct.)

**Notes to keep**

- A: The 'interval of the fifth' is here the fifth note with its ordinal 5 compared with 1–4; new ratio names formed on the Boethian pattern: duplum sesquialterum 'duple sesquialter [5:2]' (Boethius duplex sesqualter), superpartiens 'superpartient [5:3]' (exactly superbipartiens tertias).

**For the editor**

- ratio in ex ratione numeri sonorum: 'reckoning' (A, B, D) or 'ratio' (C)?


### V45 (Vinet 49.19–20)

> Ab intensione itaque acuti, non a sonorum numero, sesquialterum censetur.

**Sense.** Therefore it is reckoned sesquialter from the tension (raising) of the high, not from the number of the notes.

**Divergences**

1. *Ab intensione ... acuti*: Same term question as V43 (tension vs rise); and whether acuti ('of the high') is rendered.
   - **A**: 'Tension of the high' (acuti as neuter substantive, no supplied noun).
     - A: «по напряжению высокого» / “from the tension of the high”
   - **C**: 'Tension of the high [note]', noun supplied in brackets.
     - C: «по напряжению высокого [звука]» / “from the tension of the high [note]”
   - **B, D**: 'Rise in pitch / повышение звука': acuti is absorbed into 'rise in pitch' (Russian: 'raising of the sound').
     - B: «по повышению звука» / “by the rise in pitch”
     - D: «по повышению звука» / “by the rise in pitch”
   - *Verdict* (medium confidence; to the editor; second pass: confirmed): The term choice follows V43. Within it, A and C render acuti; B and D merge it into 'rise in pitch', acceptable in English, but the Russian «повышению звука» replaces 'the high' by 'the sound', which is not in the Latin. C's bracketed [note] is the clearest; A's bare neuter is also defensible (cf. a gravi ad acutius, V43).
   - *Recommended*: по напряжению высокого [звука] / from the tension of the high [note]
   - *Evidence*: Vinet V43 a gravi ad acutius (latin-texts.json) [checked]; Lewis & Short s.v. intensio [from memory]

**Best base.** Russian: C (Renders intensio and acuti, supplied noun bracketed.) English: C (Exact and clear; A equally exact.)

**For the editor**

- Follows the V43 term decision for intensio.


### V46 (Vinet 49.20–50.2)

> Idem autem et in reliquis quoque rationibus nobis licebit considerare.

**Sense.** And we shall be able to consider the same in the remaining ratios too.

**Best base.** Russian: A = B (Both exact (future licebit, nobis kept); D adds «всех» and uses the present, C drops nobis: trivial.) English: A = C (Exact.)

**Notes to keep**

- C: The test does not generalise: note 4 against note 3 gives 4:3 and note 8 against note 4 gives 2:1, so the argument holds only for the fifth, the case Vinet chooses.


### V47 (Vinet 50.3–11)

> Inventae autem hae sunt intervallorum rationes et deprehensae, ex dispari chordarum vel longitudine vel crassitudine: vel ea, quae per torsionem claviculorum fit, intensione; aut, quod compertum magis, ex ponderum e nervis appensione: In iis vero quae inflata sonant, aut ex fistularum amplitudine, aut ex intensione spiritus ac remissione.

**Sense.** These ratios of the intervals were found and detected from the unequal length or thickness of the strings; or from that tension which is produced by the twisting of the pegs; or, what is more certainly established, from the hanging of weights from the strings; and in those [instruments] that sound by being blown, either from the width of the pipes or from the tension and slackening of the breath.

**Divergences**

1. *ex intensione spiritus ac remissione*: The pair intensio/remissio here governs the breath: 'tension and slackening' (keeping intensio = tension) or 'strengthening and weakening'? And does the convention 'raising and lowering' (about.md § 4.5, the pair of pitch) apply?
   - **A, C**: 'Tension and slackening of the breath', with a note that the convention concerns pitch, not breath.
     - A: «по напряжению и ослаблению дыхания» / “from the tension and slackening of the breath”
     - C: «по напряжению и ослаблению дыхания» / “from the tension and slackening of the breath”
   - **B, D**: 'Strengthening and weakening/slackening' of the breath (D's Russian: of the air stream), without a note on the convention.
     - B: «по усилению и ослаблению дыхания» / “by strengthening and weakening the breath”
     - D: «по усилению и ослаблению струи воздуха» / “from the strengthening and slackening of the breath”
   - *Verdict* (medium confidence; to the editor; second pass: confirmed): The convention is defined for the pair 'of pitch'; here the nouns describe the breath, so none of the four is at fault for not writing 'raising and lowering'. A and C keep intensio = 'tension' as everywhere in their version and explain the departure; B and D give a plausible contextual rendering but change their own word for intensio again (rise / tension / strengthening) without saying so. Prefer A and C; D's «струи воздуха» for spiritus is a further shift.
   - *Recommended*: по напряжению и ослаблению дыхания / from the tension and slackening of the breath
   - *Evidence*: about.md § 4.5 [checked]; Lewis & Short s.vv. intensio, remissio [from memory]
2. *ex fistularum amplitudine*: amplitudo: 'width (bore)' or 'size' of the pipes?
   - **A, C**: 'Width of the pipes'.
     - A: «по ширине трубок» / “from the width of the pipes”
     - C: «по ширине трубок» / “from the width of the pipes”
   - **B, D**: 'Size / dimensions of the pipes'.
     - B: «по величине трубок» / “from the dimensions of the pipes”
     - D: «по величине трубок» / “from the size of the pipes”
   - *Verdict* (medium confidence; second pass: confirmed): Both lie within the word's range (wide extent, width, breadth; size). 'Width' is its first sense and the usual sense with hollow things; 'size' is the safer, unspecific rendering D argues for in a doubt. Legitimate alternatives.
   - *Recommended*: по ширине трубок / from the width of the pipes
   - *Evidence*: Lewis & Short s.v. amplitudo I ('wide extent, breadth, width, amplitude, size, bulk') [checked]

**Errors**

- **D** (both; construal; minor; second pass: modified): «по их натяжению при повороте колков или, что достовернее известно, при подвешивании к струнам грузов / from the tension obtained by turning the tuning pegs, or, as is better established, by hanging weights from the strings». In the Latin the hanging of weights is a separate source of the ratios: after the relative clause has closed on intensione, the semicolon and the repeated ex (aut ... ex ponderum e nervis appensione, parallel to ex dispari ... longitudine and to ex fistularum amplitudine, not to per torsionem inside the relative clause) mark it as a new member of the list. D makes it a second way of producing the tension («при повороте колков или ... при подвешивании»; 'by turning the pegs or ... by hanging weights'). Physically weights do tension a string, so the doctrine is not falsified, but Vinet's point, that the weight experiment is a distinct and better-established method, is lost. *Fix:* или, что достовернее известно, по подвешиванию грузов к струнам / or, as is better established, from the hanging of weights from the strings
- **B** (both; sign; minor; second pass: confirmed): «В инструментах, звучащих от вдувания воздуха, их установили ... / In instruments that sound when blown, they were established ...». 'instruments', «воздуха» and the repeated verb ('they were established') are supplied without brackets. *Fix:* В [инструментах], звучащих от вдувания, — ... / In those [instruments] that sound when blown, ...
- **D** (both; sign; minor; second pass: confirmed): «У инструментов, звучащих, когда в них дуют ... / In instruments that sound when blown, they were found ...». 'instruments' and (in English) the verb 'they were found' are supplied without brackets. *Fix:* У [инструментов], звучащих от вдувания, — ... / In those [instruments] that sound when blown, ...

**Best base.** Russian: A = C (Exact, keep the four coordinated sources and bracket [instruments].) English: A = C (Exact and complete.)

**Notes to keep**

- C: Theon lists the same methods (length and thickness of strings, tension by turning the pegs, the hanging of weights 'more evidently', width of the bores and tension/slackening of the breath in wind instruments); chorda and nervus both 'string'. C's Hiller page reference not verified by the reviewer.
- A: Physics for the reader: on strings the ratios of V40 hold for lengths; with weights the pitch goes with the square root of the weight, so the octave would need weights 4:1, which the tradition of the Pythagoras story ignores (D gives the same: 9:4 for the fifth).

**For the editor**

- intensio spiritus ac remissio: «напряжение и ослабление дыхания» (A, C) or «усиление и ослабление» (B, D)? Should the convention 'raising and lowering' be extended to this pair?


### V48 (Vinet 50.12–13)

> Caput IV. De Systematis.

**Sense.** Chapter IV. On systems.

**Best base.** Russian: A = B = C = D (All identical and correct.) English: A = B = C = D (All correct.)


### V49 (Vinet 50.14–51.3)

> Systematum autem hoc quidem Tetrachordum est, quod omnes intervalli Dia-tessaron continet facultates: aliud vero Pentachordum, quod intervalli Dia-pente facultates complectitur: aliud Octachordum, quod totam intervalli Dia-pason vim continet: aliud deinceps Heckaedecachordum, bis Dia-pason universam comprehendens intensionem, duplicesque habens facultates: eas enim, quas modo diximus, atque intervallorum Dia-pason Dia-tessaron, et Dia-pason Dia-pente, et bis Dia-pason facultates in se continet.

**Sense.** Of the systems, one is the tetrachord, which contains all the powers (capacities) of the interval Dia-tessaron; another the pentachord, which embraces the powers of the interval Dia-pente; another the octachord, which contains the whole force of the interval Dia-pason; another, next, the sixteen-string [system] (Heckaedecachordum), comprising the whole compass (intensio) of twice Dia-pason and having double powers: for it contains in itself those we have just named and the powers of the intervals Dia-pason Dia-tessaron, Dia-pason Dia-pente and twice Dia-pason.

**Divergences**

1. *bis Dia-pason universam comprehendens intensionem*: intensio here denotes the whole pitch-span of the double octave: keep the version's word 'tension', or render the sense ('range, compass, rise')?
   - **A, C**: 'The entire tension of the double octave' (constant rendering of intensio; sense explained in a doubt or note).
     - A: «которая объемлет всё напряжение двойной октавы» / “comprising the entire tension of the double octave”
     - C: «охватывающая всё напряжение двойной октавы» / “comprising the entire tension of the double octave”
   - **B**: 'The full pitch range of the double octave'.
     - B: «охватывающая всю высотную протяжённость двойной октавы» / “which spans the full pitch range of the double octave”
   - **D**: 'The full rise of the double octave'.
     - D: «охватывающая весь подъём на двойную октаву» / “which spans the full rise of the double octave”
   - *Verdict* (medium confidence; to the editor; second pass: confirmed): All understand the same thing: the whole compass of pitch the system spans. 'The entire tension of the double octave' (A, C) keeps the word but is opaque to a modern reader in both languages; B's 'full pitch range' gives the sense clearly; D's 'rise' is consistent with D's V43/V45 but awkward («подъём на двойную октаву»). Under about.md § 4.1 (intelligible text, literal sense in a note) B's solution is preferable here, with 'tension' (intensio) in a note; if the editor insists on one word for intensio throughout, A/C stand.
   - *Recommended*: охватывающая весь звуковысотный объём двойной октавы (в примечании: букв. «всё напряжение», intensio) / comprising the whole pitch range of the double octave (note: literally 'the entire tension', intensio)
   - *Evidence*: Lewis & Short s.v. intensio (a stretching, straining; tension) [from memory]; about.md § 4.1 and § 4.5 [checked]

**Errors**

- **B** (en; term; minor; second pass: confirmed): «the octachord, which contains the entire capacity of the octave». vis is rendered 'capacity', the same word B uses for facultates in the same sentence, so Vinet's two words (facultates / vis) are merged; B's Russian keeps «силу». *Fix:* which contains the whole force of the octave

**Text proposals and doubts**

- **A**, text issue: printed «Dià-pason Dià-tessáron, et Dià-pason Dià-pénte (51.1–2), without cum»; proposal: Keep as printed.. *Verdict: sound*; second pass: confirmed. The scan of p. 51, lines 1–2, shows both names without cum. The juxtaposed form is the Greek way of naming the compounds and is intelligible as it stands; no emendation is needed, and the conventional 'octave and a fourth / fifth' renders it. *Evidence*: scan vinet-p51, lines 1–2 [checked]; about.md § 4.2 [checked]
- **B**, text issue: printed «Dià-pason Dià-tessáron, et Dià-pason / Dià-pénte»; proposal: Read both bare juxtapositions as compound interval names.. *Verdict: sound*; second pass: confirmed. Same evidence as A's proposal; the context (bis Dia-pason follows, and V35, V36, V40 list the same three compounds with cum) leaves no doubt. *Evidence*: scan vinet-p51 [checked]; Vinet V35, V36, V40 (latin-texts.json) [checked]

**Best base.** Russian: A (Exact, keeps facultates/vis apart and brackets [система]; for intensio adopt B's sense rendering if the editor agrees.) English: A = C (Exact and complete; B clearest on intensio but merges facultates and vis.)

**Notes to keep**

- A: facultates and vis are Vinet's two words for one notion, the 'power' of an interval; the double octave has 15 notes, the 'sixteen-string' system being two octachords with the common note counted twice (V18).
- C: Here Vinet writes Dia-pason Dia-tessaron, Dia-pason Dia-pente without cum; Xylander describes these systems by their number of strings and names the sixteen-string system descriptively.

**For the editor**

- intensio in 'bis Dia-pason universam comprehendens intensionem': keep 'tension' (A, C) or give the sense 'pitch range' (B)?


### V50 (Vinet 51.4–7)

> Differunt autem Systemata ab intervallis quae in ipsis sunt, quod illa in materia consistunt: intervalla vero a materia abstracta consideramus.

**Sense.** Systems differ from the intervals that are in them in that the former consist in matter, whereas intervals we consider abstracted from matter.

**Best base.** Russian: A = C (Exact; all four are correct.) English: A = C (Exact; B and D equally good.)


### V51 (Vinet 51.8–9)

> Caput V. De Harmonia.

**Sense.** Chapter V. On harmony.

**Best base.** Russian: A = B = C = D (All identical and correct.) English: A = B = C = D (All correct.)


### V52 (Vinet 51.10–15)

> Harmoniae porro proprium est Systemata illa componere aptareque ad aliquod genus modulationis, ut ad Diatonon, ad Chromaticon, et ad id quod ab ipsa harmonia cognominatum, Harmonicum dicimus.

**Sense.** It is, further, proper to harmony to compose those systems and fit them to some genus of melody (modulatio), as to the diatonic, to the chromatic, and to that which, named after harmony itself, we call harmonic.

**Best base.** Russian: A = C (Exact, keep porro and the participial cognominatum; B and D correct but drop porro.) English: A = C (Exact and complete.)

**Notes to keep**

- C: Diatonon and Chromaticon are Greek neuters (διάτονον, χρωματικόν), later Latin Chromaticum, Diatonum; Harmonicum is the genus modern literature calls enharmonic, rendered 'harmonic' by convention to keep the derivation from harmonia (also D).
- A: modulatio is Vinet's word for melody (Xylander: melodia).

**For the editor**

- Term for modulatio: «мелодия» / 'melody' (A, C, D) or «мелодическое движение» / 'melodic movement' (B)? (for the concordance)


### V53 (Vinet 51.15–20)

> Si igitur tonis plurimum harmonia peragitur, Diatonon sic vocatum efficitur genus: si Hemitoniis, Chromaticum: si vero diesibus fere harmonia continetur, conficitur, quam Harmonicam appellant modulationem.

**Sense.** If, then, harmony is carried through mostly by tones, the genus called Diatonon results; if by semitones, the chromatic; but if harmony is made up almost (for the most part) of dieses, there is produced what they call the harmonic melody (modulatio).

**Best base.** Russian: A = C (Exact, keep igitur and the parallel plurimum / fere; D drops igitur.) English: A = C (Exact and complete.)

**Notes to keep**

- C: In the tetrachords of V55–V59 two of the three intervals are tones (diatonic), semitones (chromatic), dieses (harmonic): hence 'for the most part'; cf. Theon on the diatonic genus as proceeding mostly by tones (page reference not verified by the reviewer).


### V54 (Vinet 51.21–52.1)

> Verumenimvero neque omnino tonis, quae ad Harmoniam pertinent, efferuntur, neque hemitoniis omnino, neque omnino diesibus: hoc enim fieri non potest:

**Sense.** But indeed the things that belong to harmony are not produced wholly in tones, nor wholly in semitones, nor wholly in dieses: for this cannot be done:

**Best base.** Russian: A = C (Exact; D turns 'are not produced' into 'cannot be expressed' and then repeats 'it is impossible', a small redundancy.) English: A = C (Exact, with the colon that leads into V55.)


### V55 (Vinet 52.1–6)

> quinimo, quoniam ex tribus intervallis Dia-tessaron intervallum confectum est, duobusque tonis et hemitonio comprehensum, necesse est, postquam duo prima intervalla imposueris, reliquum esse Hemitonium:

**Sense.** nay rather, since the interval Dia-tessaron is made of three intervals and is comprised by two tones and a semitone, it is necessary, once you have put in place the first two intervals, that the remaining one be a semitone:

**Best base.** Russian: A = C (Exact; keep quinimo as corrective «напротив»; B («Более того») and D («Ведь», then «Значит») are freer.) English: A = C (Exact and complete.)

**Notes to keep**

- A: Here the fourth is exactly two tones and a semitone, without the 'small part' of V39 (also B, D); with exact ratios the remainder after two tones is 256:243 (D).


### V56 (Vinet 52.6–8)

> atque hoc fuerit Dia-tessaron Diatonon cognominatum genus, ex Hemitonio et tonis constans duobus.

**Sense.** and this will be the genus of Dia-tessaron called Diatonon, consisting of a semitone and two tones.

**Best base.** Russian: A = C (Exact; all four correct.) English: A = C = D (Identical and exact.)

**Notes to keep**

- A: The usual order of the diatonic tetrachord from the lowest note is semitone, tone, tone (Boethius I.21); Vinet names the semitone first after treating it as the remainder (D).


### V57 (Vinet 52.9–11)

> Si vero duo sint hemitonia, reliquum fuerit unum ex confusione intervallum, Trihemitonium simplex:

**Sense.** But if there are two semitones, the remainder will be one interval [arising] from fusion, the simple trihemitone:

**Divergences**

1. *Trihemitonium simplex*: simplex: Vinet's word 'simple' (as in simplicia, V31–V32) or the field's term 'incomposite'?
   - **A, C, D**: 'Simple' (простой), with the sense 'incomposite' explained in a note (A, C) or by reference to V32 (D).
     - A: «простой трёхполутон» / “a simple trihemitone”
     - C: «простой трёхполутон» / “the simple trihemitone”
     - D: «простой трёхполутон» / “a simple trihemitone”
   - **B**: 'Incomposite' (несоставной), as B renders simplicia throughout (V31–V32).
     - B: «несоставной трёхполутон» / “an incomposite trihemitone”
   - *Verdict* (medium confidence; to the editor; second pass: confirmed): Both mean the same interval and B is consistent within its version. But Vinet has two words, non-compositum (V34) and simplex (V31, V32, V57), and in V32 the name itself is explained ('Simplicia autem ideo dicta sunt, quod ...'), which only works with 'simple'; about.md § 4.2 fixes составной/несоставной for (in)compositum, not for simplex. 'Simple' with a note (A, C, D) keeps Vinet's distinction visible; B's choice is defensible under § 4.1 but erases it.
   - *Recommended*: простой трёхполутон / the simple trihemitone
   - *Evidence*: Vinet V31, V32, V34 (latin-texts.json and group-3 context) [checked]; about.md § 4.1–4.2 [checked]; Lewis & Short s.v. simplex [from memory]

**Errors**

- **B** (both; sign; minor; second pass: confirmed): «полученный слиянием / formed by merging». The participle supplied with ex confusione is not bracketed (A and C bracket it). *Fix:* [полученный] слиянием / [formed] by merging
- **D** (both; sign; minor; second pass: confirmed): «возникший от слияния / formed by blending». The participle supplied with ex confusione is not bracketed. *Fix:* [возникший] от слияния / [formed] by blending

**Best base.** Russian: A = C (Exact, supplied participle bracketed, Vinet's simplex kept.) English: A = C (Exact and correctly marked.)

**Notes to keep**

- C: ex confusione 'by fusion' refers back to V32 (coniunctis confusisque); simplex here, as there, means 'incomposite' (cf. Theon's τριημιτόνιον ἀσύνθετον; page reference not verified by the reviewer).

**For the editor**

- simplex / simplicia: «простой» / 'simple' (A, C, D) or «несоставной» / 'incomposite' (B)? Decide together with V31–V32.


### V58 (Vinet 52.11–14)

> eritque id Dia-tessaron genus Chromaticum, ex Hemitoniis duobus et Trihemitonio temperatum, quod priore moestius luctuosiusque est.

**Sense.** and this will be the chromatic genus of Dia-tessaron, blended (tempered) of two semitones and a trihemitone, which is sadder and more mournful than the former.

**Best base.** Russian: C («соразмерно составленный» renders temperatum ('mixed in due measure') best; A («слаженный») equally good.) English: C = A (Exact; A's 'tempered' is literal but needs its note against the later sense of temperament.)

**Notes to keep**

- A: temperatum 'mixed in due measure, attuned', not the later 'temperament'; same verb in V60.
- C: Cf. Theon on the chromatic as 'more mournful and more emotional' (γοερώτερόν τε καὶ παθητικώτερον); page reference not verified by the reviewer.


### V59 (Vinet 52.14–20)

> Si vero per duas dieses modulatio peragatur, quod reliquum est intervallum, erit unum ditonium ex confusione, ac Dia-tessaron hoc genus Harmonicum nominatur, quod sit optimum, ab ipsa communi omnium harmonia cognominatum.

**Sense.** But if the melody (modulatio) proceeds through two dieses, the interval that remains will be one ditone by fusion, and this genus of Dia-tessaron is named harmonic, on the ground that it is the best, being named after harmony itself, common to all.

**Divergences**

1. *ab ipsa communi omnium harmonia*: omnium has no noun: 'common to all [the genera]', 'common to all things', or left open?
   - **A**: Left open: 'common to all' (doubt prefers the genera).
     - A: «по самой гармонии, общей для всех» / “after harmony itself, which is common to all”
   - **B, D**: All [the genera], supplied in brackets.
     - B: «от самой гармонии, общей для всех [родов]» / “from harmony itself, which is common to all [the genera]”
     - D: «от самой гармонии, общей для всех [родов]» / “from harmony itself, which all [the genera] share”
   - **C**: Neuter: 'common to all things' (harmony of the universe, as in chapter I).
     - C: «от самой гармонии, общей для всего» / “after the very harmony common to all things”
   - *Verdict* (low confidence; to the editor; second pass: confirmed): The Latin does not decide: omnium may be the genera just listed (all three are 'harmony' in the sense of V52) or all things (chapter I). The bracketed '[the genera]' of B and D is a reasonable contextual supplement, C's neuter a reasonable alternative; A's unbracketed 'common to all' keeps the ambiguity of the Latin, which is the safest course in the running text, with the options in a note.
   - *Recommended*: от самой гармонии, общей для всех (в примечании: [родов] или «всего сущего») / after harmony itself, common to all (note: [the genera] or 'all things')
   - *Evidence*: Vinet V05–V08 and V52 (latin-texts.json) [checked]; Allen and Greenough § 288 (adjectives used substantively: omnium neuter or masculine) [from memory]
2. *Harmonicum nominatur, quod sit optimum*: quod sit optimum: which verb does the causal clause qualify (nominatur, after which it stands, or cognominatum), and is the subjunctive rendered as the reason alleged by those who name the genus?
   - **C, D**: The quod-clause gives the reason for nominatur ('is called harmonic on the ground that it is the best'), with cognominatum as an appositive participle. The alleged reason is marked in C's English and in D's English and Russian («потому что считается наилучшим»); C's Russian «как наилучший» is neutral.
     - C: «называется гармоническим как наилучший» / “is called harmonic on the ground that it is the best”
     - D: «носит имя гармонического, потому что считается наилучшим» / “is called harmonic on the ground that it is the best”
   - **A, B**: The clause is attached to cognominatum and stated as fact: 'because it is the best, it has been named / takes its name after harmony itself' (A's Russian «как наилучший» is neutral; B states it as fact in both languages).
     - A: «как наилучший, он назван по самой гармонии» / “because it is the best, it has been named after harmony itself”
     - B: «Он наилучший и потому получил своё имя от самой гармонии» / “Because it is the best, it takes its name from harmony itself”
   - *Verdict* (medium confidence; second pass: modified): The Latin order and punctuation (nominatur, quod sit optimum, ... cognominatum) attach the clause to nominatur, and quod with the subjunctive gives the reason as that of the namers (A&G § 540): C's English and D render this. A and B restructure the sentence and state the reason as Vinet's own; the doctrinal difference is slight (Vinet does not dissent), so not counted as an error. In Russian the neutral «как наилучший» (A, C) loses the nuance; «на том основании, что он наилучший» or D's «потому что считается наилучшим» keeps it.
   - *Recommended*: называется гармоническим на том основании, что он наилучший, получив прозвание от самой гармонии / is called harmonic on the ground that it is the best, being named after harmony itself
   - *Evidence*: Allen and Greenough § 540 (quod and quia take the subjunctive 'when the reason is given on the authority of another') [checked]

**Errors**

- **B** (both; sign; minor; second pass: confirmed): «образованным слиянием / formed by merging». Supplied participle with ex confusione not bracketed (as in V57). *Fix:* [образованным] слиянием / [formed] by merging
- **D** (both; sign; minor; second pass: confirmed): «возникший от слияния / formed by blending». Supplied participle with ex confusione not bracketed (as in V57). *Fix:* [возникший] от слияния / [formed] by blending

**Text proposals and doubts**

- **B**, doubt: printed «communi omnium harmonia»; proposal: получил своё имя от самой гармонии, общей для всех [вещей] / takes its name from harmony itself, which is common to all [things]. *Verdict: open* (to the editor); second pass: confirmed. Grammatically possible (omnium neuter) and supported by chapter I; equally possible is the genera (B's own choice). See the divergence. *Evidence*: Vinet V05–V08, V52 (latin-texts.json) [checked]
- **C**, doubt: printed «communi omnium harmonia»; proposal: от самой гармонии, общей для всех / named after the very harmony common to all. *Verdict: open* (to the editor); second pass: confirmed. The unspecified 'common to all' keeps the Latin ambiguity; the reviewer recommends it for the running text. *Evidence*: Vinet V05–V08, V52 (latin-texts.json) [checked]
- **D**, doubt: printed «ab ipsa communi omnium harmonia»; proposal: получив имя от самой гармонии, общей всему сущему / taking its name from harmony itself, common to all things. *Verdict: open* (to the editor); second pass: confirmed. Same question as B's and C's doubts. *Evidence*: Vinet V05–V08, V52 (latin-texts.json) [checked]

**Best base.** Russian: A = C (Exact, with the supplied participle bracketed; A keeps omnium open. Neither A nor C marks the alleged reason in Russian («как наилучший»); D's «потому что считается наилучшим» does.) English: C = D (Render quod sit as an alleged reason; omnium to be decided by the editor.)

**Notes to keep**

- A: Boethius calls the enharmonic 'optime atque apte coniunctum' (I.21): the 'best' genus.
- C: quod sit optimum: subjunctive of alleged reason (A&G § 540); cf. Theon citing Aristoxenus: the genus is called harmonia because it is the best (page reference not verified by the reviewer).

**For the editor**

- omnium: leave 'common to all' open (A), supply [the genera] (B, D), or 'all things' (C)?


### V60 (Vinet 52.21–22)

> Eodem modo et caetera quoque systemata constituit harmonia, temperatque.

**Sense.** In the same way harmony also constitutes and tempers (proportions) the other systems.

**Best base.** Russian: A = C (Exact, keep et ... quoque; A's «слаживает» and C's «соразмеряет» each match their rendering of temperatum in V58.) English: A = C (Exact; B and D leave out 'also'.)

**For the editor**

- temperatum (V58) / temperat (V60): B («составленный» / «соразмеряет») and D («образованный сочетанием» / «соразмеряет») render the same verb differently; A and C keep one rendering. For the concordance.


### V61 (Vinet 52.23–27)

> Ex proposito enim exemplo, quo ostensum in uno tetrachordo, quomodo intervalla intervalli Dia-tessaron constituantur, facile studiosus alia systemata quomodo componantur, assequi poterit.

**Sense.** For from the example set forth, by which it has been shown in one tetrachord how the intervals of the interval Dia-tessaron are constituted, the studious [reader] will easily be able to grasp how the other systems are composed.

**Errors**

- **D** (both; omission; minor; second pass: confirmed): «Предложенный пример показывает ... / From the example just given, ...». enim ('for'), which presents this sentence as the reason for V60, is not rendered (D drops such connectives elsewhere too: Porro V38, Nam V44, igitur V53). *Fix:* Ведь из предложенного примера ... / For from the example given ...

**Best base.** Russian: A = C (Exact, with the supplied [читатель] bracketed.) English: A = C (Exact; B is good and more fluent.)


### V62 (Vinet 53.1–4)

> Genus autem Harmonicum est modulatu difficillimum, meditatione multa exercitationeque indigens: unde eius est usus rarior.

**Sense.** The harmonic genus is the most difficult to perform in melody (modulatu), needing much practice and exercise; hence its use is rarer.

**Best base.** Russian: A = C (Exact; A keeps the melodic sense of modulatu, C is simpler; all four correct.) English: A = C (Exact.)

**Notes to keep**

- C: modulatu difficillimum: supine in -u with an adjective (A&G § 510); meditatio 'preparation, practice' (Cic. Div. 2.96, meditatio atque exercitatio); cf. Theon: the enharmonic is the hardest to sing and needs much habituation (page reference not verified by the reviewer).


### V63 (Vinet 53.4–6)

> Diatonum vero est simplum et generosum et magis naturale: quare id et Plato admittit.

**Sense.** The diatonic, however, is simple, noble and more natural; therefore Plato too admits it.

**Best base.** Russian: A = C (Exact; D brackets [род], equally correct.) English: A = C (Exact.)

**Notes to keep**

- A: simplum is from the rare simplus, distinct from simplex (Vinet's word for the incomposite intervals, V31); Diatonum beside Diatonon (V52–V56).
- C: Theon gives the same judgement (the diatonic simple, noble, more natural; hence Plato adopts it), with the Timaeus' diatonic division (35b–36b) in view; the Republic (398e–399c) concerns modes, not genera.


### V64 (Vinet 53.7–14)

> Horum autem unumquodque rursus variatur, et in multas divisiones et differentias et genera modulationum distribuitur: et modo Lydius, modo Phrygius, modo Dorius appellatur modulus: a quorum quolibet Paeeon, Hymenaeus, Exodius, et quotquot sunt modulationum genera, existunt.

**Sense.** Each of these [genera], in turn, is varied and distributed into many divisions and differences and kinds (genera) of melodies; and the mode (modulus) is called now Lydian, now Phrygian, now Dorian; from any of which arise the paean, the hymenaeus, the exodius, and all the kinds of melody there are.

**Divergences**

1. *Paeeon, Hymenaeus, Exodius*: Song names: transliterate (with a note) or describe in the running text?
   - **A, C**: Transliteration of all three, explained in a note.
     - A: «пеан, гименей, эксодий» / “the paean, the hymenaeus, the exodius”
     - C: «пеан, гименей, эксодий» / “the paean, the hymenaeus, the exodius”
   - **B**: Paean and hymenaeus transliterated; Exodius described as an exit/closing song.
     - B: «пеан, гименей, заключительная песнь» / “the paean, the hymenaeus, the exit song”
   - **D**: Paean kept; Hymenaeus and Exodius described ('wedding song', 'song of departure').
     - D: «пеан, свадебная песнь, прощальная песнь» / “the paean, the wedding song, the song of departure”
   - *Verdict* (medium confidence; to the editor; second pass: modified): «пеан, гименей» / 'paean, hymenaeus' are established words, so describing the hymenaeus (D) is unnecessary though not wrong. For Exodius there is no established modern term: either the transliteration with a note (A, C) or a plain description (B) is within about.md § 4.1. B's 'exit/closing song' matches the dictionaries: LSJ gives ἐξόδιος 'of or belonging to an exit', τὸ ἐξόδιον 'finale of a tragedy', and Latin exodium 'after-piece', transferred 'conclusion, end'. D's «прощальная песнь» ('farewell song') narrows the sense toward leave-taking. LSJ records no funeral sense for ἐξόδιος (only 'departure, death' for ἔξοδος); D's funerary alternative (in its doubt) rests on later Byzantine liturgical usage, which the Latin does not suggest. The choice between A/C and B is the editor's.
   - *Recommended*: пеан, гименей, эксодий (в примечании: «заключительная песнь») / the paean, the hymenaeus, the exodius (note: 'exit/closing song')
   - *Evidence*: Lewis & Short s.v. exodium (comic after-piece; transf. 'a conclusion, end') [checked]; LSJ s.v. ἐξόδιος (of or belonging to an exit; ἐξόδιον, finale of a tragedy; Lat. exodium, after-piece; no funeral sense) [checked]; LSJ s.v. ἔξοδος III.1 ('departure, death') [checked]; about.md § 4.1 [checked]

**Text proposals and doubts**

- **A**, text issue: printed «Pæéon (53.12)»; proposal: Keep; understand Paean.. *Verdict: sound*; second pass: confirmed. The scan of p. 53, line 12, reads Pæéon; normalised Paeeon, it transliterates the epic form Παιήων (ae for αι, e for η), with the printer's accent on é as elsewhere (Hypáte, Diátonum). Not a misprint; the sense is the paean. *Evidence*: scan vinet-p53, line 12 (Pæéon) [checked]; LSJ s.v. Παιάν ('Ep. Παιήων') [checked]
- **C**, text issue: printed «Pæéon»; proposal: Keep the reading Paeeon.. *Verdict: sound*; second pass: confirmed. Same evidence as A's proposal; C's argument from the epic form is correct and the translation (пеан / paean) is unaffected. *Evidence*: scan vinet-p53, line 12 (Pæéon) [checked]; LSJ s.v. Παιάν ('Ep. Παιήων') [checked]
- **D**, doubt: printed «Exodius»; proposal: погребальная песнь / funeral song. *Verdict: unsound*; second pass: modified. Nothing in Vinet's Latin, which lists kinds of song beside the paean and the hymenaeus, points to a burial song. The dictionaries give the 'exit' sense: Latin exodium is an after-piece or conclusion, and LSJ s.v. ἐξόδιος has 'of an exit', 'finale', with no funeral sense. The funerary use D cites (a Holy Saturday canon) is later Byzantine liturgical Greek. The broad 'exit/closing song' should stay. *Evidence*: Lewis & Short s.v. exodium [checked]; LSJ s.v. ἐξόδιος [checked]; LSJ s.v. ἔξοδος III.1 [checked]

**Best base.** Russian: A = C (Exact, with [родов] supplied (A) and the established song names; B a good alternative if Exodius is to be described.) English: A = C (Exact and complete.)

**Notes to keep**

- C: modulus 'mode' (Gaffiot: mesure, mode, mélodie; cf. Pliny's moduli Lydii, Dorii, Phrygii); genera modulationum are kinds of melody, not the three genera; Paeeon = paean, Hymenaeus wedding song, Exodius (masc. agreeing with them; the dictionary word is exodium) closing song; Xylander has cantilena for modulus.

**For the editor**

- Exodius: «эксодий» / 'exodius' with a note (A, C) or «заключительная песнь» / 'exit (closing) song' (B)?


### V65 (Vinet 53.14–20)

> Non enim sola intervallorum mutatione, neque sola eorum differentia: sed etiam lentioribus, et concitatis transitionibus, pausis quoque et fluxibus, atque variis tactibus pulsibusque, pro vario manus temperamento, infinitae et diversae modulationes conficiuntur.

**Sense.** For it is not by the change of intervals alone, nor by their difference alone, but also by slower and quick transitions, by pauses too and flowings, and by various touches and strokes, according to the varying regulation (due measure) of the hand, that infinite and diverse melodies are produced.

**Errors**

- **B** (en; omission; minor; second pass: confirmed): «Countless and varied forms of melodic movement are produced by changes and differences of interval, and also by slower ...». Non ... sola ... neque sola ... sed etiam ('not by ... alone, nor by ... alone, but also') is flattened to a plain list, and enim is dropped; B's Russian keeps «не только ... но также». *Fix:* For countless and varied forms of melodic movement are produced not by the change of intervals alone, nor by their difference alone, but also by slower ...
- **D** (en; omission; minor; second pass: confirmed): «are produced by changes of interval and by differences between intervals, but also by slower and more animated transitions». sola ... sola ('alone') is not rendered, so 'but also' has no 'not only' to answer it; D's Russian («Не только ...») is right. *Fix:* are produced not by changes of interval alone, nor by differences between intervals alone, but also by ...

**Best base.** Russian: A = C (Exact, keep the structure non sola ... neque sola ... sed etiam and bracket the supplied [движения].) English: A (Exact and keeps comparative + positive (slower and rapid); C equally exact but 'the varied measure of the hand' is opaque.)

**Notes to keep**

- A: The means of melody besides the intervals: speed of transitions, rests (pausae) and continuous movement (fluxus), ways of touching and striking (tactus, pulsus); temperamentum 'due measure', how the hand proportions its movements.


### V66 (Vinet 53.21)

> Finis Musicae.

**Sense.** End of the Music.

**Best base.** Russian: A = C = D (Identical and correct; B's bracketed [раздела] is also acceptable.) English: A = C = D (Correct.)


## Terms

For each term: the segments where it occurs, the four renderings, the recommended Russian and English forms, the reason and the evidence. "To the editor" marks a choice that remains open.


### sonus (both)

Segments: X08, X09, X12, X13, X14, X15, X16, X17, X29, X32, X37, X38, X39, V10, V11, V12, V15, V16, V17, V19, V20, V34, V37, V43, V44, V45

| | Russian | English |
|---|---|---|
| A | звук | note |
| B | звук | note |
| C | звук | note |
| D | звук | note |
| **Recommended** | **звук** | **note** |

Fixed by about.md § 4.5; all four follow it in both versions.

*Evidence*: about.md § 4.5; Boethius, De inst. mus. I.8, ed. Friedlein p. 195 (definition of sonus) [checked in the X08 review]


### sonitus (xylander)

Segments: X29, X37

| | Russian | English |
|---|---|---|
| A | звучание | sound |
| B | звучание | sounding |
| C | звучание | sounding |
| D | звучание | sound |
| **Recommended** | **звучание** | **sound** |

Here not a single note but the sound that extends from the first note to the last (X29 ratione sonitus qui est a primo sono ad extremum; X37 sonitus ipsius intensionem). The § 4.5 equation sonitus = note applies to sonitus 'as a musical note', so all four rightly keep it apart from sonus. Vinet has vox in the parallel places (V34, V43).

*Evidence*: about.md § 4.5 (the table limits sonus, sonitus = note to 'a musical note'); Lewis & Short s.v. sonitus, 'a sound, noise' [from memory]


### vox (both)

Segments: X08, V11, V34, V43

| | Russian | English |
|---|---|---|
| A | голос | voice |
| B | голосовое звучание (X08, V11); звучание (V34, V43) | vocal sound (X08, V11); sounding (V34, V43) |
| C | голос | voice |
| D | голос (X08, V11, V43); звучание (V34) | voice (X08, V11, V43); sound (V34) |
| **Recommended** | **голос** | **voice** |

In the definition of the note (X08, V11) vox is Boethius' vox ('Sonus ... est vocis casus emmeles ... in unam intensionem'), the sounding voice whose pitch is a note; 'voice' keeps the Latin and its Boethian model. V34 (ratione vocis primi soni) and V43 (intentione vocis) are the same word in Vinet and should keep the same rendering. B's «голосовое звучание» is a gloss rather than a rendering; D switches to «звучание» / 'sound' in V34 only.

*Evidence*: Boethius, De inst. mus. I.8, ed. Friedlein p. 195 [checked in the X08 review]; Lewis & Short s.v. vox I, 'a voice, sound, tone' [from memory]


### intensio, intentio (on its own, not in the pair with remissio) (both) (to the editor)

Segments: X08, X37, X39, V11, V43, V45, V47, V49

| | Russian | English |
|---|---|---|
| A | напряжение | tension |
| B | высота (X08, V11); повышение (X37, X39, V43, V45); натяжение (V47, pegs); высотная протяжённость (V49) | pitch (X08, V11); rise in pitch (X37, X39, V43, V45); tension (V47); pitch range (V49) |
| C | напряжение | tension |
| D | высота (X08, V11); повышение (X37, X39, V43, V45); натяжение (V47, pegs); подъём (V49) | pitch (X08, V11); rise in pitch (X37, X39, V43, V45); tension (V47); rise (V49) |
| **Recommended** | **напряжение (at the first occurrence a note: 'высота звука', Greek τάσις)** | **tension (at the first occurrence a note: 'pitch', Greek τάσις)** |

about.md § 4.5 leaves intensio on its own to the translator but asks for one rendering per version. 'Tension' / «напряжение» is the only rendering that serves every place: the definition of the note (X08, V11, Boethius' in unam intensionem), the rise from low to high by which ratios are judged (X37, X39, V43, V45), the tightening of the pegs (V47, where Vinet uses the same word for the string and for the pitch) and the compass of the double octave (V49). B and D change their word five times. The segment reviews accepted 'pitch' as legitimate in the definitions and preferred 'pitch range' at V49 (V11, V49); if the editor adopts 'pitch' there, the single rendering is lost and a note must say so. With 'tension' for intensio, Xylander's gloss tenor is best 'pitch' (X08).

*Evidence*: about.md § 4.5; Lewis & Short s.v. intensio II, 'the tune: gravis, media, acuta', Censorinus 12 [checked in the X08 review]; Boethius, De inst. mus. I.8, ed. Friedlein p. 195, 'in unam intensionem' [checked in the X08 review]; Lewis & Short s.v. intentio (intentio vocis) [checked in the V43 review]


### intensiones remissionesve (the pair, of pitch) (both)

Segments: X32, V37

| | Russian | English |
|---|---|---|
| A | повышения или понижения | raisings or lowerings |
| B | повышения или понижения | X32: 'by equal changes in pitch'; V37: 'as the pitch is raised or lowered' |
| C | повышения или понижения | raisings or lowerings |
| D | повышения или понижения | raisings or lowerings [of pitch] |
| **Recommended** | **повышения или понижения** | **raisings or lowerings** |

Fixed by about.md § 4.5 for the pair of pitch. A, C and D follow it in both languages; B's Russian follows it, its English collapses the pair at X32 (recorded as an error) and paraphrases it at V37.

*Evidence*: about.md § 4.5


### intensio et remissio spiritus (X41); intensio spiritus ac remissio (V47) (both)

Segments: X41, V47

| | Russian | English |
|---|---|---|
| A | напряжение и ослабление дыхания | tension and relaxation (X41) / slackening (V47) of the breath |
| B | усиление и ослабление дыхания | strengthening and weakening of the breath |
| C | напряжение и ослабление дыхания | tension and slackening of the breath |
| D | усиление и ослабление струи воздуха | strengthening and slackening of the breath |
| **Recommended** | **напряжение и ослабление дыхания** | **tension and slackening of the breath** |

The pair here governs the breath, not the pitch, so the § 4.5 'raising and lowering' is not required (V47 review). Keeping intensio = 'tension' follows the version's single rendering (see intensio). D's «струя воздуха» interprets spiritus, which is simply the breath.

*Evidence*: about.md § 4.5; segment review V47; Lewis & Short s.v. spiritus, 'a breathing, breath' [from memory]


### tenor (xylander)

Segments: X08

| | Russian | English |
|---|---|---|
| A | удержание | sustained holding |
| B | выдерживаемый тон | sustained tone |
| C | высота | pitch |
| D | ровное звучание | steady tone |
| **Recommended** | **высота** | **pitch** |

Xylander's gloss on intensio (intensio seu tenor). Tenor is 'an uninterrupted holding, course' and in Quintilian the tone of the voice; as a gloss on intensio rendered 'tension' it names the pitch level (C). A's «удержание голоса» is unidiomatic (it suggests holding the voice back).

*Evidence*: Lewis & Short s.v. tenor I [checked in the X08 review]; segment review X08


### acutus / gravis; acuitas (X39) (both)

Segments: X14, X18, X32, X37, X39, V17, V22, V37, V43, V45

| | Russian | English |

| A | высокий / низкий; высота | high / low; high pitch |
| B | высокий / низкий; acuitas absorbed into «повышение звука» | high / low; acuitas absorbed into 'rise in pitch' |
| C | высокий / низкий; высота | high / low; acuteness |
| D | высокий / низкий; acuitas absorbed | high / low; acuitas absorbed into 'rise in pitch' |
| **Recommended** | **высокий / низкий; acuitas: высота** | **high / low; acuitas: acuteness** |

acutus / gravis fixed by about.md § 4.5; all follow. Acuitas (ad intensionem acuitatis, X39) should be rendered, not absorbed, and kept distinct from intensio: «напряжение высоты» / 'the tension of acuteness'.

*Evidence*: about.md § 4.5; segment review X39

### ratio (both)

Segments: X16, X18, X19, X20, X29, X32, X38, X52, V19, V24, V34, V35, V37, V43, V44, V46, V47

| | Russian | English |

| B | отношение; by context | ratio; by context |
| C | отношение; V44 also «по отношению» for ex ratione numeri | ratio; V44 'from the ratio of the number' |
| D | отношение; by context | ratio; by context |
| **Recommended** | **отношение (technical); by context where ratio is 'reckoning' or 'respect' (X19, X29, V34, V44)** | **ratio (technical); by context ('reckoning', 'by reason of')** |

The technical sense is uniform in all four. The non-technical uses (ratione numeri X19, ratione sonitus X29, ratione vocis V34, ex ratione numeri sonorum V44) are rendered by context; V44 is open (segment review). Note that Vinet uses ratio for the interval ratios where Xylander has proportio (X35 / V40).

*Evidence*: Lewis & Short s.v. ratio [from memory]; segment reviews V34, V44


### proportio (both) (to the editor)

Segments: X02, X03, X25, X35, X37, X38, X41, V03, V04, V05, V37

| | Russian | English |
|---|---|---|
| A | пропорция | proportion |
| B | пропорция | proportion |
| C | пропорция | proportion |
| D | пропорция (X02–X03, Vinet); отношение for the ratio of an interval (X25, X35, X37, X38, X41) | proportion (X02–X03, Vinet); ratio (X25, X35, X37, X38, X41) |
| **Recommended** | **пропорция** | **proportion** |

Xylander, like Boethius, has two words for the ratio of two numbers, ratio (X09, X16, X18, X20) and proportio (X25, X35, X37, X38, X41). Rendering proportio «пропорция» / 'proportion' throughout keeps his pair visible (A, B, C); D renders by sense and merges the two words in the interval passages, which is defensible but loses Xylander's usage. Vinet keeps proportio for cosmic proportion (V03–V05) and for V37, and writes ratio for the interval ratios.

*Evidence*: segment review X25; Boethius, De inst. arith. and De inst. mus. II (proportio for numerical ratios) [from memory]

Uniform in all four (X04, X32).

### commensuratio (xylander)

Segments: X02, X03, X04

| | Russian | English |

| B | соразмерность | commensurateness |
| C | соразмерность | commensuration |
| D | соразмерность | commensuration |
| **Recommended** | **соразмерность** | **commensuration** |

All agree in Russian; 'commensuration' (A, C, D) is the noun of action matching the Latin. Vinet has symmetria in the same places (V03–V05): the Russian of both versions coincides, so the difference must be kept in a note.

*Evidence*: Gaffiot s.v. commensuratio [from memory]; Oxford English Dictionary s.v. commensuration [from memory]


### symmetria (vinet) (to the editor)

Segments: V03, V04, V05

| | Russian | English |
|---|---|---|
| A | соразмерность | due measure (V04 'fitting measure') |
| B | соразмерность | symmetry |
| C | соразмерность | commensurability |
| D | соразмерность | symmetry |
| **Recommended** | **соразмерность** | **commensurability** |

Vinet's Grecism, paired with proportio (V03–V05), where Xylander has commensuratio. 'Symmetry' (B, D) is in the dictionaries but suggests mirror symmetry to a modern reader (about.md § 4.1); 'commensurability' keeps the Vitruvian sense, the Greek word in a note. The Russian coincides with Xylander's commensuratio: say so in a note.

*Evidence*: Lewis & Short s.v. symmetria, 'proportion, symmetry' [checked in the V03 review]; Gaffiot s.v. symmetria [checked in the V03 review]; about.md § 4.1

In the definition of the interval (ratio, sive respectus) the gloss must differ from ratio = «отношение»; «соотнесённость» does this. Elsewhere (respectu dieseos X18, in respectu X38) the word is the ordinary 'in respect of, in comparison with'.

*Evidence*: Lewis & Short s.v. respectus [from memory]

### strepitus (X13); crepitus (V16) (both)

Strepitus is 'a confused noise, din'; crepitus 'a rattling, clattering, a noise'. Both name a sound 'uttered at random' that is not a note; «грохот», «треск» and 'clatter' make it louder or more concrete than the context needs. Keep the difference of the Latin words in a note.

| | Russian | English |
|---|---|---|
| A | геккайдекахорд; пентекайдекахорд | hekkaidekachord; pentekaidekachord |
| B | шестнадцатиструнная система; пятнадцатиструнник | sixteen-string system; fifteen-string instrument |
| C | геккайдекахорд; пентекайдекахорд | hekkaidekachord; pentekaidekachord |
| D | шестнадцатиструнный [инструмент]; пятнадцатиструнный [инструмент] | sixteen-stringed [instrument]; fifteen-stringed [instrument] |
| **Recommended** | **геккайдекахорд; пентекайдекахорд** | **hekkaidekachord; pentekaidekachord** |

Xylander glosses the first himself (hoc est, sedecim chordarum instrumentum) and presents the second as the Greeks' name (vocant Graeci); translated, the gloss becomes a tautology (D). B adds 'system' and coins «пятнадцатиструнник». The transliterations are not current in Russian, so the editor must accept them as names glossed by the text itself.

*Evidence*: segment review X15; about.md § 4.1 (no unattested coinages)


### Pentekaedecachordum; Heckaedecachordum (V18, V49) (vinet) (to the editor)

Segments: V18, V49

| | Russian | English |
|---|---|---|
| A | пятнадцатиструнная [система]; шестнадцатиструнная [система] | fifteen-stringed [system]; sixteen-stringed [system] |
| B | пятнадцатиструнная система; шестнадцатиструнная система | fifteen-string system; sixteen-string system |
| C | пятнадцатиструнный [инструмент]; шестнадцатиструнная [система] | fifteen-string [instrument]; sixteen-string [system] |
| D | пятнадцатиструнный инструмент; шестнадцатиструнная система | fifteen-string instrument; sixteen-string system |
| **Recommended** | **пятнадцатиструнная [система]; шестнадцатиструнная [система]** | **the fifteen-stringed [system]; the sixteen-stringed [system]** |

Vinet uses the compounds as substantivised adjectives without a gloss, so translating them is right; the Heckaedecachordum is one of the systems of V49, which supports [system]. The supplied noun must be bracketed (B, D add it silently). Xylander, who glosses them, is transliterated: the notes should point out the difference.

*Evidence*: segment review V18; about.md § 4.6

§ 4.2 fixes составной / несоставной for (in)compositum, not for simplex. Vinet has two words, simplex (V31, V32, V57) and non-compositum (V34), and in V32 he explains the name simplicia itself, which only works with 'simple'. ### immediatus (X29); non sine medio (V34) (both)

Segments: X29, V34

| | Russian | English |
|---|---|---|
| A | непосредственный; не без середины | immediate; not without a middle |
| B | непосредственны; не лишены промежуточных ступеней | immediate; not without intervening [steps] |
| C | непосредственный; не лишены середины | immediate; not without a middle |
| D | без промежуточной [ступени] (both) | without an intermediate [step] (both) |
| **Recommended** | **непосредственный; не лишены середины** | **immediate; not without a middle** |

Both phrases deny that the fourth, fifth and octave lack intermediates, and both are taken up at once by per media omnia (X29, V34); the literal renderings keep that link (English 'immediate' in its etymological sense). D's 'step' and B's «ступеней» bring in a modern scale-step not in the Latin.

*Evidence*: Du Cange s.v. immediatus [from memory]


### diatessaron / Dia-tessaron; diapente / Dia-pente; diapason / Dia-pason (both) (to the editor)

Segments: X28, X29, X30, X31, X34, X35, X38, X42, X47, X48, X50, X51, V33, V34, V35, V36, V39, V40, V41, V44, V49, V55, V56, V58, V59, V61

| | Russian | English |
|---|---|---|
| A | кварта, квинта, октава; in X28–X30, X35 the Greek names literally: «через четыре», «через пять», «через все» | the fourth, fifth, octave; in X28–X30, X35 'through four', 'through five', 'through all' |
| B | кварта, квинта, октава (X28 English drops the names; Latin ordinals literal in X28, X35) | fourth, fifth, octave |
| C | кварта, квинта, октава; in X28–X30, X35 transliterated: диатессарон, диапенте, диапазон | the fourth, fifth, octave; in X28–X30, X35 diatessaron, diapente, diapason |
| D | кварта, квинта, октава (Russian of X28: established terms with bracketed glosses) | fourth, fifth, octave (English of X28: 'through four' etc.) |
| **Recommended** | **кварта, квинта, октава; where Xylander sets the Greek name against the Latin one (X28–X30, X35) the Greek name literally: «через четыре», «через пять», «через все»** | **the fourth, the fifth, the octave; in X28–X30, X35 'through four', 'through five', 'through all'** |

Fixed by about.md § 4.2. Where Xylander glosses the Greek names by the names current in his Latin (quarta, quinta, octava, X28, X30, X35), the established terms belong to the Latin names, from which they descend, and the Greek names need a literal rendering (A) or a transliteration (C); both explain the departure, as § 4.1 allows. The X28 review adds the transliteration at the first occurrence; the X30 and X35 reviews prefer A's literal names. One solution must be carried through X28–X30 and X35. Vinet has no such gloss: the established term, with the literal sense beside it where he explains the names (V34).

*Evidence*: about.md §§ 4.1–4.2; segment reviews X28, X29, X30, X35, V34

| **Recommended** | **кварта, квинта, октава; октава с квартой, октава с квинтой, две октавы** | **the fourth, the fifth, the octave; the octave with a fourth, the octave with a fifth, two octaves** |

These are Xylander's vernacular names and the source of the modern terms, so they take the established terms. B (X28, X35 against X30) and D (Russian against English) are inconsistent; see the errors at X28 and X30.

Xylander's 'today's' name for the double octave. «Квинтдецима» is the established Russian name of the interval of two octaves (after нона, децима … квинтдецима), 'the fifteenth' the English; the literal «пятнадцатая» needs quotation marks (B) to be readable.

### Paraphonum / Antiphonum (X31, X32) (xylander) (to the editor)

Segments: X31, X32

| | Russian | English |
|---|---|---|
| A | парафонное / антифонное | paraphonon / antiphonon |
| B | парафония / антифония | paraphony / antiphony |
| C | парафонное / антифонное | paraphonon / antiphonon |
| D | парафония / антифония | paraphony / antiphony |
| **Recommended** | **парафонное / антифонное** | **paraphonon / antiphonon** |

The Latin word is a neuter adjective used as a noun, and Xylander glosses it by participles (quasi iuxta sonans, contra sonans; assonans, obsonans) and resumes it with illud … consonat (X32); the adjectival form fits all of these. C notes that Russian «антифон» is a liturgical genre. The noun forms (B, D) are legitimate if carried through.

*Evidence*: segment review X31


### paraphonum / antiphonum (V36, V37) (vinet) (to the editor)

Segments: V36, V37

| | Russian | English |
|---|---|---|
| A | парафония / антифония | paraphony / antiphony |
| B | парафония / антифония | paraphony / antiphony |

| D | парафония / антифония | paraphony / antiphony |
| **Recommended** | **по парафонному [типу] … по антифонному; парафонное / антифонное** | **according to the paraphonic [kind] … the antiphonic; the paraphonic / the antiphonic** |

Same word, without Xylander's glosses. The V36 review leaves the choice to the editor; the adjectival form keeps the Latin word class (Theon's κατὰ τὸ παράφωνον / ἀντίφωνον) and matches the solution for Xylander. Whatever is chosen must serve V36 and V37 alike.

*Evidence*: segment review V36


### quasi iuxta sonans / contra sonans (X31); assonans / obsonans (X32) (xylander) (to the editor)

Segments: X31, X32

| | Russian | English |
|---|---|---|
| A | «звучащее рядом» / «звучащее против»; «звучащее при» / «звучащее навстречу» | 'sounding beside' / 'sounding against'; 'sounding to' / 'sounding over against' |
| B | звучание рядом / звучание навстречу; вторящее / ответное созвучие | sounding alongside / sounding in answer; sounding in accompaniment / in answer |
| C | «звучащее рядом» / «звучащее напротив»; «призвучное» / «противозвучное» | 'sounding beside' / 'sounding opposite'; 'sounding-to' / 'sounding-against' |
| D | «звучание рядом» / «звучание напротив»; «звучание в ответ» / «встречное звучание» | 'sounding alongside' / 'sounding opposite'; 'sounding in response' / 'sounding opposite' |
| **Recommended** | **«звучащее рядом» / «звучащее против»; «призвучное» / «противозвучное»** | **'sounding beside' / 'sounding against'; 'sounding-to' / 'sounding-against'** |

Both pairs calque the Greek prefixes (παρα- = iuxta, ad-; ἀντι- = contra, ob-), so the prefixes carry the meaning. Assono is 'to sound to, respond to'; ob-sono in classical Latin is 'to interrupt by a sound', which shows that obsonans is a calque, not a classical word in its classical sense. B's 'in answer' imports the respondeant of X32.

*Evidence*: Lewis & Short s.v. assono, 'to sound to, respond to' (Ov. Met. 3.507) [checked]; Lewis & Short s.v. ob-sono, 'to interrupt by a sound' (Plaut. Ps. 1.2.74) [checked]; segment reviews X31, X32

### super bipartiens tertias (X36) (xylander) (to the editor)

Segments: X36

| | Russian | English |
|---|---|---|
| A | сверхдвучастное третей | superbipartient thirds |
| B | с избытком в две трети | with two thirds in excess |
| C | сверхдвучастное по третям | superbipartient by thirds |
| D | [двукратное] с избытком в две трети [8:3] | [the double] plus two thirds [8:3] |
| **Recommended** | **[двукратное] сверхдвучастное третей [8:3]** | **[duple] superbipartient thirds [8:3]** |

A Boethian ratio name, to be formed on the pattern of § 4.3 (A, C); B and D replace it by a description (errors at X36). The bracket gives 16:6 = 8:3, the ratio named in the context; a bare [5:3] would mislead.

*Evidence*: about.md § 4.3; Boethius, De inst. arith. I.28–29 (superbipartiens tertias) [from memory]; segment review X36

### modulatio (X44) (xylander)

Segments: X44

| | Russian | English |
|---|---|---|

| D | мелодический строй | melodic shaping |
| **Recommended** | **мелодическое движение** | **melodic movement** |

Modulatio is 'a regular measure; in music, melody' (L&S, modulatio vocis = melody). Here Xylander uses it to gloss melodia (cantus seu cantilenae modulationem), so a rendering distinct from 'melody' is needed; 'modulation' (C) means a change of key to a modern reader.

*Evidence*: Lewis & Short s.v. modulatio II (Quint. 11.3.59 modulatio vocis, 'melody') [checked]

Vinet's modulatio stands where Xylander has melodia (V52 / X44, V53 / X45, V59 / X51); L&S give 'melody'. B's 'melodic movement' is consistent but heavier. The difference between the two versions' words belongs in a note.

Xylander distinguishes genus and species (X56 divisiones, differentias atque species; melodiarum genera). «род» / «вид» keeps them apart; B and D render the non-technical genus «вид» (X18, X31), merging it with species. ### vis; potestas; materies; ambitus (X42) (xylander)

Segments: X42

| | Russian | English |
|---|---|---|

Xylander varies four words for the full range of an interval; four distinct renderings keep his variation. Materies is the same word as materia of X43, where the doctrine turns on systems being 'in matter', so D's «содержание» / 'content' and B's 'material' break the link. «диапазон» echoes diapason in a text where that is a term.

### cavitatis amplitudo (X41); fistularum amplitudo (V47) (both)

Segments: X41, V47

| | Russian | English |
