| A | величина полости; ширина трубок | size of the cavity; width of the pipes |
| B | величина полости; величина трубок | size of the cavity; dimensions of the pipes |
| C | ширина полости; ширина трубок | width of the bore; width of the pipes |
| D | величина внутренней полости; величина трубок | size of the cavity; size of the pipes |
| **Recommended** | **ширина полости; ширина трубок** | **the width of the cavity; the width of the pipes** |

Amplitudo is first 'width, breadth'; 'size' is a legitimate, less specific alternative (V47 review). Fistula is the pipe.

*Evidence*: segment review V47

| | Russian | English |

| B | дыхание | breath |
| C | дыхание | breath |
| D | струя воздуха | breath |

Spiritus is the breath; D's «струя воздуха» ('air stream') interprets.

### concinnare, concinne; coaptare, coaptatio (X05, X12, X32, X44, X45, X47, X56); aptare (X56) (xylander)

Segments: X05, X12, X32, X44, X45, X47, X56

| | Russian | English |
|---|---|---|
| A | слаживать, слаженно; прилаживать, прилаживание; приспосабливать | to frame; to fit (together), fitting together; to adapt |
| B | стройно устраивать / соединять / сочетать, прилаживать (varies); сочетание, соединение | ordered / fitted / arrange / arranged; fitting together |
| C | слаживать, слаженно; прилаживать, прилаживание; приспосабливать | to attune, harmoniously; to fit together, fitting-together; to adapt |
| D | стройно устроенный / согласовать / складываться (varies); сочетание, соединять | fitted together / fitted / fit / fashioned; fitting together |
| **Recommended** | **слаживать; прилаживать; приспосабливать** | **to frame; to fit together; to adapt** |

Xylander's standing words for the fitting together of harmony. Concinno is 'to join fitly together, to order, arrange appropriately'; 'to frame' (A) serves every place (the world framed in God, a note framed into harmony, harmony framing the systems, a Lydian tune framed), while 'attune' (C) adds music where the Latin has none (X05). B and D change the word from place to place.

*Evidence*: Lewis & Short s.v. concinno [checked]; Lewis & Short s.v. coapto (eccl.) [from memory]


### temperare (X07, V58, V60); temperamentum manus (V65) (both)

Segments: X07, V58, V60, V65

| | Russian | English |
|---|---|---|
| A | упорядочивать (X07); слаживать (V58, V60); мера [движения] руки | to temper; control of the hand |
| B | соразмерять (X07, V60); составленный (V58); «как действует рука» (V65) | to temper (X07); compounded (V58); to regulate (V60); 'how the hand is controlled' (V65) |
| C | умерять (X07); соразмерять (V58, V60); мера [движений] руки | to temper (X07); to blend (V58, V60); measure of the hand |
| D | давать строй (X07); сочетание (V58); соразмерять (V60); то, как управляет рука | to bring into due order (X07); blended (V58); to proportion (V60); control of the hand |
| **Recommended** | **соразмерять; соразмерно составленный (V58); мера [движений] руки** | **to temper; tempered (V58); the measure of the hand** |

Tempero is 'to combine in due proportion, to regulate'; temperamentum 'a mixing in due proportion; measure, moderation'. One rendering per version (V58 and V60 are the same verb) is preferable to B's and D's variation; English 'temper' keeps the classical sense.

*Evidence*: Lewis & Short s.v. temperamentum [checked]; Lewis & Short s.v. tempero [from memory]

### Paean, Hymenaeus, Exodius (X56); Paeeon, Hymenaeus, Exodius (V64) (both) (to the editor)

Segments: X56, V64

| | Russian | English |
|---|---|---|
| A | пеан, гименей, эксодий | paean, hymenaeus, exodius |
| B | пеан, гименей, заключительная песнь | paean, hymenaeus, exit song |
| C | пеан, гименей, эксодий | the paean, the hymenaeus, the exodius |
| D | пеан, свадебная песнь, прощальная песнь | paean, wedding song, song of departure |
| **Recommended** | **пеан, гименей, эксодий (in a note: «заключительная песнь»)** | **the paean, the hymenaeus, the exodius (note: 'exit, closing song')** |

Paean and hymenaeus are established names. For Exodius there is no established modern term; the transliteration with a note keeps the Latin's name, a description (B, D) states one possible sense (closing song; in Byzantine usage also a funeral hymn, as D's doubt notes).

*Evidence*: LSJ s.v. ἐξόδιος [checked in the V64 review]; segment reviews X56, V64


### Universitas (V03, V05, V06); universum, universi (X02, X04, X05) (both)

Lower case: the printer's capitals carry no meaning, and A's English mixes 'Universe' and 'universe' in one sentence (V05). Vinet's noun universitas and Xylander's universum are different words for the same thing; a note.

## Term inconsistencies

- **B**, intensio (on its own): Five renderings across both versions: «высота» / 'pitch' (X08, V11), «повышение» / 'rise in pitch' (X37, X39, V43, V45), «натяжение» / 'tension' (V47), «высотная протяжённость» / 'pitch range' (V49), and «усиление» / 'strengthening' in the pair with the breath (X41, V47). about.md § 4.5 asks for one rendering per version; the term list states the renderings but no note argues the changes.
- **D**, intensio (on its own): «высота» / 'pitch' (X08, V11), «повышение» / 'rise in pitch' (X37, X39, V43, V45), «натяжение» / 'tension' (V47), «подъём» / 'rise' (V49), «усиление» / 'strengthening' (X41, V47): the word changes with each context, against § 4.5, without a note.
- **D**, vox (Vinet): «голос» / 'voice' in V11 and V43, but «звучание» / 'sound' in V34 (ratione vocis primi soni ad ultimum).
- **B**, vox (both versions): «голосовое звучание» / 'vocal sound' in the definitions (X08, V11), «звучание» / 'sounding' in V34 and V43; «звучание» is also B's word for Xylander's sonitus, so vox and sonitus coincide.
- **D**, materia / materies (Xylander): «материя» / 'matter' in X19 and X43, but «содержание» / 'content' for materiem in X42, the sentence before X43 states that systems are seen 'in matter'.
- **B**, materia / materies (Xylander): English 'material' in X42 against 'matter' in X19 and X43 (the Russian keeps «материя»).
- **B**, concinnare / coaptare (Xylander): Xylander's standing pair is rendered differently at each occurrence: «стройно устроенный» (X05), «стройно соединить» (X12), «стройно сочетать» (X44), «прилаживают» (X56); coaptatio «сочетание» (X45), coaptatis «соединения» (X47); English 'ordered', 'fitted', 'arrange', 'arranged'.
- **D**, concinnare / coaptare (Xylander): «стройно устроенный» (X05), «согласован» (X12), «согласовывать» (X44), «складывается» (X56); coaptatio «сочетание» (X45), «соединены» (X47); English 'fitted together', 'fitted', 'fit', 'fashioned'.
- **B**, genus / species (Xylander): The non-technical genus is «вид» (X18, X31) and the technical genus «род» (X44 ff.), while species is also «вид» (X15, X26): the two Latin words are merged in Russian (see X18).
- **D**, genus / species (Xylander): As B: genus «вид» in X18 and X31, «род» from X44, species «вид» (X15, X26).
- **A**, constitutio (Xylander): «устроение» (X43) but «составление» (X48) for the same sense; C shows one word serves both.
- **B**, constitutio (Xylander): «устройство» (X43) but «построение» (X48); «построение» is also B's word for constructio (X11).
- **D**, constitutio (Xylander): «строение» / 'structure' (X43), 'composition' (X48).
- **B**, vis / facultas: In Xylander, «сила» / 'capacity' for facultas (X27) and for vis (X42); in Vinet, 'capacity' for both facultates and vis (V49, recorded as an error). Distinct Latin words are merged.
- **D**, proportio (Xylander): «пропорция» / 'proportion' in X02–X03, «отношение» / 'ratio' in the interval passages (X25, X35, X37, X38, X41), so Xylander's ratio / proportio pair disappears there. Defensible by sense; put to the editor at X25.
- **B**, diatessaron / quarta (Xylander): Xylander's double naming is solved three ways: Latin ordinals rendered literally (X28, X35: «четвёртая»), both name-sets by the established terms (X30, tautological gloss), and the Greek names dropped from the English running text (X28, a major error). See the errors at X28 and X30.
- **D**, diatessaron / quarta (Xylander): Russian and English follow opposite strategies in X28 and X35 (Russian: established terms for the Greek names, quarta as «четвёртая»; English: Greek names literal, 'through four'), with no note (error at X28).
- **B**, heccaedecachordum / Pentecaedecachordum (X15): The pair is rendered unevenly in one sentence: «шестнадцатиструнная система» but «пятнадцатиструнник» (an unattested coinage); and Vinet's same words are «...струнная система» throughout (error at X15).
- **B**, consonus / dissonus (X13): «консонантный» beside «диссонирующий» in the same sentence; one formation should serve both.
- **A**, Universitas (Vinet, English): 'Universe' and 'universe' in one sentence (V05), copying the printer's capitals (error at V05); the Russian capitalises throughout.


## Overall assessment

### Translator A

**Strengths.** Exact and literal, with the most disciplined terminology: one rendering of intensio («напряжение» / 'tension') throughout both versions, Xylander's word pairs kept apart (ratio / proportio, concinnare / coaptare, coalitus / confundere), ratio names formed on the Boethian pattern where the Latin has them (super bipartiens tertias, X36). Its solution for Xylander's double naming of the intervals (Greek names rendered literally beside the established terms, X28–X30, X35) is the one the reviews prefer. Careful arithmetic, well-argued text proposals (7 judged sound), and notes on Xylander's and Vinet's characteristic words. 0 major and 12 minor errors; best base in 96 segments for Russian (16 alone) and 98 for English (12 alone); 67 notes recommended for the commentary.

**Weaknesses.** Literalism sometimes costs idiom: «удержание голоса» (X08), «звучащим при» (X32), «Это о звуках» (X17), 'dwellings' for mansiones (X57), 'eight cannot be squared' (V27), 'terminates through four' (V34). Twice it translates its own conjecture instead of the reading text (X24 propositi duplum, X38 quam), which belongs in the text issues. Two note claims are wrong or overstated (X15 on LSJ headwords; V24, where its Russian «её же» cannot keep the ambiguity it claims). The English of V05 mixes 'Universe' and 'universe'.

### Translator B

**Strengths.** Readable, natural prose in both languages, with some well-judged idiomatic solutions (the closing formula 'So much for notes', X17; 'the full pitch range of the double octave', V49, which the review prefers). Its readings of the arithmetic are correct, and its contextual renderings of intensio, though inconsistent, are each intelligible. 1 major and 36 minor errors; best base in 6 segments for Russian (0 alone) and 7 for English (0 alone); 6 notes recommended for the commentary.

**Weaknesses.** The only major error of the whole comparison: at X28 the English drops the Greek names diatessaron, diapente, diapason, which the sentence exists to gloss. Many minor faults of the same kinds: connectives left out (Enimvero, Caeterum, Porro, Verum, utique, horum), additions without brackets (eight sign errors: 'system', 'instruments', 'the ratio', 'proceed', 'steps'), paraphrase that flattens the Latin antitheses (X37, X57, V65), and the conjecture of X24 translated instead of the reading text. Terms: heccaedecachordum made a 'system' and Pentecaedecachordum a coined «пятнадцатиструнник» (X15); super bipartiens tertias paraphrased (X36); the pitch pair collapsed (X32); vis and facultates merged as 'capacity' (V49); intensio rendered five ways; Vinet's simplex merged with non-compositum and Xylander's incompositum; non tamen read as 'not yet' (X33). Few notes, and few worth keeping.

### Translator C

**Strengths.** The closest to the construction of the Latin and the most reliable: it renders the second quoque of X26, the idiom duae partes = two thirds (V42), the accusative of respect of X32, the adjectival paraphonon / antiphonon, and keeps Vinet's and Xylander's different words visible. Consistent terminology in both versions and in both languages, a single rendering of intensio, and the best notes of the four (doctrine, arithmetic, the page images, the translators' habits); its text proposals are the most often judged sound (8), and it argues and itself rejects the weaker alternatives in its doubts. 0 major and 3 minor errors; best base in 104 segments for Russian (22 alone) and 108 for English (18 alone); 82 notes recommended for the commentary.

**Weaknesses.** Its transliteration of the Greek interval names in X28–X30 and X35 is reasoned (note at X28) but «диапазон» misleads a Russian reader, for whom it means 'range' (X30). English 'genus' for the non-technical genus (X18) and 'modulation' for Xylander's modulatio (X44) invite confusion with the genera and with the modern sense; «меса единственная» / «меса одна» half-translate the qualifier of mese (X14, V17); an occasional calque («через один-единственный интервал», V32); one note claims an ambiguity that its Russian does not keep (V24).

### Translator D

**Strengths.** Fluent, modern Russian and English that read well on their own; a sound grasp of the doctrine and the arithmetic; some good idiomatic restructurings (X01) and legitimate alternative construals that the reviews accept (V44 scope of the negative, X30 relative quod); careful bracketing in places ('[of pitch]', '[ratio]' in English). 0 major and 35 minor errors; best base in 9 segments for Russian (0 alone) and 11 for English (0 alone); 17 notes recommended for the commentary.

**Weaknesses.** Freer than the brief allows: it regularly drops the connectives that carry Xylander's and Vinet's argument (Porro, Nam, Sed, Verum, enim, proinde, Caeterum, quin et … quoque, plane), flattens 'not … alone, but also' into a plain list (X57, V65), recasts the definition of the note as a relative clause (V11) and adds words without brackets (seven sign errors). In X28 its Russian and English follow opposite strategies without a note. Terms: the ratio name of X36 replaced by a description; materies as 'content' (X42), which hides the 'matter' of X43; vox, intensio, concinnare and genus / species rendered differently from place to place; hymenaeus and exodius described rather than named; literal «две восьмых» / 'two eighths' (X30). Two note claims are wrong (X04 on Xylander's reason for proportionalitas; V42 on the numerals of the figure).

### Summary

Across the 124 segments the team recorded 32 divergences, 1 major and 54 minor errors and 24 editor questions for Xylander, and 29 divergences, 0 major and 32 minor errors and 24 editor questions for Vinet. All four translators understand both Latin versions and recompute every ratio correctly; no wrong number, ratio or ratio bracket was found in the running text of any of the four, and there is only one major error in the whole set (B's English at X28). The four fall into two pairs. A and C are close, literal and consistent: they keep the Latin's construction, its signs and its word pairs, and they are the best base almost everywhere (most often jointly, 'A = C'); C has the fewest faults and the most useful notes and is the best single base, A is close behind and often more exact in its terminology. B and D write freer, more idiomatic prose but leave out many of the connectives that carry the argument, add words without brackets, paraphrase antitheses and vary their technical terms; their Russian and English are rarely the best base, though individual phrasings are worth taking over. The decisions left to the editor are chiefly terminological: intensio ('tension' throughout, or 'pitch' in the definitions); how to render Xylander's double naming of the intervals (X28–X30, X35); proportio beside ratio in Xylander; the form of paraphonon / antiphonon; transliteration of the Greek compounds in X15 and of mese monos (X14); Vinet's simplex and melos; the name Exodius; and whether a translator's conjecture may ever stand in the running text (X24, X38). The term concordance recommends one rendering for each, consistent with about.md § 4, and lists twenty term inconsistencies, all minor, chiefly in B and D.

### Contamination

none: no member of the team reported meeting forbidden material, and the assembler met none (only dictionary pages, Lewis & Short, were opened for the concordance).
